# Devlog

Session logs documenting changes made during AI-assisted development sessions.

**Convention:** `dlYYYYMMDD_topic.md` — see `doc/ai_collaboration_protocol.md` Section 6 for format.

**Distinction from `./todo/`:** Devlogs record *what was changed and why* (backward-looking). Task files in `./todo/` document *what needs to be done* (forward-looking).

**Campaign state pages:** [`campaigns/`](campaigns/) — one ≤1-page current-state file per
ACTIVE campaign (protocol §11); the dated entries below stay append-only.

- [dl20260904_thinshell_volume_shell_align.md](dl20260904_thinshell_volume_shell_align.md) — `Kernel::compute_element_volumes` aborted on `undulator2d` under MPI (proc 4: element maps to facet ordinal 5190 of 1456): the thin-shell volume loop did one `share`/`receive` + one `broadcast` **per shell**, but workers skip shells they hold no element of (`ProtoMesh::create_thinshells`), so a worker missing one tape consumed the previous tape's areas and first ids — 5190 − 4368 (one tape's element+edge id stride) = 822, a valid ordinal. Replaced by two ordered flat `share`/`receive` payloads before the loop (all areas, and an id-keyed header of offsets and first ids), unconditional on every rank, shells and blocks resolved by id; setup-tier `BELFEM_ERROR` on duplicate shell ids, empty blocks and header bounds. Plan jury and code jury at xhigh (Codex + Grok): merge, no P0/P1; a latent count/fill mismatch in `collect_thin_shell_facet_ids` noted, untouched. **Verified: `undulator2d` at np 8, cold and warm, clean; `make check` green (Christian, same day)**; an MPI thin-shell regression in `make check` still owed

- [dl20260903_todo_currentness_sweep.md](dl20260903_todo_currentness_sweep.md) — `todo/` triage in **two rounds, the first of which was wrong**. Round 1 read Status lines and counted unchecked boxes, moved 9 files; Christian rejected it — far more were obsolete. The bias was directional: a plan is written when work is *planned* and rarely updated when it *lands*, so boxes and OPEN statuses both overstate what is left. The tell was in round 1's own output — `matvec_csc_omp_stack_overflow` has 17 unchecked boxes and was called ACTIVE, when its fix is fully landed (`splinalg.f90` gated on `BELFEM_OMP`, `USE_BELFEM_OPENMP` OFF) and most of those boxes describe a **rejected** design kept as record. Round 2 asked only *is the work already in the tree?* and required a grep, not a quotation, for every verdict (Codex terra/high + Grok 4.6/high, disjoint batches of 24). **19 more moved; active 57 → 29.** New `cancelled/` bucket for plans whose work was not done and will not be: `arpack_small_end_configuration` (its spectral fold was REPLACED by shift-invert — the source says so at `cl_FEM_DofMgr_EigenValues.hpp:129-136`) and `sch04_history_purge` (Christian's ruling; here the tree *disagreed* with the obsolete reading — `fysc.hdf5` still in HEAD, 6 sch04 objects still reachable — so it is obsolete by decision, not completion). Plans the tree flatly contradicted: `nedelec_edge_function_defects` said "No source modified" with both fixes and a 22/22 battery in tree; `dr128_spap_low_field_axis` framed as unimplemented with the transition at `cl_JcFunction_Database.hpp:440-480`; `nitrogen_eos_completion` claiming three unwritten bodies and absence from `SOURCES`; `thermal_matrices_cleanup_and_newton_plan` awaiting a `T_h_newton` that is 77 lines at `mt_thermal_h.cpp:57`. Disposition rule now recorded in `todo/README.md`: a finished plan whose only remnant is a gate moves to `closed/` with that gate named — an owed gate belongs in the debt register, not on the active list. Nothing compiled or run; reviewed, not verified. **Round 3 (Fable):** Christian's review of the 29 set the final standard — purpose served in the tree → closed even if the plan's refined scope is not (`ac_loss`: `dotQ` is in the exodus), and undone-but-unblocking → cancelled. **29 → 6 active**; 23 moved with a dated reason banner each; `rho_lambda_b_first_reorder` cancelled by ruling (keep T-first); `globals.cmake` no longer installs the retired `hphirun`/`hphiTrun`. The doxygen sweep's ~245 unapplied corrections were dispatched to five applier agents the same day

- [dl20260901_dr131_deck_completion.md](dl20260901_dr131_deck_completion.md) — **DR-131's last residual is discharged: a deck HAS now run to completion on Darwin.** With DR-155 fixed, a Release + `USE_SHARED_LIBS=ON` build from current source was installed to a clean sandbox prefix and both remaining decks run with **no `OMP_STACKSIZE` set**. `tape_quench_usermat` cleared the step that used to kill it ( `magnetic 1.033e-10`, `thermal 6.684e-12` ) and then ran **54 timesteps over 6 h 50 m with zero crash signatures**, five plugin-supplied materials across two `dlopen`ed libraries — **stopped deliberately, not failed**, since its 0.3 s of stiff coupled quench projects to ~114 h ( an earlier ~375 h estimate was too pessimistic; it extrapolated while Δt was collapsing ). **`disk_pulse` then ran to COMPLETION: 106 accepted timesteps, 0 rejected, t = 500.0000 ms, 0 crash signatures in 3085 lines, 100 Exodus frames, clean self-exit** — and its `bgpulse.so` was confirmed MAPPED INTO THE LIVE PROCESS by `lsof`, so the plugin was on the run path rather than merely on disk. That completion supplies what 54 `tape_quench` steps could not: the **teardown path**, output finalisation and `dlclose` at exit. By-catch: `hatch_turtle()` fired live on the blind default ( 16 threads requested, 8 physical cores ), which is **executed** confirmation that leaving the oversubscription warning on the plain `OMP` define — rather than sweeping it onto `BELFEM_OMP` — was right; and `libgomp` was mapped with `BELFEM_OMP` off, matching the corrected docs. Strike is Christian's ruling, not taken here. No `src/` change.

- [dl20260901_dr155_plan_jury.md](dl20260901_dr155_plan_jury.md) — **DR-155's plan went to a jury, came back unapproved, and was then closed by DELETING the parallelism rather than repairing it.** The drafted fix would have produced SILENTLY WRONG matvecs: The kernel is correct today because of *two* zeroings and only one is visible in the source — `y = 0.0d0` (`splinalg.f90:92`) plus `reduction(+:)`'s identity-initialisation of each private copy. R1 removed the second and did not replace it; `allocate` does not zero, so the hand-written combine would have summed heap garbage. Same hole a second way: sizing from `omp_get_max_threads()`, the cap rather than the team, leaves unwritten columns in the sum. Grok found it, Claude confirmed it against the source — neither the pre-registration nor Codex had it. **Second: the row's `USE_MKL=ON` scope guard was FALSE**, raised independently four times (Christian, Claude, Codex, Grok) — the `#ifdef BELFEM_MKL` block bounds only the 5-argument `SpMatrix::multiply`; the 2-argument overload (`cl_SpMatrix.cpp:1737-1774`) has no MKL branch and crashes MKL builds on the ARPACK eigen path. Christian's ruling: **one DR-155 sweep**, and the same site read forward — *if MKL is set we always want the MKL routines* — makes the repair a forwarder, which must `fill(0.0)` first because the eigen driver passes an uninitialised `aY` and MKL is not documented to short-circuit β = 0. Also struck: the Linux 1,048,576-row cliff was assumed (two incompatible readings; **settled by one `OMP_DISPLAY_ENV=TRUE` run**, and it decides the severity), 'measured to the byte' overstated a 1152-byte bracket, R3 named a Fortran abort primitive that does not exist, `mSwap` is live *across* the kernel at β ≠ 0, O1's 'once per Krylov iteration' was refuted (it is per Newton step), and the regression fixture both auditors asked for already exists at `tests/sparse/test_SpMatrix.cpp:1782`, `tN = 64`. **Two documents assert this defect is impossible** — `coding_philosophy.md:674` and `parallel_execution.md:20-22` both claim BELFEM compiles no OpenMP — and `check_doc_claims.py` passes 37/37 over them. One Grok finding REFUTED and kept as D13; three of Claude's own findings corrected, one resting on a malformed `grep`. **Then Christian asked the question none of the three reviewers had — what does this threading actually BUY? — and the answer closed the row:** both matvecs are MASTER-ONLY, they are ~2 flops per nonzero beside a full assembly and a direct factorization, and no threaded-vs-serial measurement exists anywhere in the tree (the kernel comment compares two THREADED variants). So BELFEM's own `!$omp` directives are now gated on a new **`BELFEM_OMP`** define (`USE_BELFEM_OPENMP`, **OFF** by default) and the kernel does not thread at all — no buffer, no registry, no hand-written reduction semantics. **Nine `#ifdef OMP` sites deliberately stayed put** (a blanket sweep would have silently deleted the oversubscription warning, serialised PARDISO and hidden the banner's thread budget); those carry 'NOT a mistake' comments. A Darwin-only ban was argued against and dropped — O2 unresolved means a carve-out could leave the same cliff live on Linux, and platform-conditional threading gives platform-divergent rounding. `USE_BELFEM_OPENMP=ON` **re-arms the defect**, and the process is still multi-threaded via STRUMPACK/MKL/BLAS so `OMP_STACKSIZE` stays a live knob — both documented. Side benefit: `matvec_csc` is now bitwise deterministic across thread counts. Executed: preprocessor gating (11 `!$omp` with the define, 0 without) and `gfortran -fsyntax-only` green on all three kernels in BOTH token states (6/6). **The MKL half was fixed in the same session:** the 2-argument `SpMatrix::multiply` now forwards to the 5-argument one (`multiply( aX, aY, 1.0, 0.0, false )`), so MKL builds reach `mkl_sparse_d_mv` on the ARPACK path, the overload gains the two dimension asserts it lacked, and both remaining Fortran call sites end up inside the `#else` of `BELFEM_MKL` — making the row's originally FALSE scope guard true as an end state. Its `aY.fill(0.0)` is MKL-only on purpose (the Fortran kernels self-zero; MKL is not documented to short-circuit beta = 0 and `0.0 * NaN` is NaN). **Build + `make check` in both token states, a Linux `-DUSE_MKL=ON` build — MKL cannot even be configured on Darwin, so the path this change exists to enable is the one unverifiable where it was written — and the deck on a default environment are all OWED.** Reviewed, not verified.

- [dl20260901_darwin_plugin_gate.md](dl20260901_darwin_plugin_gate.md) — **DR-131's Darwin gate finally ran, and the templates it suspected were the half that worked.** Both plugin templates build on macOS 15.7.9 under gcc and Apple clang, against a source tree and a real `/tmp/belfem` prefix: `lib<name>.so` as documented, init symbols exported, 11 undefined `belfem::` symbols left for the host, nothing linked. The row's "higher-risk" `MODULE` change breaks no Mac user's load path — because on this tree no Mac user ever had a working one. **The four shipped example decks had the same defect and had never been fixed:** `SHARED` with no `dynamic_lookup` (hard `ld: symbol(s) not found` on Mach-O), *and* `if(APPLE) SUFFIX ".dylib"` contradicting each deck's own `input.conf` `file : src/build/usermat.so`. Two independent fatals per deck; all four were dead on arrival on macOS. Fixed all four; DR-131 widened rather than branched, per extend-don't-branch. **The dlopen half is executed, not inferred** — the template plugin and all five `tape_quench_usermat` materials (incl. the `JcFunctionUserDefined` vtable case) load and evaluate, and the deck itself reaches 295,315 dofs and the first BDF1 step, with a falsification control (plugin hidden → abort at `Could not load library`) proving the load path is on the run path. **By-catch, unrelated and larger:** that run then died in `matvec_csc._omp_fn.0` — `splinalg.f90:103`'s array reduction puts each worker's private copy of the whole result vector on the thread stack, and the crash boundary lands on n = 262,144 = 2 MiB / 8 B *exactly*, with `OMP_STACKSIZE=64M` and `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` both surviving as controls. Filed DR-155 + `todo/matvec_csc_omp_stack_overflow.md`; no `src/sparse` change made, since the fix's shape is an open design fork. Linux is unmeasured but the mechanism predicts the same cliff at 1,048,576 rows.

- [dl20260831_l21_card_repair.md](dl20260831_l21_card_repair.md) — **`L-21`, the tripwire that third-party libraries are never called directly, cited a contract that had moved and shipped an enforcement grep that could not go red on a real violation while going red on four kinds of non-violation.** Its central citation `src/core/assert.cpp:294-320` pointed at a five-line stub — the MPI lifecycle contract moved into `comm_abort` on 2026-08-30 (`cl_Communicator.hpp:235-258`, `commtools.cpp:67`) — which is `INC-543`'s exact shape inside the card written to prevent it, and `coding_philosophy.md:631` was stale the same way **and repeated one sentence near-verbatim**; both descend from one edit that updated the code and its in-place comments meticulously and neither document that describes them. The Rule named `commtools.hpp` as *the* MPI wrapper while its own worked example `comm_abort` is declared in `cl_Communicator.hpp` deliberately, because `commtools.hpp` drags linalg and core's include path does not carry it. **The grep returned fourteen `MPI_` hits outside `src/comm/`, none of them a call** — comments, a serial-build `typedef int MPI_Comm`, parameters and members, a Blaze macro, a PETSc error enumerator — while structurally blind to the 33 `MPI_` lines in `src/sparse/*.f90`. **Christian ruled the Fortran question closed in the direction that costs nothing:** the wrapper requirement is specific to C++, and the Fortran drivers for MUMPS, PARDISO and ARPACK **are** the wrappers the C++ tree uses, their traffic confined inside the vendor package — now a named, printed exclusion rather than a silent `grep -v`. `scripts/check_wrapper_policy.py` replaces the recipe: it matches **calls** (identifier then open paren, comments and string literals stripped) across MPI, HDF5, PETSc, MUMPS, STRUMPACK, SuperLU, LAPACK/BLAS and ARPACK, counts in-wrapper calls so an empty owner column reads as a broken pattern rather than a clean tree, and was **falsifier-gated** — a deliberate `MPI_Barrier` under `sparse/` reported, the commented/typedef/parameter/string-literal forms of the same token on the same file not. **Its first real run was red on 11 sites, none of them MPI, and Christian ruled all 11 by design the same day** — so the sweep's first output was not a defect list but a map of two ownership facts that existed only in people's heads: PETSc's requirements are unusual enough that its scope reaches into the communicator (`PetscInitialize` is paired with `MPI_Init` in `Communicator::init` and cannot live behind a `Solver*`), and the ten `H5*` database probes in `physics/materials` are sanctioned as written, since they test for a group that is a *sibling* of the one the io wrapper opened and the wrapper exposes no root handle. Both are **owner entries that print their reason** on every run rather than silent allowlist lines, scoped tightly enough that an `MPI_` call in `physics/materials/` or an `H5*` call in `physics/gasmodels/` still goes red — **re-proved by falsifier after the widening**, since a widened allowlist is exactly when a green run stops being evidence. Sweep green on the tree. `INC-565`/`INC-566` filed (the plan assumed they existed; they did not), the second recording that the wrapper drafted to close the sweep **re-introduced the `gComm.world()` contract hours after it was fixed**, alongside a latent wrong-arity template body, a `const T*` recvbuf, a serial branch that never copied send→recv, and an `int_t` count narrowing above 2^31−1. The file header's "none is mechanically enforced yet" was a false universal and is corrected. **Reviewed, not verified** — `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37 throughout, which corroborates none of the prose; the only executed gate is the sweep's own falsifier.

- [dl20260831_cohomology_ai_edit_ban.md](dl20260831_cohomology_ai_edit_ban.md) — **The cohomology core is closed to AI edits: `cl_Cohomology`, `cl_Homology`, `cl_SimplicialComplex`, `cl_Chain`, `cl_Cochain`, `fn_Smith` — six units, twelve files — banned to Claude, Codex and Grok alike, and NOT liftable by the ordinary session edit approval; only Gregory Giard's named authorization reaches them.** Ruled by Christian and Gregory (module owner) off DR-29, whose analysis Gregory read and could not extract a main message from, against Christian's record of having "broken it and had to restore it often enough in the last few months." **The reason is novelty relative to TRAINING DATA, not difficulty** — this is a *modified* Pellikka reduction, **unpublished** (Giard et al., in preparation — **drafted 2025, shelved, resumed 2026, which is why the tree carried both years for one work** (Christian, settled after both rounds flagged it as irreconcilable); the author is **Giard**, and the module docs' "Giarda" was a typo in nine places across four files, all corrected here, with a `literature_references.md` entry added where there had been none), so a model's nearest prior is textbook Pellikka, every deliberate modification reads as a defect, and "fixing" it regresses the algorithm toward the published version it deliberately is not. **The canonical example was WRONG in the first draft and the corrected one is better because it is attested:** `coreduceOmit` (`cl_SimplicialComplex.cpp:1236-1257`) removes ONE 0-cochain per stall and re-runs the whole `pCoreduce(0..2)` cascade each time, with `reduceOmit` (`:939-961`) the dual that removes top-dimensional chains — and on 2026-07-03 an AI proposed **batching** that omission, struck as unsafe because it strands 1-cells with empty boundary and `pCoreduce` only ever pairs a cell whose boundary holds exactly one entry (`cl_SimplicialComplex.hpp:364`), so they survive into cocombine as spurious generators. The loop looks trivially hoistable and is not. **The jury (Codex `gpt-5.6-terra`/high + Grok `grok-4.6`/high) found four load-bearing errors in the policy's own first draft**, all confirmed against source: DR-111 is `fem/kernel` thin-shell winding and rectification lives INSIDE the ban, so the single example offered for "scope boundary, not directory ban" was false twice (correct IDs: DR-107, DR-108, DR-133); Layer 1 was miscounted as 210 lines / "2 entries, 6 lines" against an actual 218 / 18 — an L-08 miss inside the tripwire file itself, caused by measuring before a second edit; "Gregory's published work" is false; and the mathematical non-unit obstruction was conflated with the implementation's documented inability to handle it (`thin_cut_nonunit_rectification.md:22-30`, hard error at `cl_Cohomology.cpp:880-886`). **"Three models will concur" was itself an overclaim and the record refutes it** — DR-29's F6 had Claude and Grok concurring on a false defect with Codex breaking the tie, so the rule is *do not treat concurrence as coverage*, errors correlated but not unanimous. **Left open for Christian and Gregory, deliberately unsettled:** the file list is not derived from the stated criterion (`fn_Smith` is textbook SNF, banned on coupling grounds; `cl_BeltedTree` IS a cohomology algorithm with no citation and is outside), comment-only edits are unruled (treated as banned meanwhile), and `tests/homology/` sits outside the ban. **The policy's central empirical claim is not reconstructible from the repository** — both git-visible reverts are `cl_CutProcessor` from mid-2025, nine months pre-collaboration, while two AI edits *inside* the ban held up (`759a0801`'s `pGeneralizedCocombine` restructuring, pre-image read and semantics confirmed preserved; DR-107's `clean_greedy` deletion). **Precedent sweep across all 429 devlogs, filed as INC-559 … INC-564 and distilled into a new Layer 2 card `L-22` "An algorithm with no published implementation inverts your prior":** the pattern is one shape every time — a deliberate deviation read as a defect, the "repair" moving the code toward the textbook version. Beside INC-559 and the batch-omit strike sit INC-561 (the in-tree full rescan flagged as a deviation from Mrozek Alg. 6.1; the paper reversed the direction of the finding and Thm 6.2 dissolved its severity — deviation ≠ defect) and INC-562 (the 2026-07-03 "we already have a worklist in-module" reused as a template, refuted 8 weeks later as a category error: LIFO `pop`, sorting `unique()`, no ±1 check — shape matched, semantics did not). **INC-564 fixes the rule's boundary, and Christian's correction to it narrowed the ban's cost rather than widening it:** the two AI edits that landed *inside* the core were first filed as clean successes, and neither is — `759a0801` preserved DR-29 #10's zero-neighbor skip, and DR-107's `clean_greedy()` deletion was **heavily human-guided surgery** in which the human supplied the constraint the code does not state. Both algorithms are needed: production is the SPFA certifier *plus* the greedy rectifier `cohomology::rectify_greedy_sweeps`, live inside `clean_spfa` (`cl_Cohomology.hpp:135` forwards `clean()` there), and only the standalone member and the `mFunClean` switch were cut. An AI seeing "two cleaners, one dead dispatch" deletes the live rectifier. So the discriminator is **"was the constraint supplied from outside the model's prior"**, and the list of moves that still work is just two — read the primary source, or derive it independently. Deleting apparent redundancy is not on it, because "this looks redundant" is reasoning, and reasoning is what fails here. **Refinement the record forces on the framing:** the hazard peaks at *near* similarity, not at absence of a prior — alien code produces visible uncertainty and gets flagged, 95 %-isomorphic code produces confidence. **Round 2 (`gpt-5.6-sol`/xhigh + `grok-4.6`/xhigh, full file uncut at `DIFF_CAP=700` — L-22 sits past the default 400-line cap) found the card committing its own failure mode:** "Mrozek §5 licenses omitting one 0-cell per connected component" is FALSE — `mrozek2009.txt:444-464` adds ∅ as a dimension **−1** simplex carrying every vertex in its coboundary, one per component, and licenses no 0-cell omission; `coreduceOmit` adds no −1 cells. The gloss came verbatim from `dl20260703_coreduce_risk_reeval.md:25-27`, survived two months and INC-476, and reached §7.1 and L-22 because nobody opened the paper — inside a rule whose entire content is *open the primary source*. The safety conclusion is BELFEM's own and survives; only the citation was borrowed, and the cascade itself remains un-re-derived. A second citation of the same class: `cl_Cohomology.hpp:135` is the *declaration* of `clean_spfa`, not the forward (`cl_Cohomology.cpp:189-192`; rectifier at `:548`) — produced by reading a header declaration as a call and reporting it verified. **P0: the Layer 1 heading over-banned** — "anything in `src/homology/`" against a policy that bans six units and explicitly frees CutFactory, CutProcessor, CutData, CutSet, InterfaceProcessor, BeltedTree, Topology and the module's docs; corrected, with the override gate now carrying its full condition. L-22 had also been appended *after* the "candidates not promoted" annex and now follows L-21, and "in every case the deviation was the point" was struck as contradicted by its own INC-561 (where Thm 6.2 made the deviation harmless) in favour of *treat the deviation as unknown until checked*. **Nine pre-existing defects in other cards were found and deliberately left for Christian** — L-21's `assert.cpp:294-320` points at a contract that moved to `cl_Communicator.hpp:235-258` (and `coding_philosophy.md:631` is stale the same way), its enforcement grep excludes `.f90` and misses the direct MPI in `mumpstools.f90`/`parpacktools.f90`, the header's "none is mechanically enforced" is a false universal, INC-555 is pinned to ARPACK when the row is MUMPS, and the `git stash` tripwire is unsafe in a shared tree. Written into `doc/ai_collaboration_protocol.md` §7.1 (authoritative), `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `doc/lessons_learned.md` Layer 1 (224/250) + `L-22` + a failure-signature row, `doc/lessons_learned_evidence.md` (INC-476 annotated, INC-559…564), `src/homology/doc/README.md`, DR-29 and DR-71's rows. **Reviewed, not verified** — `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37 corroborates none of it; the checker cannot see prose. Not mechanically enforced.
- [dl20260831_doc_currentness_sweep.md](dl20260831_doc_currentness_sweep.md) — **All 96 markdown files in `doc/` and `src/*/doc/` swept by Claude + Codex + Grok; the headline is that `hphirun` and `hphiTrun` are no longer built and six documents still tell users to run them.** `src/executables/CMakeLists.txt:40-47` comments out both `Add_Executable` blocks ("the old executables are obsolete"); `cmake-build-debug/bin/`, built the same morning, holds `belfem`/`electricalCircuit`/`material`/`gas`/`db2exo` and neither of them. The `.cpp` sources survive, so source citations stay valid and only *run* instructions break — `README.md:44`, `doc/getting_started.md:45-46`, `doc/parallel_execution.md:12`, `maxwell_usage_guide.md:310-311`, `solver_memory_and_compression.md:106`, and `examples/README.md:38-40`, which asserts they "still exist and still work". **That sentence is why Grok got the severity wrong** — it read a document where the tree was available and downgraded Codex's P0 to "stale, not immediately fatal"; Iron rule 1 paid for itself in one round. **Three taught code examples are broken, one of them silently:** `maxwell_usage_guide.md:945,951` teaches `rho( norm(b), angle, T )` against a `rho( T, B, beta )` signature — all three parameters are `real`, so it compiles and returns a wrong resistivity (L-06's all-`real` trap, and the sibling `materials_usage_guide.md:70` gets it right); `dof_manager_usage_guide.md:2332-2337` teaches a `comm.send(data, target, tag)` object API that does not exist, with a `sum_all` that appears nowhere in `src/`; `iwg_usage_guide.md:263` calls `create_iwg` with three arguments against a two-argument declaration and `maxwell_usage_guide.md:506` calls `new Kernel(mesh)` against a `KernelParameters *` constructor. **Two defaults are documented backwards:** `USE_DEBUG` is documented ON in three files and defaults OFF unless `$SCLS` names the `debug` flavor (the round's only triple-agreement, and cheap because the check is mechanical), and the Coulomb-gauge penalty is "off by default" in two docs while `cl_IWG_Maxwell.cpp:48-51` has had it ON at `chi=1e-4` since 2026-08-27. **Countable drift that `check_doc_claims.py` is structurally blind to** (it passes 37/37): Layer 1 of `lessons_learned.md` is 200 lines documented as 141/~140, Layer 2 has 21 cards documented as 18, the catalogue holds 558 distinct incident IDs documented as 539/537, and `CLAUDE.md:520-523` lists 23 module doc dirs where 26 exist. **Theory came back mostly clean, with one open question and one misattribution.** My prediction that a doc would reproduce the pre-erratum Arsenault air coupling was falsified by my own grep and then *confirmed by Codex through a channel the grep could not see* — `maxwell_usage_guide.md:329-369` states the air region as the static `∇·(μ∇φ)=0` while `mt_maxwell_phi.cpp:26-46` assembles a φ mass term; **routed to Christian, not decided** (formulation). Read firsthand, `messe2023.txt:509-517` says the paper "opted to couple … using Lagrange multipliers" and credits static condensation to Alves et al., so `maxwell_usage_guide.md:186-210` and `CLAUDE.md:699,711` misattribute BELFEM's (correct, and correctly recommended) choice to a paper that implemented the other one. Also found: `src/numerics/opt` is live code (`USE_NLOPT` ON) documented nowhere. **Reviewed, not verified** — no gate ran; the `hphirun` finding rests on a build tree's contents.
- [dl20260831_doc_repair_campaign.md](dl20260831_doc_repair_campaign.md) — **The repair of the 19 defect groups the sweep found, run as plan+audit → correct → audit on Christian's instruction to treat documentation like code: 89 files, 1295 insertions, seven batches, 16 auditor rounds (Codex `gpt-5.6-terra`/high + Grok `grok-4.6`/high on the plan and on every batch). The finding is not the repair — it is that EVERY batch shipped at least one new false statement and the audit caught all of them.** R1 replaced a false claim about the Exodus output name with a different false one (the segregated coupled path hardcodes `hphi_results.e-s`, `belfem.cpp:303`); R2 asserted `dmudH` returns ∂B/∂H when it returns dμ/dH, so the differential permeability μ + H·dμ/dH is returned by nothing; **R3 introduced an outright regression** — replacing a non-existent `create_local_mesh()` with `run()`+`partial_mesh()` on every rank turned a compile error into a rank-0 runtime abort, i.e. a loud failure into a quiet one, and **R3's own audit passed it; R6's audit caught it three batches later**; R4's contribution was a flag manufacturing an open question `nedelec_derivation.md` had already settled, repeating the very conflation it flagged; R5 renamed a declaration and left `propane.v(...)` calling it; R7 shipped an example that could not compile (`Objective` has a required ctor, `dimension()` is non-virtual). **A tooling defect invalidated a whole class of claim:** `grep` here is a shell function wrapping `ugrep --ignore-files`, which honours `.gitignore` — so every root-level `grep -r` silently excluded `archive/`, `nonfree/`, `literature/` and `tmp/`. It made me assert in a module document *and in a handoff to the module owner* that no Tarjan implementation existed, when `archive/graph/fn_Graph_tarjan.{hpp,cpp}` does, and my own stored memory said so — **I trusted the empty search over the note.** Every load-bearing negative from R2–R6 was re-run with `command grep`; all others held. **Two code defects, each approved mid-campaign:** `cl_Graph_Vertex.hpp:42` initialised `mOwner` to `gNoID` (`id_t`, unsigned) rather than `gNoOwner` (`proc_t`, signed), narrowing to **−1**, so `owner() == gNoOwner` was false — and the real evidence of intent is not the naming but the `std::min` ownership sweeps (`cl_Mesh_Partitioner.cpp:253-260`, `cl_FEM_Kernel.cpp:427`), which need the sentinel to act as **+∞** and got an absorbing element instead; the agent that fixed it corrected me twice, downward — the old value converted to 2⁶⁴−1 and **always** faulted where the new one (2³¹−1) is marginally *less* certain to, and `mesh::Basis` sets owner 0 at construction so **no mesh entity ever saw the default**. `tests/math/test_GraphVertex.cpp:27` had **pinned** the defect, its cast to `id_t` the tell that it matched the buggy initialiser rather than the contract. `scripts/update_doc_index.py` **resolved O5 durably**: the stale `hphirun`/`hphiTrun` names lived in the generator, so editing the generated `.dox` files would have been reverted on the next run — and fixing it surfaced a second pre-existing `--check` failure the first was masking. Scope is measured, not asserted: of 15 non-markdown files, eight are comment-only by filtering, and no §7.1-banned cohomology file was touched. **Reviewed, not verified** — one real compile gate ran (`-fsyntax-only -std=gnu++17 -Wall -Werror` on the test, clean); a build and `make check` are owed. `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37 throughout, citations 564 → 665 resolving. Open: **O1** (φ-region static Laplace form vs the `mt_maxwell_phi.cpp:26-46` mass term) and **O2** (static-condensation attribution — `messe2023.txt:509-517` credits it to Alves et al.) are Christian's; twelve homology API-drift rows are routed to Gregory Giard.
- [dl20260831_plugin_build_against_installed_prefix.md](dl20260831_plugin_build_against_installed_prefix.md) — **An installed prefix could not build a user plugin: the headers were fine, the shipped build glue was not.** Replaying the install rule into a staging directory (628 headers, no sources) showed the API side already worked — all three `example_user_*.cpp` compiled and linked into a `.so` with 62 undefined `belfem::` symbols left for the host. The glue failed twice: the four example decks' plugin `CMakeLists.txt` — the only plugin build files `make install` ships — pointed `-I` at `${BELFEM_DIR}/include`, the wrong root with no module dirs, and generated the `<belfem_user_api>` forwarder only for a source tree, so a real prefix died on the first include; and the two templates that *do* handle an installed prefix were never installed, the header glob matching `*.hpp` only. The working recipe was the one artifact the install did not deploy. Both fixed: the decks now derive a `BELFEM_HEADER_ROOT` and iterate one module list for both branches, and the templates plus the three annotated examples install to `share/belfem/templates/`. **Verified by build:** 4 decks × {staged prefix, source tree} = 8/8 `.so` produced, both templates build against the prefix, the new install rule deploys 5/5 files, `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37. **Not verified:** no plugin was `dlopen`ed by an installed binary, and Darwin is untouched. Closes R5/D2/D3 of `todo/user_api_header_install.md` without prejudging its flat-API replan.
- [dl20260831_examples_usermat_migration.md](dl20260831_examples_usermat_migration.md) — **`examples/block3d` and `examples/undulator2d` migrated onto the `tape_quench_usermat` plugin layout; block3d could not have run at all.** Its bulk conductor was declared through a `custom { }` subsection — the pre-0.9.0 spelling, refused with a rename message since 2026-08-29 — so the deck died in `MaterialFactory` before any physics. The same-day examples audit recorded "no deck uses it": true of the tracked tree, and `examples/block3d` is an untracked directory that was outside that sweep. `undulator2d` had no `custom` section but the same generation of scaffolding: a copy of `UserLibraryTemplate.cmake` at C++14, with `BELFEM_DIR` hardcoded to a collaborator's home directory and no `BELFEM_USER_DATA_DIR` — a copy that never followed the template forward, which is the actual failure here. Both decks now carry `src/` + `data/` and the reference CMakeLists (C++17, `<belfem_user_api>` shim, `PREFIX ""`, `BELFEM_USER_DATA_DIR` compiled in from `../data`): `MatData/` → `src/` → `usermat.so`, `lib/` → `src/` → `usersource.so`, with both decks' `file :` keys repointed. **Verified by build, not by run** — both plugins configured and built warning-free out-of-source against this tree and their init symbols were read back with `nm -D` and matched to the deck labels (`bulk_init`; `MyCurrent_init`/`MyCurrent_reverse_init`), which is the exact failure mode the ABI-stale-plugin incident showed is otherwise silent; neither deck was executed, since both need a mesh built from their `.geo` first. Two source comments were wrong and were corrected in passing: the undulator's current documented itself as "interpolation from data file" while reading none, and its init comment gave the material-plugin signature `_init(Material*)` for a function taking `SourceFunction*`. `ParamsGeo.dat` deliberately stays beside `undulator.geo`, which `Include`s it relative to itself. **C++17 checked while the question was open, and the framework standard is not in doubt** — `CMakeLists.txt:62`, `config/compiler/config_gcc.cmake:2`, and `-std=gnu++17` on the real compile line; both shipped templates already set 17 (`UserMaterialTemplate.cmake:207`, `UserLibraryTemplate.cmake:230`), so only these two private copies were behind. C++14 was **not** breaking them: the umbrella still compiles clean at `-std=gnu++14`, tested, and the framework uses one `if constexpr` and no `std::optional` — this is trap-prevention, not a bug fix. **Left open:** `config/compiler/config_icc.cmake:44` appends `-std=gnu++14` to `BELFEM_CXXFLAGS`, a dead flag that reads as policy (CMake emits the `CXX_STANDARD` flag after `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS` and the last `-std` wins, so Intel builds at 17 anyway) and should be deleted; the templates build a `MODULE` named `lib<name>.so` for a stated reason while `tape_quench_usermat` and its descendants build `SHARED` with `PREFIX ""`, so template-readers and example-readers are told different things; and `custom` is still absent from the schema's refused-input vocabulary — now a pure completeness gap, since no deck in the tree uses it.
- [dl20260831_dr113_fabric_drain_check.md](dl20260831_dr113_fabric_drain_check.md) — **DR-113 option (a) landed: `comm_drain_check()`, the DR-100 probe ladder made permanent.** Debug-only wrapper in `commtools` ( barrier → `MPI_Iprobe` sweep of both pair tags → allreduced verdict → `BELFEM_ERROR` on EVERY rank ) at five exchange-boundary chokepoints, so a stray dies at the boundary where it was born instead of poisoning a recv a month downstream. The plan round's load-bearing catch ( both auditors ): the planned "error → MPI_Abort" story was false where the check exists — debug throws — and Grok's allreduce fold shipped instead of Codex's policy-breaching direct abort: the reduction doubles as the anti-race fence and puts every rank on the same throw path. REVIEWED at xhigh ( Codex accept-with-changes applied, Grok accept ); build/check-fast/gate-deck/deliberate-stray runs owed. Option ( b ) tag separation stays deferred.
- [dl20260831_dr147_plugin_path_resolution.md](dl20260831_dr147_plugin_path_resolution.md) — **DR-147 fixed: one search order for every plugin `.so`, plus three by-catch defects.** The "layering block" that parked the row since 2026-08-29 was not one — `data_file()`'s body needs only `file_exists` and `gBelfemDataPath`, and `Add_Library.cmake:4,15` puts core and io on EVERY module's include path, so the search moved into `io/filetools` with **no `src/` CMake change** and `material::data_file()` became a forwarder. **Two of the row's own claims were wrong**: three plugin keys resolved TWO ways, not three (usermat and defect both used `data_file()`), and the fix cost zero caller churn, not "touches every caller". **Both plan-stage auditors refuted the proposed gate** — a unit test on a new function cannot run red where that function does not exist — which is why this landed the suite's **first MODULE target and first `dlopen`-based test** (Christian ruled for the fixture over a hand gate, on the DR-140 precedent). Grok settled the ownership question with the factory's own comment: one `SourceFunction` per group member from one `( file, label )`, so the leaked handle was N mappings, not one; both loaders now close guarded, and `SourceFunction` copy AND move are deleted. The code round then found the gate **was not hermetic** (`Add_Test.cmake` never clears the loader path) and it gained Grok's `EnvironmentIsNotContaminated` control; that the test's own comment about `dlopen` was wrong; and that `LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY` is load-bearing for discrimination, not tidiness. Codex found the docs were wrong on macOS (`$DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH`) in six places, and a **pre-existing** false claim in three documents: an unset `$BELFEM_DATA` does NOT leave only step 1 on an installed tree. **Both auditors refuted a rationale of mine** — `Material` copy/move were already deleted, so the "pre-existing hazard" reason for not touching them was false on its premise. One deliberate deviation from the audited plan (the bare subdirectory constant) was recorded BEFORE the code round and confirmed behaviour-preserving against HEAD. Split verdicts both ways, resolved not argued. **`make check` RAN GREEN**, with the new cases confirmed present in the built binaries by name and EXECUTED (5/5 + 12/12) rather than inferred — the file is `#ifdef`-wrapped and would otherwise have passed vacuously. Both halves of the red-then-green ran in one binary: the contamination control doubles as the pre-fix path, and it fails as required.
- [dl20260831_riva_n_guard_and_dr105_wall.md](dl20260831_riva_n_guard_and_dr105_wall.md) — **A setup gate for the riva law's n source, and the DR-105 np=8 "wall" re-read out of the surviving logs as a residual floor that was there the whole time.** Christian asked for an `n <= 1` checker in riva; riva's n<=1 handling is deliberate (`riva_rho_pl` takes it as the ohmic limit `ec/jc` — it IS DR-105's remedy), so an assert there would have re-created the `rho_piecewise` abort riva exists to avoid. The check went in at setup instead: `check_riva_n_source()` (`cl_Material.cpp:790`) called from **three** sites — `set_resistivity_law`, `set_n_function`, `set_constant` — so installation order cannot dodge it, plus a factory guard for a usermat that registers jc but no n (`cl_MaterialFactory.cpp:248-256`), which release ran fully normal via NaN, silently. **The three-vendor round found four holes in the first draft:** `+inf` passed `tN > 1.0` while the message promised "finite"; `set_constant` bypassed the gate; a source-less riva material was accepted; and a constant NaN died on `constant_property`'s unrelated debug assert before reaching the riva message. **C3 was my error and both auditors caught it** — I claimed the shipped tables' B-spline control net bounds the interpolant above 1.01; database eval is piecewise **Lagrange** (`cl_Database.cpp:205,235`), not a Bernstein hull, so the control net is a build-pipeline invariant, not an eval-time bound. Split verdict on the *global* table test (Codex: rejects above-T_crit values riva never reads; Grok ~70% misclassification) **ruled kept global** — `/meta/n_floor` is 1.02 in all five shipped tables, so a stored value <= 1 anywhere marks a table that escaped the pipeline; the gate tests the construction convention, not riva's read set. Grok separately found live drift at `resistivity_laws.md:157` ("any table output"). **DR-105 could not be closed, so its row was made current instead:** the reconstructed T5 deck is repaired ( `usermat` + `critical temperature`, residue (1) UNBLOCKED ), the shipped deck is now a different model on `riva` — giving the row two independent remedies where it recorded one, and its defect rewrite is residue (3)'s physics ruling taken in code — and **residue (2)'s "floor-collapse at step 264" framing is RETRACTED in the row**, not merely amended. Codex's first dispatch at `gpt-5.6-sol`/`xhigh` died at vendor capacity after 156k tokens — **the sol-vs-terra comparison §9.1 wants is still unmeasured.** Probe 12/12, and **`make check` green** (Christian, branch `claude` @ `aecea892` + working
tree) — though the suite carries no riva-gate case, so the four closed states rest on the probe. **Separately, DR-105:** the rebuilt `tape_quench_usermat` moved `piecewise` -> `riva`, removing the abort class independently of the 08-29 `critical temperature` remedy, and its defect rewrite is the row's physics ruling taken in code — but it discharges neither run residue (different mesh, mumps, no Anderson). **And the np=8 wall is not a wall:** both runs used the IDENTICAL binary (`dcca23cc`, verified from both headers, so the "debug binary" confound is common-mode and eliminated), np=8 spent 229 floor-retry warnings over 264 steps (87%) against serial's 47 over 4191 (1.1%), and serial is at the floor at its own step 264 too — **nothing happened at step 264**, it is just where 20 consecutive retries ran out. The discriminator is the achievable residual: serial descends 3.5 decades to 4e-7, np=8 sits flat at 1.6-2.2e-4 through every iteration of all twenty retries, ~200x above tolerance. A ~500x residual-floor difference from rank count alone, insensitive to Δt and to relaxation — not a physics wall, and not yet mechanism-diagnosed.
- [dl20260831_examples_schema_audit.md](dl20260831_examples_schema_audit.md) — **All 15 example decks audited against `doc/input_schema.yaml`; of the seven errors `belfem-conf check` reported, five were the checker's fault — and the checker's worst defect was one it never reported.** `_spec_for` matched deck sections to schema entries by exact name only, so `linear magnetic`, `linear thermal` and `nonlinear magnetic` — reached through `resolution_chains` and `aliases` — matched NO entry, and a section with no entry is skipped in silence: every key of the magnetic solver configuration in three decks was unchecked (planting `another bogus : 7 ;` in `linear magnetic` passed). Fixed with alias/chain resolution in which an exact entry still beats an alias, verified in both directions on the magnetic/thermal key split; `per_field_override` merges `compute conditioning` / `mumps error analysis` into the field-specific spellings only, per `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:4671-4689`. Coverage on 3D_tapestack 47 → 61 keys. Two more false positives fixed: `builtin : Pb38Sn62` is a legal solder (`to_pair`, `core/stringtools.hpp:312`, matches `Pb`/38 and `Sn`/62 in any order; `Alloy` reports `PureMetal`) — the schema said so only in prose, so `also_accepts` is now structured and applied; and `phase : 0` is legal because `check_unit` compares dimension CODES and `rad` carries no exponents, so the checker now resolves the signature instead of testing the dimension's name. **Real deck defects, left for a ruling:** `examples/corc_solder` lost its `mesh` header (FIXED — the block is now byte-identical to `corc_periodic`'s, and the deck validates clean at 58 keys that had never been reachable) and did not fail cleanly — the buffer then starts with `{`, and `Section::create_children` evaluates `mBuffer( k - 1 )` at `k == 0` with unsigned `index_t`, an out-of-bounds read that ANY deck whose first non-comment line is `{` will hit; `examples/sidecoating` loads a `lib/build/libcustom.so` it does not ship; `target iterations` in `tape_quench_usermat`'s `nonlinear thermal` is read by nobody. Reviewed, not verified — no deck was executed. Follow-up in the same session: `range: [0, 8]` / `out_of_range: fatal` is now enforced (severity from the schema, not assumed; `constraint:` stays prose and unparsed), which exposed a **fourth** false positive — the `int` rule rejected `3.7` although `Section::get_int` is `round( get_real )` (`cl_Input_Section.cpp:349`), so it is now a warning naming the rounded value, with `_round_half_away` checked against `libm` on the half-cases Python's banker's rounding would get wrong — and a schema inconsistency: the thermal `anderson depth` lacked `out_of_range: fatal` although it is the same `BELFEM_ERROR` as the magnetic one (`:4273` / `:4445`). **Also:** a concurrent session appended un-indented lines to the `resistivity type` note in `doc/input_schema.yaml`, breaking the block scalar and making the file unparseable for all three tools; re-indented here, their wording untouched, and flagged because a read-modify-write cannot distinguish a mangled peer edit from one it clobbered. **Launcher question answered:** `Allrun`/`run.conf` did NOT go stale with the unified `belfem` executable — `pick_executable()` and `examples/scripts/README.md` were already updated, and `run.conf`'s keys are gmsh/MPI concerns. What HAD not kept up: `examples/README.md` §2 (2026-08-10) still listed `belfem`/`hphirun`/`hphiTrun` as co-equal and never mentioned the launcher — rewritten; `examples/scripts/Allclean:31` removed `circuitAnalysis.out` while `electricalCircuit.cpp:42` writes `.txt`, so it never cleaned the file it exists to clean — found by the Codex sweep, verified, fixed; and the per-deck `Allrun` wrappers are down to **3 of 14**, so the documented `./Allrun` usage works on three decks — left as a fork for a ruling (restore, or drop the concept). Also fixed: a nested `CLAUDE.md` under `examples/` aborted `scripts/update_doc_index.py` entirely, since every collected page needs a `PAGE_GROUPS` entry; a `NOT_DOCUMENTATION` set now excludes bootstrap files at all three collection sites, verified narrow (a real page in the same directory still trips the check). **`NDIMS` deleted:** it fed only `gmsh -$NDIMS`, and measurement killed it in both directions — `-2` vs `-3` on a volume-less geometry is BYTE-IDENTICAL (gmsh's 3D pass returns in 31 us), so the only value any deck set was a no-op; while `-2` on a 3D geometry exits 0 and writes a mesh with every tet missing (`helix.geo`: 26084 triangles, 0 of 217223 tets), sailing past `ensure_mesh`'s did-gmsh-produce-the-file guard. `Allrun` hardcodes `-3`; all three shipped `run.conf` files held nothing but `NDIMS=2` and went with it, so no deck carries one now (a leftover local file degrades to `ignoring unknown key`, rest still applied). **`ensure_mesh` no longer assumes gmsh:** `corc_solder` has no `.geo` — it builds its cable in `python/main.py` — so the launcher now names the generator script instead of reporting a geometry file that was never meant to exist. **Header guard added to `Section::create_children`** and audited by Codex (gpt-5.6-sol/high) + Grok (grok-4.6/high) -- the FIRST dispatch of both produced no audit (Codex at capacity; `grok-4.2` is not an accepted model name) and is recorded so it is not mistaken for one. The two SPLIT on the predicate: Grok said keep `k > 0` (release day), Codex said `k > mStartFlag` (the real invariant is that the header lies inside the section's own span; nested, the read is in-bounds but grabs the PARENT's brace and names the child `"{"`). Codex adopted after its safety claim was CHECKED -- scanning every deck for a brace without a header returns zero hits -- and Grok's objection that the message would lie was fixed, not dismissed. Probe evidence against `HEAD` vs fixed: headerless root goes from `Cell index out of bounds: 4294967295` to a named error; nested `solver { { } }` from a silent section named `"{"` to a named error; all 14 decks still parse. **Left open deliberately:** `--tSectionCount` is unsigned and unconditional (`:85`), so ONE stray `}` silently swallows every later section -- verified, `section_exists("solver")` goes false with no error -- but guarding it would newly reject decks that parse today.
- [dl20260831_dr120_parse_gate.md](dl20260831_dr120_parse_gate.md) — **DR-120's owed run gate RAN GREEN in seven cases; the row was never a pending fix, only a pending gate.** The session opened on the premise that DR-120 "would be an easy fix" — but its code landed 2026-08-28 and both Aug-30 binaries were confirmed to carry the error strings before anything was run, so the Aug-29 suite had already run against a binary with the checks compiled in. What the row owed was the negative-deck abort gate, and running it needed a mesh: `Controller::set_params` is called from `MaxwellFactory::create_controller()` (`cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:1047`), *after* kernel construction, and **no `.msh` in the tree fits a current deck** (`cmake-build-debug/tapestack3d.msh` is a Jul-2 two-volume mesh; `examples/3D_tapestack` now wants blocks `1` and `2:8`). `examples/2D_Tapestack` meshed by gmsh 4.13.1 into the scratchpad carried it. **One control** — unmodified deck accepted, 12 completed timesteps — and **six abort cases**, each inside `Controller::set_params()` after mesh/cuts/edges/connectivities, each naming its key: magnetic `max iterations : -1` (`:4211`), `max 30`/`min 200` cross-check (`:4226`), `stall window : -1` (`:4256`), magnetic `watchdog window : -1` (`:4282`), thermal `max iterations : -1` (`:4412` — the row's literal gate), segregated `coupling factor : 0` (`:4483`). **The discriminator is the 0-vs-−1 pair on `watchdog window`:** the control deck ships the documented disable sentinel `0` and passes, `-1` aborts, so the check separates legal from illegal rather than rejecting the key outright. **What did NOT run is the pre-fix half** — the silent `uint` wrap is a static argument from `get_int` = `round( get_real )` (`cl_Input_Section.cpp:349-352`) plus the `uint` members, and re-measuring it needs a rebuild with the checks reverted, judged disproportionate for a P3; row and devlog both say so rather than letting "gate ran green" imply more. **The row's citations were stale by ~210 lines** (`:3997-4018`/`:4198-4219` vs the actual `:4208-4227`/`:4408-4428`) — and the 2026-08-30 currentness sweep had *already* re-anchored two DR-120 citations, which drifted again inside a day because `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp` is under active uncommitted modification. That is exactly the failure `CLAUDE.md` names for the input contract, and whether the register should anchor by searchable token the way `input_schema.yaml` does is left as a question for Christian, not a change this session made. By-catch, observation only and deliberately not filed: `coupling factor` is read with `get_int` into a `real` member, so `2.5` silently becomes 3. **DR-120 STRUCK and archived the same session on Christian's ruling** — lifted into `debt_register_closed.md` with ID and description struck and the status column intact, `[P]` recounted 9 → 8 (8 `[P]` + 3 `[W]` = 11 live, 138 closed), `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37. No code changed, so no audit round was owed. **Struck is not verified — and this is the mild case of that rule: the post-fix behaviour is verified by execution, the pre-fix claim is reasoned.** **By-catch:** the cell-count check on the archived row exposed **two pre-existing malformed rows in the closed register** — seven cells in a six-column table, so the blocking-1.0 column rendered the wrong text. `~~DR-111~~` carried a literal shell pipe inside a code span (`strings <binary> | grep -c …`) — **GFM splits a table row on `|` even inside backticks**, escaped to `\|`; `~~DR-129~~` had a stray separator between its STRUCK paragraph and the retained fix-shape note. Delimiters only, no wording changed; both registers now pass a per-row cell-count check.
- [dl20260831_dr153_meshchecker_orphan.md](dl20260831_dr153_meshchecker_orphan.md) — **DR-153 struck: the orphaned `fem/postproc` MeshChecker deleted — after it turned out to be holding a real fix hostage.** Commit `d205680a` ("fixes for pyramid", 2026-03-12) had landed the PYRA13/PYRA14 → `swap_pyra14` dispatch in the DEAD copy only, so the compiled checker was still repairing second-order pyramids corners-only — reversed base, mid-edge nodes bound to the pre-swap edges, a corrupted element rather than a reoriented one on any CW gmsh mesh carrying PYRA13/14. That made the orphan's capture count THREE (INC-185's doc fix, the DR-111 vendor cite, `d205680a` itself). The fix was ported to `src/fem/kernel/cl_MeshChecker.cpp` and confirmed by independent derivation: the known-good `swap_pyra5` realizes the orientation-reversing reflection (ξ,η,ζ)→(−η,−ξ,ζ), and pushing all 14 `cl_IF_PYRA14.hpp` coordinates through it yields exactly `swap_pyra14`'s swap set {0↔2, 5↔6, 7↔8, 9↔11}, cross-checked against the element edge topology; PYRA13 rides along (identical coords on nodes 0-12, no index above 11 touched). Then both orphan files `git rm`'d — kernel ruled canonical as a strict superset — the postproc doc README row dropped, and a latent shared-include-guard trap (`CL_MESHCHECKER_HPP` in both headers, right one winning on include-path ordering luck) died with it. Fix criterion RAN (grep returns exactly one path); `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37. **The ported dispatch is REVIEWED, NOT VERIFIED — no CW second-order-pyramid deck exists, `make check` does not reach the pyramid path, and the port landed on Christian's instruction with the in-session derivation as its review, no vendor round.**
- [dl20260831_dr135_gate_and_background_field_ruling.md](dl20260831_dr135_gate_and_background_field_ruling.md) — **DR-135's gate needed no new mesh, and the run DISCRIMINATES the defect rather than merely executing it.** Three wrong turns first, each of which the row's one-line gate had actually stated: the trigger is a `topology` symmetry DOMAIN and not a `dirichlet` BC; "crossing" is load-bearing, because `tag_periodic_sidesets` ( `cl_Mesh_PeriodicityFactory.cpp:1754-1775` ) silently OVERWRITES the domain type of any sideset lying wholly on a periodic plane with `Periodic`, so a symmetry sideset ON the target face can never fire; and the current drive cannot coexist with the trigger, since an outer-boundary symmetry surface takes the cohomology to 0 generators. **Christian's redirect is what closed it:** drive the model with a `background` field, which is imposed weakly through its own stiffness matrix and spends no generator. Result on the EXISTING helix mesh — `SideSet 133: 16 node(s) are condensed onto sources outside the free set`, prescribed 2 -> 143, 17/17 timesteps, 0 rejections, exit 0, and the imposed H tracking the analytic ramp to six figures at every step. Discrimination proven against the mesh's own `/hanging/nodes/` table: of sideset 133's 17 condensed nodes, **16 sources lie OUTSIDE the sideset**, so the pre-fix path would have lost 16 constraints. The equivalence clause is WAIVED as unconstructible ( the sources are a node rim, and a deck pins sidesets, not arbitrary nodes ), not merely unrun. **DR-136 struck on Christian's design ruling — the background field is imposed WEAKLY by design:** the strong variant would force removal of the automatic gauge pins ( with phi prescribed on the boundary an autopin becomes an INCONSISTENT constraint, not a redundant one ) plus a phi-domain gradient for the transform-aware source value, all for a handful of dofs; the reasoning and the `pin_dirichlet_dof` veto moved into `doc/input_file_reference.md` so they outlive the row. **DR-137 struck as accepted exposure and NEVER EXECUTED** — its scope is wider than the row said ( `iterate_magnetic()` carries the same ceiling-before-watchdog ordering ), and the attempted reproducer FAILED to discriminate: n = 40 with a 1e-16 tolerance grinds monotonically to a floor instead of plateauing, so every iterate is a new best and the watchdog is never ELIGIBLE — windows 30 and 10 gave byte-identical logs. By-catch recorded but NOT filed, per Christian: a `dirichlet` BC naming an unowned sideset is silently discarded via the `mEmptySideset` fallback ( a deck may name sideset 9999 and converge ).
- [dl20260831_debt_register_archival.md](dl20260831_debt_register_archival.md) — **Debt register reconciled: five fully struck rows (DR-109, DR-111, DR-151, DR-152, DR-154) moved to the closed register; 16 live and 136 closed physical IDs remain, with no overlap or unstruck closure-marked status.** Static register audit only.
- [dl20260830_dr109_pairverdict_bisect.md](dl20260830_dr109_pairverdict_bisect.md) — **`mPairVerdict` bisected to its birth commit: it was born without a consumer, and the deletion that DR-26 recorded as landed is sitting unclaimed in `stash@{0}`.** `git log --all -S` over `src/homology/` returns exactly two commits. `6347dd7b` ("saving work in progress", 2026-06-16) creates `mPairVerdict`, `classify_periodic_pairs()`, `CutPairVerdict`, `CutPairInfo` and `CutSet::cut_pattern()` **in the same commit** that rewrites `CutSet::create_duplicates` into its live three-way branch and writes the comment promising a "Step 6c ( consuming mPairVerdict )". `69fe2a58` two days later only strips `#DIAG 4h` probes — it matches the pickaxe because a header comment changed wording, not because the data flow moved. So the producer and the promise shipped together and the consumer was never written; Step 9's original-identity keying solved the real problem instead, and `todo/closed/periodic_thin_cut_continuity_fix.md:111-129` records that pairing the induced duplicates was **rejected as unsafe**, not deferred — it would overwrite a node's single `mPeriodic` and corrupt slave T-matrices through `set_entity_dependencies()`. **That one comment has now misled four separate audit rounds** (2026-06-17 fix-option C, 2026-07-10 the three-AI round recorded as INC-030, 2026-08-13, and DR-109 itself on 2026-08-26). **Two register rows are in open conflict and both are wrong:** DR-109 (open, P2) asserts `create_duplicates` *consumes* the map and rates the 64-cut silent cap on that consumption; DR-26 (closed) asserts the machinery was deleted on 2026-08-24. Codex `terra`/`xhigh` and Grok `4.6`/`xhigh`, blind and parallel from a brief that stated the conflict without naming a side, converge: **0 reads, 1 declaration, 2 writes**, indirect paths (accessors, friends, `NDEBUG` blocks, `save_debug_meshes`, serialization, tests, `nonfree/`, CMake-conditional sources) all empty; `classify_periodic_pairs()` is **mesh-state pure** — no flag, no entity, no index, no ordering — so removing the `:82` call cannot move a solver result; and `create_duplicates` takes no verdict argument at all (`cl_CutSet.hpp:85-86`), branching on `mNodeBitset` alone (`:125-159`). **Q5 was a real question and both swept it independently:** the only `unsigned long long` / `1ull` sites in `src/homology/` are `CutPairInfo::mUnionBits` and the four locals at `cl_CutProcessor.cpp:750-768`, all inside the deletion set — the live cut-membership path is a runtime-sized `DynamicBitset`, uncapped — so **DR-109's mechanism dissolves with the deletion rather than migrating**. The recovery is the actionable part: `stash@{0}` ("bisect: session homology/mesh changes (DR-26/102/103/13)") holds the exact 2026-08-24 edit across all four files **plus** the corrected `cl_CutSet.cpp` comment that cites the closed plan instead of promising 6c — but it is **not** a clean cherry-pick, carrying DR-103's `check_midside` signature change and `src/mesh/` edits alongside. **Verdict: safe to delete — reviewed, not verified** (three static traces, no build, no periodic run). Four things owed *with* the deletion, not after: rewrite `cl_CutSet.cpp:107-123` or INC-030 recurs a fifth time, strike `todo/test_hardening_campaign.md` **R3d** (an open unticked test contract aimed at the function being removed), close DR-109 as **dissolved** rather than fixed, and correct the DR-26 row that records a closure which never reached git. By-catch, out of scope and confirmed by reading: `CutSet` allocates `mNodeBitset` at `cl_CutSet.cpp:31` and `~CutSet()` deletes only `mBitset`. **Executed 2026-08-31 (same devlog, appended section):** the deletion landed by hand — homology set only, the stash's DR-103/`src/mesh/` freight left unclaimed — with all four owed items done (comment rewritten, R3d struck, DR-109 struck as dissolved with the `[W]` recount, DR-26 corrected); zero remaining symbol references tree-wide, both TUs pass a production-flag syntax-only compile, `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37; still reviewed, not verified — `make check` owed.
- [dl20260830_dr39_closing_plan_audit.md](dl20260830_dr39_closing_plan_audit.md) — **DR-39 ruled closed by executing (a) and re-homing (b); the harvest plan made implementation-ready, then audited — and the audit found the doc inventory wrong by nine sites.** Christian's second ruling: (b) `Pulse`/`PWL` is a feature at parser-plan Phase 6, not debt, so DR-39 strikes once (a)'s gates run. Asked whether PWL is even in scope for HTS magnets, the answer went in as a Phase-6 recommendation: **`PULSE` earns its place** (its trapezoid IS the magnet duty cycle and the AC-loss cycle, which `Ramp`/`Sigmoid` only approximate), **`PWL` does not** on current evidence — a measured-profile replay nobody has asked for, and the leg that costs most (variable-length payload ⇒ `share`/`receive`, `Cell< real >` only). **Eight defects found in the plan before dispatch**, two of them fatal to R4's switching gate: the pre-switch amplitude window was HALF a period (20 ms = 400 steps at 50 Hz, not 200) and it INCLUDED a post-switch sample (`shift()` fires the latch before `compute_MNA_matrix()` stamps, so step 500 is already the closed regime); and **`t_switch` at exactly 25 ms is a floating-point tie, not determinism** — 500 accumulated additions of `50e-6` differ from `1.25/50` at ~1e-18 with unpredictable sign, so the latch fires at step 500 or 501 by build, and possibly DIFFERENTLY in the hand-built and netlist runs, which would have broken R6 for a reason having nothing to do with the parser (moved to 24.975 ms; firing instant unchanged). R1′ was also demoted to conditional: the demo's own ω update is a **no-op on the first iteration**, so it cannot protect the iterate most at risk. **The jury round (Codex terra/high + Grok 4.6/high, blind, both read-only) then returned three P0s.** The largest: **R8 named four documentation sites; there are thirteen** — and the decisive one was found by neither auditor, `scripts/update_doc_index.py:124`, **the generator** that would have regenerated the dead binary name into `doxygen_nav.dox` and `groups.dox` on the next refresh. Grok's best catch, `examples/scripts/README.md:63-72`, is user-facing and ALREADY FALSE (says the binary *reads* `circuitAnalysis.txt`; it writes it). Both auditors independently caught that **R6 would not compile under `USE_HDF5=OFF`** — `FileGuard` lives inside the HDF5 `#ifdef` and R6 writes a `.cir`. Six P1s confirmed at `file:line`, including that the 1e-12 gate is undefined at the sine's zeros (which a 400-step window contains exactly), that Appendix A's ε₀ anchors point at the MAGNETIC Newton and R9 would have regressed the register by copying them, and that **R1′ cannot repair a first-iterate overflow at all** (`solve()` mutates `mX` with no rollback). Grok also refuted the plan's named failure mode: a 10 V source cannot reach the 18.45 V `exp` bound — **the real risk is commutation at the zero crossings**, and the mitigation is a smaller Δt, not relaxation. O2 resolved: keep ±1 %. **By-catch, fixed in the tree with Christian's approval:** four documentation claims that were false *independently* of DR-39, so parking them behind R7's gates would have shipped them — `examples/scripts/README.md` and `examples/scripts/Allrun` both said the binary READS `circuitAnalysis.txt` (it reads no input file at all; the only stream is an `ofstream`), and `circuit_usage_guide.md` §11 was wrong in three ways about the demo's own loop (MNA recomputed every attempt, not only on Δt change; `shift()` at the TOP, not the end; output written after acceptance, so each line is that step's own result) plus a dead `todo/deferred/` pointer calling the shipped ngspice importer "deferred, nothing written". **No C++ touched, nothing built or run — reviewed, not verified.**
- [dl20260830_dr39_ruling.md](dl20260830_dr39_ruling.md) — **DR-39(a) ruled: no `CircuitSolver`, delete the demo — and the already-audited harvest plan had been overtaken three times in two days.** The extraction is refuted for good: the two copies (`cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:2528-2585`, `electricalCircuit.cpp:134-164`) are the same ~15-line loop differing only in constant source and print box, and the one difference that ever looked numerical — `tEpsilon0_Circuit` at the loop TOP vs BOTTOM — is structural, since `residual()` is `norm(mRHS)/mRHSnorm` and holds no state. **That anchor was stale in the row** (`:900-903` had drifted into `compute_jacobian_and_rhs`'s `dIdV` stamping as the file grew; corrected to `:997-1001`, claim intact). **What the plan no longer knew:** DR-118 was fixed *in place* 2026-08-29 rather than deleted, so §1's "and a broken one" premise is gone and R9 no longer discharges it; **R1 and R3 are already satisfied in-tree** (`solve_attempt`/`take_step` and `create_rlc_circuit`+`RLCRingAcrossRejectedStep` in `test_ElectricalCircuit.cpp`), so the harvest goes into that existing TU — a second TU would duplicate the driver, which is a poor look on a de-duplication row — with R1′ reduced to adding the ω relaxation behind a defaulted parameter; and O3 is resolved by events (DR-123 struck, the rejected-step switch case now green). **The deletion case is unweakened:** the binary takes no input file, solves only Frederic's circuit, ships installed, and three of its four circuits name a `SourceType` enum that `rg -w` finds **nowhere** in the tree. The file-driven-runner counter-case lost on freeze-day grounds — that is a feature to fund (parser Phases 4-5), and keeping the stub ships the placeholder instead. **The hole the ordering protects:** `create_diode` appears in the whole suite only in the structural adjacency test, so the diode Newton has no transient gate at all and R5 is its first. **DR-39 reduces to (b), with three constraints written into the row:** it is not circuit-scoped (`boundary_condition_function_type` feeds Maxwell and thermal BCs too — an Input Contract change in both artifacts), `mValues` is a fixed 7-slot `Cell<real>` broadcast whole so **PWL does not fit at all** (variable-length payload ⇒ `share`/`receive`), and PWL storage must be `Cell<real>` because the header bans `cl_Vector.hpp` on plugin-ABI grounds. Register + plan + index only; **no source touched, nothing built or run — reviewed, not verified.**
- [dl20260830_mumps_error_analysis_split.md](dl20260830_mumps_error_analysis_split.md) — **`mumps error analysis`: the MUMPS ADD diagnostic split out of `compute conditioning`, all 16 steps landed, deck gates owed.** Blind naming jury (split 1-1; Christian pinned the name on the ground that the flag is a *no-op without MUMPS*, so a vendor-free name would sit inert in every STRUMPACK deck). Two plan-audit rounds: round 1 **both auditors independently found D1** — `arm_conditioning_thermal` did two jobs behind one early return, and the draft's re-gate would have silently dropped `set_symmetric(true)`, sending every existing deck's thermal field through the nonsymmetric ARPACK driver; the shipped `examples/3D_tapestack` deck is exactly that shape. Christian's ruling to delete the κ₂ label branch turned D1 from *accept* into *design out*: `set_symmetric` moved to `setup_thermal_eigen()`, called from **both** attach paths, leaving the arming functions pure ICNTL(11) toggles. Round 2 caught the plan contradicting itself — a value pin cannot detect a lost Lanczos driver, because both drivers converge to the same ratio, so the real tripwire is R12's `BELFEM_ASSERT(is_symmetric())`. Code audit PASS both vendors, scoped **by symbol** because the shared checkout carried other sessions' work in the same file; its C1 fix replaced a first-caller-wins latch that would have consumed the thermal check against default flags. A **pre-existing warning that had never fired on any coupled deck** was repaired in the same move (D3). The last open question was answered from the sibling reuse plan and then ruled on: under Newton the eigen ratio and COND1 come from **different matrices** — `jacobian()` returns `mSystemMatrix` — which Christian ruled expected, unblocking R8b. Two prose sweeps, each of which caught a real technical error rather than prose. Reviewed, not verified: Christian's own run verified the warning, the unified label and the 76-column box; six deck gates are staged in `build/gates/` and owed.
- [dl20260830_dr111_quad4ts_winding.md](dl20260830_dr111_quad4ts_winding.md) — **DR-111 struck: the 2-D thin-shell winding is a forced CONVENTION, not a factory defect, and the row's own root cause and severity were both wrong.** `process_nodes_line2` rotates the facet tangent CLOCKWISE (`cl_ThinShellFactory.cpp:923-924`), so `n` is derived from `t` and reversing the facet reverses both — the negative determinant is UNCONDITIONAL, not orientation-dependent as the row said (replication reproduces the gdb value `-5.000000e-12` for `+x`, `+y`, 37° and the reversed facet; the 3-D branch gives `+2.0e-16` throughout). It is also FORCED: `mS` comes from node order while `∇ξ` comes from the facet, so with the edge-index contract exactly one corner order is legal and **no permutation is both CCW and sign-neutral**. Fix moved to the consumer on Christian's ruling (factory untouched, changes confined to QUAD4TS): `dV_quad4ts = abs( dV_ts )` and `Pipette::measure_quad4ts`, `|det J|` being the EXACT change-of-variables weight since `N` is reference-space and `B = J⁻¹dN/dξ` survives an orientation-reversing map. **Grok caught that the obvious implementation ships inert:** `invJ2D3D` shares the `mDetJ` cache and the thermal kernels call `B(k)` before `dV(k)`, so at k=0 the cache hits and abs-on-the-assignment never executes — abs on the RETURN is immune (INC-549 class). **Severity corrected too:** for an all-thin-shell thermal domain `M`/`K`/`f` are uniformly negated and the system is homogeneous in them, so temperatures are UNCHANGED — the DR-06 cold-spot claim does not apply; the real exposure is a MIXED thin-shell/volume domain. Both vendors independently flagged the same false control-flow sentence in my comments (magnetic does dispatch to the wrapper; safety is that `0.25·h·L > 0` makes the abs an identity), and both caught six fabricated `file:line` anchors of mine. `make check` green, fix confirmed present by SYMBOL not by build, 750+ coupled timesteps on a quench deck — **but that binary was release-configured with the guard compiled out, so it cannot discriminate and the row is struck with an assertions-ON gate owed. Struck is not verified.** By-catch DR-151/152/153.
- [dl20260830_scls_flavor_presets.md](dl20260830_scls_flavor_presets.md) — **`make check` could not link against the `debug` SCLS flavor, and the one flag responsible had already been adjudicated.** `config_mkl.cmake` emitted `-llapack -lcblas -lblas` unconditionally; only `/opt/scls/gcc` ships a `libcblas.so`, and there it is a **symlink to `libopenblas.so`** — the same file `libblas`/`liblapack` point at, a redundant third alias. Reference netlib has none, so `ld: cannot find -lcblas`. The flag never provided a symbol on this path ( zero undefined `cblas_` in `libbelfem.a` or the test objects; Armadillo is `ARMA_USE_WRAPPER` on the Fortran symbols; debug SLATE reaches BLAS through `blaspp`/`lapackpp` ), which is D1 of the plan, closed earlier by both auditors on link *and* runtime evidence. A `find_library` probe was written and **backed out** — the plan flags exactly that as a `$SCLS`-repoint cache hazard, and the decided form is to drop the flag. **Then the plan was cut down twice in one day, both Christian's calls, and the audits are the casualty worth recording.** First: no autodetection — the knob selects, the probe verifies, a miss is a configure-time error — which withdrew the tri-state `AUTO` design and dissolved **D4, D6, D7, D8, O1, O2, O5**, i.e. most of round 1 including its most serious finding, since D4's hazard lived entirely inside the `STREQUAL` chain `AUTO` required. Second: **read `$SCLS_FLAVOR`**, which is what the earlier design was circling without naming — and O6 then closed with *no candidate ladder and no vendor knob*, because the netlib names already resolve to whatever a flavor ships. Method note: **both audits were thorough and both were scoped to the wrong question**; a round asking "what does the environment already tell us?" before "how do we detect it?" would have reached the smaller plan first. The distinction written into the scope guards so this is not read as a flip-flop: **a preset reads a declaration and is a default any `-D` overrides; autodetection searches the host.** Landed: `find_scls_flavor.cmake` at `CMakeLists.txt:74`, **above both `option()` calls** — the whole trick, since `option()` defaults only when the cache entry is absent, so a default computed after the declaration does nothing *and does it silently*; `find_scls.cmake` runs at `:163`, far too late. `debug` → `USE_DEBUG=ON`; `gcc` → both OFF; `mkl` → `USE_MKL=ON`; no `$SCLS` → both OFF and silent. Basename derivation when `SCLS_FLAVOR` is unset; `STATUS` and no preset for `cea` or a private prefix ( deliberately not fatal ); `WARNING` on a half-sourced shell where the two disagree. **Verified by ten cases against a standalone CMake project**, including `-DUSE_MKL=OFF` beating an `mkl` preset and — the one that will look like a bug — a tree configured as `debug` and reconfigured as `mkl` **keeping its cached knobs**, because `option()` cannot overwrite a populated cache: a build tree is effectively per-flavor, and a repoint is not supported so much as caught. **Deliberate compatibility break:** `USE_DEBUG` now defaults OFF without SCLS, so a fresh non-SCLS clone builds Release where it built Debug; `CLAUDE.md` fixed the same session, and `check_doc_claims.py` can no longer see that default at all ( `:135` matches a literal `ON|OFF`, and it is now `${BELFEM_DEFAULT_USE_DEBUG}` ) — recorded as a known gap, `USE_TEST`/`USE_VTK` unaffected, 37/37 green. **Nothing built or configured inside BELFEM itself; the probe work R2–R7 has not started; and the landed CMake has had no audit round.**
- [dl20260830_dr138_dr139_gates.md](dl20260830_dr138_dr139_gates.md) — **DR-138's owed coupled A/B was mis-scoped, and re-scoping it produced the session's only clean result.** The controller's reject/retry sequence on the circuit object is call-for-call what `RLCRingAcrossRejectedStep` already runs, so a coupled A/B would only re-measure green arithmetic; what the coupled path actually adds is the **FEM leg**, which carries DR-138's exact shape — `ElectricalCircuit::shift_back()` reverts `FEMTwoTerminals`' registers then calls `compute_current()`, which reads the **un-reverted** `mIn`. Executed by scratchpad probe: the leg is safe **entirely because `set_current_and_voltage` is accept-only** (`finalize()` is its sole call chain); `shift_back()` protects `mIn` not at all — the diverged-iterate control survives the rollback into the retry's stamp and moves the terminal current 91x more than the faithful case. Latent fragility recorded. The coupled deck (`examples/circuit`, 2 ranks) **rejects timestep 17 naturally** and the retry path now runs six times without abort, with DR-118's accepted-state-only output contract holding coupled and the binary symbol-checked for the fix — but **no rejection-free reference twin could be produced, so the trajectory A/B is NOT established**. Attribution trap avoided: the 08-27 `hphirun` abort at this same rejection is DR-115's (its rank guard was committed 2.6 h AFTER that log was written), not DR-138's. **Two of my own claims refuted in-session and recorded as such:** the "-56 dB residual floor 24 dB above tolerance" was an extraction error (real plateau -70 dB, later attempts within 1-3 dB and tightening with dt), and the reference run built on it still rejected — by a different mechanism, reaching 20 dB *below* its own tolerance at Picard 2 and then diverging, so the Picard exit is not simply "residual < tolerance". An intrinsic continuity test was discarded as measuring curvature, not offset. **DR-139's gate then RAN GREEN on a non-MKL debug tree and found something worse than the leak it was looking for:** no `SpMatrix` in any leak record (all 34 definitely-lost blocks are OpenMPI/PMIx `MPI_Init` allocations), but 22 error contexts — **14 invalid writes, 8 invalid reads, every one BELFEM** — expose a live heap buffer overflow in `ElectricalCircuit::compute_adjacency()`: node vertex containers are sized (`:462`) counting only passive neighbors plus the self-insert, while `:534` also inserts the unknown-current vertex (voltage source / current source / switch) into that container, uncounted. Exact match to the record on `SolvedDcDivider` (2 slots, 3 inserts, *"0 bytes after a block of size 16"*). Masked in many circuits because sizing counts duplicates while population dedups, so parallel R/L/C over-allocate and absorb it. A first diagnosis blaming the switch self-insert (`:541`, a real but separate second shortfall) was refuted by the DC divider having no switch. `BELFEM_ASSERT` in `insert_vertex` cannot catch it — it checks the counter WIDTH, not the capacity. **98/98 tests pass while the code writes out of bounds.** **Now FIXED** through a full plan+audit -> code+audit round at safety-boundary depth (terra/xhigh, grok-4.6/xhigh), both vendors APPROVE with no defects in the landed code. The round refuted my own mechanism: the denylist/allowlist difference is a **SUBSTITUTION, not an over-count** — a source contributes its other node to the size and its BRANCH vertex to the inserts, netting zero when that terminal is live and a **deficit of one when tied to ground**; the `unique()` padding on parallel passives is what saved the rest.
