Grok also corrected two counts of mine (three switch tests, not four; the switch tests overflow BOTH containers). `compute_adjacency()` now derives sizing and population from ONE computation, with the switch diagonal counted and a post-fill assert. Landmines recorded: never fold branch-vertex indices into the neighbor cell (**the first voltage source shares ground's numeric index**), never `unique()` the unknown-current attachments, never 'clean' the allowlist (`TERMINALPAIR` is a structural zero but load-bearing for nnz). **REVIEWED, NOT VERIFIED — nothing built or run.** The audit rewrote the gate: `make check` green is WEAK (it already passed with the overflow live); the real gate is valgrind's 22 BELFEM invalid read/write contexts going to zero, NOT `ERROR SUMMARY: 0`. Pre-fix baseline binaries preserved so the comparison survives the rebuild.
- [dl20260830_dr29_coreduce_analysis.md](dl20260830_dr29_coreduce_analysis.md) — **DR-29 analyzed by a three-way blind jury, nothing touched: the cohomology module's fragility is named ( unguarded map reads safe only by an unchecked phase-order invariant ), the #10 zero-neighbor cleanup skip confirmed 3-way with a production-reachable abort cascade both auditors constructed independently — and the highest-severity suspicion REFUTED:** Claude and Grok both concluded the Smith matrices miss the phase-2 merge projection; Codex's Schur derivation ( δ∘δ=0 forces the transformed lower rows to [0; D] ) shows the live-restriction is exactly the retracted operator, re-verified independently. Do NOT mirror merges into (p−1) coboundary rows, do NOT switch `createMatrixFromCoboundaryMap` to boundary maps. Ordered fix proposal ( invariant check → guarded #10 cleanup → live-and-unit pair test → Alg. 6.1 port last; `pCocombine` is NOT a worklist template ) written up for Gregory in `tmp/dr29_cohomology_report.md`. Register live-path clause corrected ( production is PellikkaGeneralized, not CCR ).
- [dl20260830_dr110_capacity_contract.md](dl20260830_dr110_capacity_contract.md) — **DR-110 closed by Christian's ruling.** `Basis` deliberately retains no source-capacity member; callers allocate precisely the number of sources they append, and that invariant is now documented on the public API and implementation.
- [dl20260830_dr97_strike.md](dl20260830_dr97_strike.md) — **DR-97 struck by Christian's DR-42/DR-49 single-run exception.** The conservative coupled thermal-budget enforcement remains in the tree, but its planned A/B run was not executed and is retired with the row; status is explicitly REVIEWED, NOT VERIFIED.
- [dl20260830_dr129_kernel_controller_init.md](dl20260830_dr129_kernel_controller_init.md) — **DR-129 (the register's only P1) from row to code-audited fix in one day, and the xhigh plan round found a production defect the row never knew about.** The row said one consumer read the uninitialized `Kernel::mController`; the census found seven accessor reads — and Codex found the eighth, which is not a call at all: `ThermalFactory` built the thermal kernel as `make_shared<Kernel>(Kernel(params))`, and with a user-declared dtor C++17 selects the implicit **copy** ctor, which copies the indeterminate `mController` AND the supposedly-dead `mMyCommIndex` on **every coupled run**. On the live path the temporary owns nothing, so formal UB only — but the unused mesh-based ctor would have made it a use-after-free. Both vendors independently killed two plan claims: `have_thermal()` had zero consumers (the row's "wrong physics" severity story was a dead store) and the order-shuffle gate was meaningless post-fix (a defaulted pointer cannot become garbage). Christian ruled O1(c) and all-three-members scope; landed across 7 files: three `= nullptr`/`= 0` defaults, `has_controller()`, `BELFEM_ERROR` in the accessor, a both-arm `mHaveThermal` guard, in-place `make_shared`, and the DR-46 fixture's skipped `link_to_group` re-enabled with `have_thermal()`'s first-ever consumer as its contract assertion. Code audit unanimous APPROVE, zero logic defects, two comment nits applied. **Debug AND release gates BOTH RAN GREEN the same session ( `GhostElementContract [ OK ]` in the rebuilt binary, suite green; then 17/17 suites green again under `USE_DEBUG=OFF` / `-O2 -DNDEBUG` ) — the release run is the decisive one, since it is the configuration in which the pre-fix code dereferenced the indeterminate member with no assert to catch it. The coupled thermal smoke ran too — release build, 8 ranks, 483 converged coupled timesteps, zero DR-129-class failures — and was closed early on the reasoning that the R8 construction site executes ONCE at startup, so the deck's remaining hours could add no evidence for this row. All three gates green — **DR-129 STRUCK the same evening, clearing the register's only P1.** Second lesson, from a false start on that release gate: `option( USE_DEBUG ... ON )` takes the cached value unless `-D` is on the `cmake` line, so a bare `make` reconfigure silently keeps debug — confirm the mode from `flags.make` or from an ASSERT-only string in the linked binary, never from the tree's name. Lesson: enumerate writers and copies, not just named reads; and `grep "TypeName("` does not find `TypeName tVar(...)`.
- [dl20260830_mpi_wrapper_contract.md](dl20260830_mpi_wrapper_contract.md) — **L-21 stated as hard policy ( Christian ): third-party libraries are NEVER called directly, only through the dedicated wrappers — and the first enforcement round caught the wrappers themselves.** Christian's draft `allreduce`/`abort` went to a blind jury with Claude's findings sealed: both vendors REVISE, three-way convergence. The reduce call had the wrong arity for the MPI function it named — latent because a template body is only checked at instantiation and nothing instantiated it; const recv; serial branch that copied nothing; `int_t` count that narrows silently on the 64-bit suite ( the answer to Christian's own datatype question: `comm_type<T>` makes the element safe, the COUNT was the hazard ); and an abort wrapper on `gComm.world()` that re-introduced, hours later, the `error_abort` contract fixed that same day. Codex alone: serial abort returning 0 lets `assert::error()` RESUME after a failed check. Grok alone: `comm_check` on the abort path is recursion, and `MPI_Op` stays OUT of the signature — a parameter re-leaks the vendor token the wrapper exists to hide. Landed: `allreduce` ( comm_check, int count, static_assert arithmetic, serial identity copy ) and `comm_abort` ( WORLD + lifecycle guards + unconditional `std::abort()` ), declared in `cl_Communicator.hpp` because core's include path cannot reach `commtools.hpp`'s linalg pulls; four sites replaced, `error_abort` now a one-line delegate. All five TUs syntax-clean with their own build flags, solver TUs instantiating the template for real. **Gate RAN green ( Christian ): rebuild + `make check` 17/17, `allreduce` executing at np=2/4 through the Tier 2 exit fold.** Not claimed: `comm_abort` executing — a green suite cannot reach the abort path; its body is the verbatim move of long-running code.
- [dl20260830_test_hardening_tier2.md](dl20260830_test_hardening_tier2.md) — **the suite was serial by construction, and its own history says it is not what finds our bugs.** A three-AI jury over `doc/lessons_learned_evidence.md` ( 539 incidents mined from 298 devlogs, so the sweep was a re-read of an existing catalog rather than a fresh pass ): **280 code defects, 38 closed by adding a test.** Discovery was AI/static audit 356, production runs 158, hand probes 90. Coverage correlates — `src/mesh` 52,318 source lines against 915 test lines. Three gaps, both auditors converging on the first and third: the periodic/thin-shell pipeline is never driven through its production factory ( the mesh tests *document* deferring it; the one periodic test bypasses `PeriodicityFactory` **on purpose**, which is false comfort rather than a zero ); the suite has no `mpirun` anywhere while `tests/comm/test_CommMPI.cpp` — 19 KB, written three days earlier, own `main()`, 29 rank guards — sat in no `CMakeLists.txt`, a Tier 1/Tier 2 scheme named in three files and never built; and the reject/warm-restart contract, whose two Layer 1 signatures have no call sites at all. **Sixteen defects recorded, four of them mine, none reaching a build.** The sealed pre-registration rested on a DR that had been **retracted the same day it was filed** — both auditors caught it, and the retraction record names the exact mechanism this round reproduced. Three planned assertions would have failed against *correct* code or passed with their defect live ( R5 asserted a post-restore Δt equal to a cold run, but the cap it violates **is** the INC-351 fix ). **D9: the Tier 2 design failed open** — a `GTEST_SKIP` returns 0 and ctest reads the exit code, so a launcher degrading to one rank would have shown green with 29 skips: the campaign's headline failure mode reproduced inside its own fix. D15: the second revision introduced four consistency defects of its own, because *a rewrite reconciles the text it replaces, not the text that referenced it*. **The disagreement worth keeping:** on the landed `set_throw_on_error( true )` both auditors found the same real defect and prescribed **different** remedies — Codex's was the rank-asymmetry argument reverted by Christian the same day, and was refused; Grok's ( delete the call, let debug throw and release abort ) was already applied. An auditor finding a real defect does not make its fix right. R1/R1b landed, R2 partial. **Reviewed, not verified — nothing built or run**; `mpicxx -fsyntax-only` in both the MPI and no-MPI shape is not a build, and the rank-3 failure-propagation probe that must see the new Allreduce turn ctest red is still owed.
- [dl20260830_mumps_minus9_retry.md](dl20260830_mumps_minus9_retry.md) — **DR-106 fixed and DR-144 completed in one round over the MUMPS wrapper: a `-9` workspace soft-fail now walks an in-wrapper retry ladder ( `INFOG` export, ICNTL(14) 30→240, JOB 5 ) before the controller ever sees it.** The shim exported only rank-local `INFO`, so any retry keyed on it was a strand — MUMPS puts the true code on the failing rank and `-1` everywhere else; `INFOG(1:2)` is the documented rank-uniform pair and now drives the retry, the error arms, and `print_soft_fail` ( whose `-9` arm was dead code on a multi-rank job ). Two blind jury rounds; the code round ran at xhigh and caught the `free()` restore on the wrong side of `#ifdef BELFEM_MUMPS`, a millions-convention lie in the `-9` box, and a latent zero-relaxation collective hang. Retry job JOB 5 adjudicated AGAINST both auditors' conditional objections by opening the manual they could not: ICNTL(14) is read at both analysis and factorization, and JOB 2 alone would return the RHS copy as "solution" through this shim. G10/G11 ( DR-144 R12/R13 ) landed in the same functions. **Gates RAN the same afternoon and both rows are STRUCK:** Christian's rebuild + restart recovered two real `-9` events through the ladder ( step 791 converged where the old code cut the timestep; zero soft-fail boxes ) and `check-fast` ran 15/15 suites green — R8 at the top rung of the evidence ladder.
- [dl20260830_register_todo_currentness_sweep.md](dl20260830_register_todo_currentness_sweep.md) — **no row strikeable, and that was the wrong question: seven of seventeen in-scope rows are FIXED IN THE TREE while their description still states the defect in the present tense**, and eight `file:line` citations now name a different function than the row is about ( DR-97 `:3434` is a BLR warning; DR-111's whole path is dead, `ThinShellFactory` moved to `src/fem/kernel/`; DR-110 cites three `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp` callers where the file has **no** `add_source` caller at all ). Each of the seven gained a dated CURRENTNESS clause naming what survives; the diagnoses were kept, not deleted. DR-105 is narrower than it reads — its quench-front `n > 1` abort got a landed remedy on 08-29 ( the `critical temperature` key, and the shipped deck carries `90.0 K` ), so the residue is a rerun. **DR-138 is the one strike candidate and it is Christian's:** its unit gate RAN green, but the row still names a coupled reject-retry A/B, which makes it the DR-42/DR-49 exception, i.e. a ruling. `todo/` 48 → 44: `ai_wrapper_tier_selection` and `tapestack3d_jjc_noise_2125ms` solved, `run_gate_batches` obsolete ( its §6 evidence-decay rule is NOT in `lessons_learned` and is flagged for promotion ), and — **Grok's find, missed by both Claude and Codex** — `restart_timestep_consistency` obsolete, because the archived DR-112 row says in terms that it is superseded by a retraction. Grok found it by reading the *closed* register, which is where supersessions get recorded. **Carries a same-day correction: I called a true Grok finding invented.** Its DR-110 replacement line numbers ( `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:1581`, … ) are exact; I had greped `src/fem/kernel/cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp`, which **does not exist** — the file is under `src/fem/maxwell/` — and a tree-wide fallback truncated by `head -6`. Two rules: an empty grep is not evidence until the path is confirmed to exist, and never conclude 'zero occurrences' from a command containing `head`. Codex ( a quote it attributed to the weekly report is not in that file ) was the one genuine vendor error. **Phase 2 compacted eleven rows ( table 79,656 -> 68,888 chars, DR-97 alone 10,774 -> 4,719 ) and the xhigh deletion audit found EIGHT false statements in my compacted text, seven inherited from the old rows and carried forward unread** — the exact failure compaction was meant to end. The expensive one: DR-105's residue is a ~60 h serial rerun of a deck that **aborts at setup**, because its material block still says `custom { }` and the factory hard-errors on that spelling since the 08-29 rename. DR-97's severity argument cited a `run_coupled` that **does not exist**, and the real accept test lets an UNCERTIFIED thermal stall through. Three tags were wrong against the preamble's own `[RUN]` definition ( DR-105 -> MIXED, DR-111 -> CODE, DR-135 -> RUN-BLOCKED ). Two rows got LONGER because their old text was false. Both vendors praised the same judgement — **delete votes, keep constraints**.
- [dl20260830_debt_register_strike_sweep.md](dl20260830_debt_register_strike_sweep.md) — **No rows moved:** the live register contains no struck row, every prior strike is already archived, and each apparently solved live row still names a gate or residue.
- [dl20260830_dr112_and_abort_default.md](dl20260830_dr112_and_abort_default.md) — **DR-112 struck: a memdump without the BDF triple is refused rather than cold-starting the order ramp** ( gate run by hand, `make check` never reaches `load_memdump`: 4 of 4 ranks refuse, 0 re-anchor ). Grok's plan round caught the thermal check sitting AFTER the magnetic restore, which would have half-restored the integrator before aborting, and the duplicate-broadcast deadlock in the fix for it. **Also records an abort policy that was implemented, audited five times and REVERTED:** making the error reaction depend on `gComm.size()` is right about the code and wrong about the workflow — debug must throw in parallel so the per-rank `lldb` sees the failure. Four real bugs survived the revert, all on the error path itself ( `error_abort()` indexing an empty `mComms`, a failed `MPI_Init` routed through a MPI-querying reaction, `finalize()` not restoring the sentinels, `print_errorbox` printing `proc 2147483647` ). Plus INC-558: `git checkout --` has no undo, `git stash` is the safe form, and `git diff --stat` is the authority on whether a file changed.
- [dl20260830_physics_stats_footer.md](dl20260830_physics_stats_footer.md) — **the per-timestep physics summary was one postprocess behind, and the asterisk marked the wrong rows.** `JJCx/y/z` are written only by `MaxwellPostprocessor`, called from `Controller::finalize()`; the summary printed from the certified-exit branch of `iterate_coupled()` / `iterate_magnetic()`, which returns BEFORE finalize. So `save()` correctly predicted *whether this step would be postprocessed* — but a prediction about the future is not a statement about the field's contents, and between two saves the field is stale unconditionally. Both rows carried the same value; only the unmarked one lied about it. Fixed by recording the fact instead of a proxy for it ( `mPostProcessed`, set where the pass actually runs ) and printing from `print_footer()` after it. A third case appeared once the branch was written honestly: a deck with no postprocessor leaves the cell **empty** rather than printing `0.000000`, which would read as a measurement of zero rather than an absence. **A second defect fixed for free:** in the segregated path the row sat at the magnetic convergence exit, so `T_max` reported the temperature from before the thermal sub-steps advanced it. The move split a box across two emitters, so the divider glyph is handed over through `mBoxSectionOpen` rather than re-derived from deck topology ( `┼` continues a three-column section, `┬` opens a fresh one after the thermal sub-step boxes close their own ). Same proxy-instead-of-fact shape one row down: 'Time for postprocessing' was gated on `mLastSave`, which is now unread — still assigned in three places in `adjust_timestep()`, left pending a decision. Gates: syntax-only compile of the TU with the tree's own flags including `-Wall -Werror -pedantic-errors`, clean; frame widths measured at 76 display columns counting the combining asterisk ( U+20F0 ) as zero width; Christian built and ran it and confirmed the box renders. `make check` NOT run, and the segregated three-emitter path has not been seen in real output.
- [dl20260830_ai_wrapper_depth_selection.md](dl20260830_ai_wrapper_depth_selection.md) — auditor wrappers take an explicit model+effort and stamp it into the exchange header; two jury rounds, thirteen steps, R10 gate green.
- [dl20260829_expk_removal.md](dl20260829_expk_removal.md) — **`expk` removed ( DR-146's second half ), as a clean ABI break before the design freeze.** The algebra settled the open design question: `expk` and `fuzzyness` are ONE logistic parameter under `fuzzyness = 1/(1+expk)`, with defaults coinciding exactly at 4.59512 — `fuzzyness` superseded it and the conversion was never wired up. Codex refuted my softer 'deprecated 5-arg forwarder' fallback as the one UNSAFE option: `sigmoid()` was inline, so an old plugin carries slot index 7 inside itself and would read off the end of a shrunken 7-element Cell. **Grok's correction changed the user-facing outcome** — my error message told users to migrate with `fuzzyness = 1/(1+expk)`, which is the intended-equivalence map and NOT the preserve-your-run map, so it would have CHANGED the waveform of every deck that carried the inert key. Now says DELETE to preserve. Guard sits before the type dispatch in all three factories, or the defect just relocates to ramp/sine/constant. Three separate YAML structural errors were made and all three PARSED, one of them silently deleting `file`/`label`/ `units` from the accepted contract — replaced parse-checking with structural assertions. **`make check` has NOT covered this**: the passing suite predates it ( binary lacks the three new tests, library still exports the 5-arg symbol ). Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_dr146_materials_validator.md](dl20260829_dr146_materials_validator.md) — **DR-146 materials half FIXED: a shape-aware allow-list that refuses any key or subsection the resolved material shape never reads.** Christian ruled fatal + both decks repaired in the same change. Four audit corrections changed the outcome: Grok's `type()==PureMetal` RRR gate replacing my name list (a name list rots when a material is added), the HTS split by `file` presence (a union list would have re-silenced case S9 itself), the usermat `defect` SUBSECTION gated on `have(jc)` and not just its keys, and refusal of labelled subsections (`defect : mine { }` was never consumed). Duplicate labels now fatal, closing the last-wins ambiguity and the leaked `Material*` together. **The executable control caught two defects review missed:** the first probe linked the prebuilt libbelfem.a so every must-fail case passed with the validator absent from the binary; and once live, an allow-list entry spelled `RRR` rejected EVERY copper deck, because `key()` returns the lowercased name while `key_exists()` lowercases its argument for you. Ten of eleven decks pass (sidecoating fails on a missing plugin .so, pre-existing). `expk` half deliberately NOT fixed — a design question across three factories. `make check` owed. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_r6_restart_and_phase_sign.md](dl20260829_r6_restart_and_phase_sign.md) — **DR-124's R6 coupled gate CONDUCTED and PASSED ( restore is bitwise exact ), the defect it exposed folded into DR-112, DR-148 phase-sign convention landed.** Determinism control first: the truncated leg and the baseline are bitwise identical over 27 rows, so the seam is interpretable. With the first post-restart step at the dumped Δt the resumed run is bitwise identical to the uninterrupted baseline over 20 rows — v2 restore VERIFIED at coupled level. With the deck's own `initial timestep` it is not: the step advances the clock 1e-5 s but returns the currents the baseline reaches 2e-4 s later ( 144 A where neighbors rise 4.6 A ), voltages meanwhile tracking the new time — the DR-92 cap makes the first step Δt-inconsistent, exactly the interaction Grok predicted on the DR-124 row. recorded WITHOUT localizing the module ( likely the restored BDF history, not the circuit ); the 'physics permanently ahead of clock' hypothesis was refuted by measurement ( shifting makes the fit worse, 452 vs 137 A ). DR-148: Christian ruled SPICE ( positive phase advances ), overruling my delay+negate recommendation; three sign flips, and `f_phase(t)==f_zerophase(t+T/4)` now holds at exactly 0 for all four waveforms where it held for sine alone. Both contract artifacts + circuit guide updated. Grok caught an ID collision ( a peer had taken DR-146/147 ) and refuted my test table's 'today' column. Uncommitted.
### [dl20260829_dr144_dr140_dr90_cluster.md](dl20260829_dr144_dr140_dr90_cluster.md) — the MUMPS-lifecycle and conditioning cluster

Scoped DR-144 / DR-140 / DR-90 as one cluster and re-verified each against the working tree rather
than the register text. **DR-90's gate turns out to be inapplicable, not merely blocked** — the
magnetic field now runs `library : mumps`, so the `relative tolerance` the A/B was meant to vary
never reaches the solver; and the conditioning half was already answered by surviving logs, which
report a magnetic conditioning number every timestep from two independent estimators. The stall
class the row describes does not reproduce; both rejections in that run are thermal-limited. First
register row recovered from evidence decay rather than lost to it — then **struck** by Christian's
ruling, because a recovered measurement showed the row could not be settled on this deck by any
means. **DR-140** needs no code, only one `make check`. **DR-144** was smaller than its row (gaps 3
and 4 collapse into one line: `MUMPS::mInitialized` is written three times and read exactly once,
and is a different member from `Wrapper::mIsInitialized`) but grew from seven gaps to **nine** under
audit, and three of the fix shapes the register itself proposed were wrong — one would have enabled
a cross-wrapper destroy. All nine **implemented** the same day; `make check` owed.

- [dl20260829_dr118_demo_run.md](dl20260829_dr118_demo_run.md) — **DR-118's owed demo run conducted; DR-118/121/123 struck.** The shipped `bin/electricalCircuit` was dated 08-28 and predated both this fix and the DR-138/139 library fixes — `make check` refreshes the library and tests, NOT the solver executables — so running it would have exercised the old loop and passed; the run used a sandbox binary linked against the freshly built libbelfem.a, with the forced-rejection probe confined to a scratchpad copy of the source. A plain run proved nothing (412 steps, zero rejections, so none of the fixed path ran); three forced cases did: first-step rejection (the old hard `ShiftRegister::revert` abort) completes, a mid-run rejection re-solves the SAME interval at dt/2 with no time advance and no row for the failed attempt, and unbounded rejection hits the new dt floor after 28 halvings instead of spinning. Trajectory tracks the never-rejected baseline to 9.2e-4 relative. A first-cut 4.7e-2 deviation was a clamping artifact of the comparison (314 V/s x 150 us grid offset), not a defect. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_usermat_rename_and_tcrit_key.md](dl20260829_usermat_rename_and_tcrit_key.md) — **New `critical temperature` input key (fatal on a builtin), `custom` → `usermat` rename, and the tape_quench custom-material deck revived from four blockers.** The deck's jc/n fits were loaded and unreachable — registered through the one-dependency overload, whose `set_custom()` writes NaN into the constant slot, so `jc_eval` took its null branch; rewired to the three-dependency path. `T_crit` was never set, which aborts in debug and is SILENT in release. 90.0 K is forced by the fits themselves: the jc quartic roots at 90.0371 K and the n quintic crosses 1 at 91.8784 K, so the YBCO builtin's 92.5 K sits past both — DR-105's quench-front abort. The new key is **fatal on a builtin** (Christian's ruling, stronger than either auditor proposed): YBCO bakes `T_crit` into its Callaway lambda spline at construction, so an override would move the resistivity gate and leave κ stale. Codex's `inf K` P1 confirmed live by execution. Both audits corrected our own plan: `rho_powerlaw` reads no `T_crit` at all — only `piecewise` and `riva` do. 11 of 12 tests pass; the two that initially disagreed were a **stale `.so` whose mtime was newer than its source**. Rename decided by measurement — no shipped deck uses the shape, so it is free now and breaking forever after the release; both auditors had said keep `custom`, both rejected `resistivity`. Plugin restructure collapsed eleven 68-line table loaders into one class (1027 → 403 lines) with the interpolation change proven equivalent over 1.2M probes. DR-146 (thirteen silent-ignore cases, one live on this deck) and DR-147 (plugin `.so` paths resolve three different ways) filed rather than folded in. Uncommitted.

- [dl20260829_mumps_pool_recycling.md](dl20260829_mumps_pool_recycling.md) — **DR-143 fixed: the tapestack3d step-8 abort was mumpstools solver-pool exhaustion — the ID allocator was monotonic and never rescanned freed slots, so the production magnetic MUMPS (slot 1) plus the per-timestep shift-invert conditioning churn (thermal on PETSc) burned the 8-slot pool in exactly seven steps.** Three-piece fix, one both-vendor round: first-free scan as allocator authority + occupancy recount + whole-body `allocated()` guard + `nullify` of freed user pointers; the shift-invert instance now persists across timesteps (also buys JOB 5 analysis reuse); the create site marks the wrapper initialized only after success and soft-fails exhaustion, with both solve overloads bailing flagged on `mSolverID <= 0` — Grok refuted the first Fix-C sketch (dual init flags; release builds would have indexed `gSolvers(0)`). Regression test `SolverLifecycle.MUMPSPoolRecyclesFreedSlots` closes Grok's evidence gap (the consumer fix removes the production trigger). Three peer sessions cross-checked the diagnosis (exact 1+7=8 fit; 660294f6 had no shift-invert path, explaining the serial run). DR-144 filed for the pre-existing lifecycle-accounting gaps, and grown from three to seven once Grok's code leg landed (pool exhaustion in create is non-collective, so a mixed-occupancy pool deadlocks in Fortran ahead of every C++ guard; `report_unavailable` reports a solver failure as a Krylov-budget failure). Reviewed not verified, now with both vendors on both rounds; the regression test is a ceiling gate at the magic pool size 8, not an ID-recycle assertion, and does not cover Fix C. `make check` + deck rerun owed. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_dr117_dr141_pair.md](dl20260829_dr117_dr141_pair.md) — **DR-117 + DR-141 fixed as a pair; the DR-117 row was wrong about its own severity AND about its own fix.** The flagged `set_time_offset` call followed an already-correct `set_phase`, and because that setter back-computes phase from offset it also clobbered `PHASE` to `aPhase*pi^2` — so `function_sine`, which the row called immune, was wrong by the same factor (probe: `f(0)=0.20339575` vs `1.0`); shipped decks escaped only because all four set `phase : 0`. Both auditors independently rejected the row's proposed rewrite: at `aPeriod == 0` it evaluates `0 * INFINITY` in the phase write-back, and the probe confirms the pre-fix tree already returns `phase = -nan` there. Landed fix is to DELETE the redundant line; the NaN goes with it. Five-test `tests/circuit/test_SourceFunction.cpp`, with the pre-registered round-trip assertion dropped on audit advice (the pi^2 corruption is self-consistent across both slots, so it was green on the buggy tree). DR-141: all four `= delete` on `Solver`/`ElectricalCircuit` per the DR-142 idiom (NOT `SpMatrix`, which keeps assignment on purpose) plus the `Circuit` base on a split verdict, and `mSolver` brace-initialized so it no longer leans on C++17 elision; positive control ran — three copy/move probes rejected, ten consumer TUs clean. DR-145 filed as by-catch and measured: `std::fmod` sign makes `square(0)=+1` where the wrap demands `-1`, `sawtooth(0)=-0.25` vs `+0.75`, and a triangle at offset `0.95 T` returns `+1.8` on an amplitude of 1. **Both rows STRUCK and archived same day — `make check` passed (Christian); the five `SourceFunction` cases were confirmed present in the binary via `--gtest_list_tests` and run 5/5 green rather than assumed from the suite result, and DR-141's gate WAS the clean build.** VERIFIED, not reviewed. DR-145 stays open. Stale 'latent for sine' claims corrected in the ngspice plan. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_dr86_refuted.md](dl20260829_dr86_refuted.md) — **DR-86 REFUTED by source trace and struck: the aura-φ lag it described has no write path.** Christian challenged the premise ("we do distribute the fields at the end of each iteration") and the tree agrees — `DofManager::solve` and `solve_from_residual` each end with `distribute( all_fields )`, so the synch is per ITERATE, not per step; and every solver field write sits behind `if ( mKernel->is_master() )`, with workers receiving the WHOLE field vector (auras included, via `comm_table(p)->nodes()`), so the owned/aura split the hypothesis needed does not exist. Both of the row's anchors were misread: `:2236` is the rank-0-only multi-column-RHS `Direct` branch, and `Controller:4100` is deck parsing — the controller's `distribute_fields` calls are all memdump-restore. Static trace, reviewed not verified, but structural (a rank guard plus a whole-vector replace) rather than statistical. Probe retired; the master-centric-solve caveat is kept for a future distributed solver. INC-331's residue clause corrected in the same pass.
- [dl20260829_debt_register_cleanup.md](dl20260829_debt_register_cleanup.md) — Debt-register cleanup reconciled after the day's parallel closure rounds: all struck rows are archived, with no duplicate or cross-file IDs. The remaining fixed-but-unstruck rows still carry explicit run, code, or design residue. Mechanical checks leave 27 live rows (2 `[F]`, 18 `[P]`, 7 `[W]`) and no struck rows in the live table.
- [dl20260829_dr139_mmna_leak.md](dl20260829_dr139_mmna_leak.md) — **DR-139 fixed: `~ElectricalCircuit` leaked `mMNA` — guarded destructor delete beside the `mJ` arm, destructor-only exactly as the row demanded.** DR-124 precondition re-checked (sole allocation is the `mMNA == nullptr` lazy-init; no mid-run free anywhere): the one-shot guard cannot re-enter. Code audit both vendors, convergent approve; Grok tightening adopted (leak only per lifetime that stamped). By-catch filed as DR-141: `ElectricalCircuit`/`Solver` implicitly copyable while owning raw pointers — latent double-free, unreachable today. Reviewed not verified; valgrind gate owed. The hunk sits inside the exited circuit-cluster session's uncommitted set — stage explicit paths.
- [dl20260829_circuit_restart_v2.md](dl20260829_circuit_restart_v2.md) — **/circuit memdump format v2: per-component state lands, removing the accepted v1 limitation (DR-124 residue).** Component `save_state`/`load_state` virtuals dump/restore L/C `ShiftRegister` histories (capacity-guarded both directions via new header-only `fn_circuit_state_io.hpp`, container untouched), component currents, terminal-pair `mIn`/`mVn` with `set_current(mIn)` on load (Grok's blocker: the coupled Jacobian reads `get_current()`), source values, and the switch latch (supersedes the v1 self-correcting re-fire, pending Christian's ack). Creation-index prefixes with count+type validation; pre-v2 dumps refuse loudly. Loaders rebuild companions via the DR-138 round's `update_companions()` so stamp-without-shift stays coherent. Four new tests incl. 1e-12 continuation equivalence vs an uninterrupted twin (full AND partial registers) and an old-format refusal probe. Guide §9 publishes the format (Codex-swept). Gate RAN same day (Christian, full suite 15/15): all green incl. the 1e-12 continuation equivalence and the DR-138 RLC twin — v2 restore and reject-retry semantics VERIFIED at unit level; coupled R6 stays open (controller restart-entry Δt cap is outside the circuit). Uncommitted.
- [dl20260829_circuit_cluster.md](dl20260829_circuit_cluster.md) — **Circuit cluster DR-118/DR-121/DR-123/DR-124 all fixed in one both-vendor round (plan+audit → code+audit, convergent approve-with-amendments).** DR-121: `++tCount` SWITCH case in the MNA walk, ngspice v1 layout refusal removed, refusal test flipped to acceptance. DR-123: `Switch::shift_back()` snapshot/restore + the SWITCH case in the circuit walk (live consumer: coupled `reset_timestep`). DR-124: one-shot stamp block preserves `load_state`-restored `mX`/`mPrevX` (both-vector length guard); coupled R6 gate still owed. DR-118: demo loop rebuilt to controller-parity shift-then-stamp with `continue` on reject, dt-floor abort, corrected output semantics; by-catch double free (`delete tFunction` after the owning circuit) removed. Five new tests in `tests/circuit/test_ElectricalCircuit.cpp` incl. the DR-124 wipe discriminators (HDF5) and the forced-rejection RLC twin. DR-138 filed (suspected inductor `shift_back` current loss — the pre-registered reading of a red RLC twin test) and DR-139 (`mMNA` destructor leak). Gate run confirmed the pre-registration: four tests green (DR-121/123/124 verified at unit level), RLC twin red at 7.4e-3 A = DR-138. **DR-138 then fixed the same day in round 3**: vendor split (Codex snapshot-approve vs Grok refute on the `set_timestep` BDF1 clobber of `mRL`) resolved by the tree in Grok's favor; landed as companion recompute from the reverted registers via a shared `update_companions()` in Inductor AND Capacitor, empty-register guard for first-step rejection, `miLh`/`miCh` zero-inits. Surviving gates: RLC twin rerun (pre-registered green < 1e-3), one demo run, coupled R6.
- [dl20260829_dr128_low_field_bridge.md](dl20260829_dr128_low_field_bridge.md) — **DR-128 fixed, run-verified and STRUCK: the jc tables' 10 mT clamp floor replaced by a reader-side quadratic bridge anchored at the MEASURED angle-free self-field level** (read from the tables' embedded B = 0 rows, unit-calibrated against their own metadata), Kim-consistent finite slope at zero field, monotone limiter, C1 at the join. Two plan rounds + code round reconciled; executed probes green; tapestack3d crossed 10 mT at t = 2.725 s at ×1.0465/frame with zero cuts. Also fixed: h5py vlen-UTF-8 string datasets were unreadable by `hdf5_tools.hpp`. **Closes the jjc-noise cluster.**
- [dl20260829_dr127_certified_exit.md](dl20260829_dr127_certified_exit.md) — **DR-127 certified exit landed (plan v4 Fork A, three plan-audit rounds, code audit in the same slug): the nonlinear loops now exit only on a state whose residual was measured from a fresh assembly.** Two-phase `compute_residual()`/`solve_from_residual()` seam in SolverData; `iterate_coupled` restructured head/body/tail with the first body trial consuming the head assembly (a solving trip costs what it does today); thermal head at the Gauss-Seidel position doubles as co-certificate; new `solve_coupled()/solve_magnetic()/solve_thermal()` wrappers own the loop, `iterate_*`/`run_*` privatized, all 7 driver loops collapsed. `min iterations` now counts body solves (0 = zero-solve predictor accept allowed); input contract updated in both artifacts. **Run-verified and DR-127 STRUCK the same day:** build + suite green, the jjc-noise deck reproduced as smooth (the 2.125 s frame grows ×1.047 like every neighbor, where the defect was ×3 in one step), segregated and warm-restart paths clean, and the one branch no well-behaved deck reaches — the `mResetThermal` return — forced with a temporary probe, which retried at a halved sub-step without hanging; probe removed same session.
- [dl20260828_dr97_119_120_controller_guards.md](dl20260828_dr97_119_120_controller_guards.md) — **DR-97 latch, DR-119 coupled clamp and DR-120 parse validation landed in one two-round jury (plan+audit → code+audit, Codex + Grok, code round unanimous zero-defect).** DR-97: `mMagneticHitTarget`, a per-attempt latch, replaces the instantaneous magnetic-at-target clause in `tThermalBudgetSpent` — closes the recorded step-267 gap where continuing thermal updates perturbing a converged magnetic field disarmed the budget; `run_coupled` still refuses a degraded accept. DR-119: in fully-coupled mode the effective thermal watchdog window is clamped to `magnetic max − thermal min − 1` (floor 1, 0 stays disable, segregated untouched), at the deck snapshot so floor escalation and finalize stay coherent; both vendors demoted it to a conservative policy sized to an early thermal best (the last watchdog-capable iterate is the magnetic ceiling itself, `tMagneticDoomed` skips thermal there). DR-120 widened on Christian's call: max/min iterations validated in both blocks with `max ≥ min` cross-checks, watchdog windows ≥ 0, the `stall window` clamp-after-wrap sibling (negative promoted to uint BEFORE the ≥ 2 clamp), and `coupling factor` > 0 — the last after a vendor disagreement resolved against the tree in Codex's favor (it unconditionally divides `initial timestep`; segregated 0 was a div-by-zero). Input contract both artifacts + `nonlinear_controller_theory.md` aligned and swept. DR-137 filed (magnetic watchdog vs its own ceiling, Grok's flag). Syntax gate green, then build + full test suite green (Christian's run, 2026-08-29); the three behaviour-specific run gates survive in the rows — the DR-119 gate must show ε2 > 10× thermal tol and non-growing ω2 at the trip. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260828_dr130_dr132_rulings.md](dl20260828_dr130_dr132_rulings.md) — **DR-130 and DR-132 ruled by Christian and struck the same session.** DR-130: the symmetric `h_ghost` operator is the formulation of record — the "accidentally symmetrized" horn was refuted from git (commit `8fd48922`, 2026-04-07, ships `dl20260406_symmetric_nitsche_audit.md` saying the change was "intended to make the ghost/Nitsche matrix symmetric"); the scheme is Ern-Stephansen-Zunino 2009 SWIP. Doc §2 rewritten (nonsymmetric claim, RWG/Baumann-Oden cites, and the "coercive for any eta>0" argument all corrected), and the symmetry pin landed in `GhostElementContract` through a unanimous two-round Codex+Grok jury: upper-triangle `max|K(i,j)−K(j,i)| < 1e-12·maxK`, relative gate by design (the measured exact 0.0 is a scale accident, not an IEEE guarantee). Reviewed not verified — `check-fast` rerun with the pin is the surviving gate. DR-132: the plugin `dlopen` boundary stays a documentation-only contract ("rebuild your plugin"); no version handshake for 1.0; any future enforcement is a new row. Follow-up archive sweep the same session: all 19 struck rows moved to `debt_register_closed.md`; the register now holds exactly the 30 live rows its preamble counts (1 `[F]` + 22 `[P]` + 7 `[W]`).
- [dl20260828_dr135_sideset_pin.md](dl20260828_dr135_sideset_pin.md) — **DR-135 fixed through a full three-round jury ending unanimous: sideset Dirichlet pins survive periodic condensation.** New shared `fem::pin_dirichlet_dof` ( single-source unit-weight shape → pin the SOURCE at value/weight; loud errors on non-unit weights, broken sources, and post-freeze first flips via new `DofManagerBase::is_initialized()` ) called by BOTH `SideSet::impose_dirichlet` and the Maxwell factory pre-fix under the same non-duplicate gate, so the factory pre-pins exactly the per-timestep reroute set. Codex round 1 caught the post-freeze Maxwell path and the provenance overclaim; Grok round 2 refused the factory fork ( pin-set mismatch on duplicates would have turned the guard into a production abort; thin-shell/cut duplicate pairs carry inhomogeneous relations ); round 3 approved the reconciliation 2/2. Tolerances unified to `BELFEM_EPSILON` on Christian's direction, bearing constant included. **Corrections kept:** Claude's DR-136 "deck-reachable" claim retracted ( creation switch refuses the type since 2026-08-11 ); DR-136 filed as the checklist for the planned background-dirichlet deck, with Grok's veto on reusing the helper there ( spatial value ≠ source copy ). Reviewed not verified — gate is a periodic+symmetry probe deck, not yet authored. Vendor friction root-caused: Grok podman sandbox + two phantom bash errors from `ask_grok.sh` being edited mid-execution by a parallel session; fixed by running a scratchpad snapshot. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260828_dr96_dump_hook_fix.md](dl20260828_dr96_dump_hook_fix.md) — **DR-96 fixed in source, runtime gate owed: the `BELFEM_DUMP_SYSTEM_STEP` hook no longer misleads its operator by one step.** All three findings landed in one pass under the probe exemption (Christian's ruling; env-gated diagnostic, zero production impact). Direction call: the filter's post-increment numbering was the *correct* dialect — it matches the step headers the LIVE arm targets from — so the WARM RESTART banner was moved to it, now printing "next step %u" (`mRunningTimeStep + 1`, the step the first assembly carries) instead of the pre-increment count; teaching the filter banner-numbering would have broken live-log targeting the other way. The bare `atoi` replaced by digits-only `strspn` validation + `strtoul` behind one `BELFEM_ERROR` (empty, non-numeric, and negative probe values now abort loudly; `strtoul` alone silently wraps a leading `-`, hence the explicit check). The two textual copies folded into `Controller::dump_system_if_requested()` with ONE shared 4-dump budget across both iterate paths. Probe-removal did not apply: DR-92's O2/O6 are still open and this hook is their named decisive instrument. **Gate RUN same session in the `cmake-build-claude` sandbox and PASSED both halves — row STRUCK**: rebuild + `check-fast` 10/10, then a scratchpad copy of `examples/circuit` warm-restarted from its shipped memdump (dumped step 7): banner "next step 8" matched the first step header "BDF1 8", `STEP=8` produced exactly step 8's two `sysdump_magnetic_*` assemblies with steps 9–10 adding nothing, `STEP=abc` aborted loudly. The gate verified the MERGED helper — a parallel session had extended it with per-field member budgets, filename tags, and thermal-site instrumentation before the gate ran. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260828_dr134_thinshell_doc.md](dl20260828_dr134_thinshell_doc.md) — **DR-134 closed: §20 of the DofManager guide rewritten against the live thin-shell code, and the prose stopped preserving DR-85's bug.** The row was filed as stale naming (a walkthrough of the deleted `h_ts_metal`), but the listing also showed the **unprojected** master/slave average `bn = -0.5*mu0*(Bm*phi_m + Bs*phi_s)` — the exact defect DR-85 found in the postprocessor and Christian fixed on 2026-08-16. The fix landed in the code; the documentation kept the bug, and a reader routed to §20 by the module README had no way to tell. Rewritten against the two-layer split the guide had collapsed: kernel selection by **solver algorithm only** (`Conductor` and `ThinShell` share one `case` in `IWG_Maxwell::link_to_group`; `mt_maxwell_h.hpp` exports six kernels, none of them per-material), and the thin-shell specifics bound at construction in the `calculator::MaxwellData` ctor (`tIsThinShell`). New §20.4 quotes the live `compute_hn` **including** `hn = dot( hn, n ) * n`, with the `k == 0` cache (exact for flat linear shells, not an approximation), the `element_is_linear()` assert, and the fact that the normal's **sign** is a modeling input flipped by a minus prefix on the `thinshell` sidesets key. §20.5 rewritten around `bn_angle`, where the old text's physics was **backwards** — it claimed B perpendicular to the tape gives maximum Jc; for REBCO that is the minimum — plus the unfolded `[0, pi]` rule and why `mFundRhodBeta` stays `return_zero` on HTS blocks (the metal `bj_angle` tangent describes a different angle). §20.6's six-deleted-function table replaced by the deck's `resistivity type` enum mapped onto the `compute_rho_*_ts[_defect]` bindings; §20.7 onto `compute_T_fem` and its clamp contract. Two sibling sites carrying the same defect fixed in the same pass: `maxwell_usage_guide.md` §8 (same deleted kernel, same unprojected average, and it routes the reader here) and one naming line in `contact_impedance_theory.md`. `grep -rn 'h_ts_metal\|h_ts_hts' src/ doc/` now returns nothing. **Follow-on the same session, on Christian's call: the dead `compute_h_ts_node` removed** through the full standing round (plan+audit, code+audit, Codex and Grok; plan APPROVE WITH CHANGES from both, code APPROVE from both). Never bound in any commit since it entered in `82a1efc1`, and `nm` shows it emitted no symbol, so no codegen or ABI change. Grok supplied the argument that settles leftover-vs-placeholder: the one open plan that might want a nodal thin shell (`todo/deferred/thinshell_hphi_formulation.md` §3.1) uses `h = -grad phi`, i.e. `compute_h_bulk_node`, whereas the deleted body added `hn` on top of a gradient a φ element already carries in full, so it would **double-count** — the wrong formula for its only plausible future use. Both auditors independently demanded deletion of the §20.3 sentence written hours earlier calling it 'declared and defined', which would have re-created the very stale-name class DR-134 had just closed. Grok also caught a near-miss: `cl_Maxwell_FieldList.cpp` has TWO ThinShell tables, and the `FieldList::ThinShell` **sideset** member does carry `phi` while `collect_block_dofs` routes ThinShell **blocks** through `Conductor` — comment and doc now name the routing function and warn about it. Gates: `-fsyntax-only -Wall` on four TUs, baseline green before the change; reviewed not verified, `make check-fast` owed. **Tree hazards recorded:** `ask_grok.sh` was edited mid-run (22:24:39), leaving it transiently unparseable and killing the audit — looked exactly like the known read-only breach and was not; and a parallel session is implementing DR-135 in this same checkout, so **nothing was committed** from here. **Earlier by-catch:** `compute_h_ts_node` is declared and defined but never bound — documented as unreachable rather than presented as live; and the DR-134 row's own evidence was overstated (it claimed the `mt_maxwell_h.*` **files** are gone; they are live, it is the `h_ts_*` **function family** that is gone), corrected in the struck row. Doc-only, so it landed without an audit round under the 2026-08-28 ruling; the required Codex language sweep ran and found no factual errors. `[P]` count 25 → 24. No source touched. Uncommitted.

- [dl20260828_current_sign_2d.md](dl20260828_current_sign_2d.md) — **Every 2-D model ever run imposed its transport current with the sign reversed; 3-D was always correct.** Christian saw `J/Jc ~ -1.2` on a gantry deck where all 464 tapes carried a *positive* current, and suspected the postprocessor. Measurement cleared it: integrating the written `Jz` gives exactly **-340.000 A on every one of 464 tapes**, and CCW Ampere loops on the written `H` agree to four digits (-19720 A per 58-tape coil) — two independent paths (`C(k)*dofs` on the layer element vs `-grad(phi)` in air), so the *field itself* carried -340 A. The discriminator that chose the repair site — does 2-D **bulk** invert too? — was answered with **no new run**, from an existing cos-theta result: 68 bulk coil blocks, Ampere ratio **-0.988**, uniform, so a thin-shell-only fix was excluded. Root cause: `Homology::reorient_generators` multiplied 2-D generators by +1 and 3-D by -1, carrying the comments *"there must be another sign error elsewhere in the code in 3-D"* and *"somehow the sign is good in 2-D"*. With `<c,g> = +1`, `phi_dup = phi_orig + I` and `h = -grad(phi)`, the generator must run **clockwise** for `+I` to mean `+z`; 2-D left it counter-clockwise. The dimension split was never a property of the machinery below it (the `mIs2D` flip in `check_edge` compensated it) — what differs is that `suggest_Homology` builds 3-D from the input-minus-output terminal boundaries and 2-D from the input loop alone, with opposite sense. **Fix: one line**, both branches now `-1`. **Gates (fresh cut generation — a cached `.bfm` skips the cut stage and cannot see this fix): cos-theta -0.988 -> +1.00; gantry mixed polarity 464/464 tapes at their own declared sign, which also clears the positional abstract-dof ordering across 464 separate conditions that the old all-positive deck could not test; corc 3-D no-op -0.6851 -> -0.6852 at operating points three decades apart; `make check-fast` 10/10.** Input contract written in the same session — the convention had never been documented anywhere. Grok **refuted Claude's own mechanism** for the voltage behaviour (it is a port *redefinition*, not `e_i -> -e_i` on an assembled system; `save_IV`'s columns do **not** flip, they keep their numbers and change meaning) while confirming the conclusion; the durable record was corrected. By-catch: a false comment at `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:501` states the current/voltage ordering backwards. Owed: **G-V1**, a 2-D voltage-driven run; plus the debt that `orient_terminal_curves_2D` is geometrically vacuous (`tR` reduces identically to `-(facet tangent)`, never consulting which side the loop encloses). Uncommitted.
- [dl20260828_make_doc_repair.md](dl20260828_make_doc_repair.md) — **`make doc` repaired: four breaks, one root cause — every Doxygen input in the tree was authored against 1.18.0 while the only installed Doxygen is 1.9.1.** B1: `doxygen -w html` was called with no config, so Doxygen read `./Doxyfile` anyway and aborted validating the `HTML_HEADER` that same command creates — a chicken-and-egg fatal on any fresh tree. B2 (the reported abort): the logo-patch needle carried 1.18's `$logosize`. B3: 46 tags ignored, silently dropping the site to the MathJax **2** CDN. B4: the 1.18 layout dump produced the run's only `error:`. The docs themselves were never broken — 3194 pages built fine once B1/B2 were bypassed. Fix: the `doc` target now derives every Doxygen input from the *installed* Doxygen — pristine Doxyfile copied then `doxygen -u`'d (never in place, because `make doc` does not reconfigure), layout regenerated with `doxygen -l` and BELFEM's delta re-applied *read from* `doc/DoxygenLayout.xml`, header generated from a config copy with `HTML_HEADER` blanked, logo needle made idempotent by construction. **Result: fails → exit 0; console tag warnings 46 → 0; log 200/1 → 90/0, and all 90 remaining are in one file (`lessons_learned_evidence.md`, a 1.9.1 table-parser limit established three ways) — zero warnings anywhere else in the tree.** Content by-catch: the `$$…$$` equations in `thermal_expansion_from_heat_capacity.md` had been rendering as **literal LaTeX source** on the site (Doxygen has no `$$` math) — converted to `\f[…\f]`. Two audit rounds, Codex + Grok, corrected three of Claude's own errors: fabricated `Bezier::dNydxN` identifiers (an artifact of Claude's own `sed 's/[0-9]+/N/g'`; real names `d3ydx3`/`d3xdy3`), a false `configure_file` premise (it rewrites on *every* configure — proven by scratch project), and a misleading baseline comment that buried real defects (fixed instead of baselined, hence 90 not 100). Also caught: `LAYOUT_FILE` left pointing at the source tree (the generated layout would have been ignored), `;`-in-tab-title list splitting, a too-loose idempotence guard accepting stale URLs, and two silent-nav-loss paths now `FATAL_ERROR`. **Verified by execution** for the pipeline (run from both header-absent and header-present states, site inspected); **not verified on 1.18** — no such binary here. Open for Christian: **O5** the warning gate only `message(WARNING)`s so it cannot fail a build, **O6** `make doc` writes tracked files (`update_doc_index.py` without `--check`). Plan: `todo/make_doc_repair.md`.

- [dl20260828_dr102_face_key_2d_guard.md](dl20260828_dr102_face_key_2d_guard.md) — **DR-102 closed, and a false closure record corrected.** The corner swap in `face_key_3d` was real and was the whole working diff of the file — but both the register's status cell and the 2026-08-24 exchange recorded the landing as "corner swap **+** `set_size(3|4)` in `face_key_2d`", and that second edit had never been applied. No runtime consequence today ( every live caller of `create_faces` passes an empty sideset list, so `face_key_2d` is unreachable ), but the swap had **inverted the sign** of the latent hazard: the shared scratch used to be left over-sized at 6, so the QUAD branch's `aWork(3)` was in-bounds and merely midside-contaminated; the corner getter shrinks it to exactly 3, making that same write a real OOB the moment a sideset list is passed — precisely the transition Codex had rated a land blocker and Grok latent. Guard landed ( `set_size` in both branches of `face_key_2d` ), `-fsyntax-only` clean under the module's own `-Wall -Werror -std=gnu++17`. The one failure has a shape: DR-102 was the only landing split across two edits decided in the same breath, one of them a hazard guard the auditors disagreed about — nothing forces the second half of a divergence resolution to be checked, and that is where the write-up outran the disk. - [dl20260828_arpack_spectral_fold.md](dl20260828_arpack_spectral_fold.md) — **The conditioning diagnostic's small end replaced by a spectral fold, and the ARPACK knobs sized automatically instead of hardwired.** `tapestack3d` was reporting `n/a` for the thermal kappa on every step and spending 4747 ms of a 16 s step to do it. Not a tuning problem: `'SM'` in regular mode asks IRAM for the interior of the spectrum. Fold about `sigma = max|lambda|*(1+1e-2)` and both ends become exterior `'LM'` requests; `sigma` cancels exactly, so only the folded run pays for the cancellation with a tight tolerance. Codex audited three times and was right each time it objected — folding about the *signed* `lambda_max` silently returns `lambda_max` when the spectrum straddles zero (`spec = {-10,1}`, reproduced numerically), an underestimated `rho` does the same (hence the margin), the direct-`'SM'` fallback answers a different question and was dropped, and `maxit` is a restart cap not a matvec ceiling (measured 10.1 and 12.2 products per restart at `ncv-nev = 19`). Found while editing and missed by every audit: **an OpenMP data race in the serial matvec** — `a`/`b` declared at subroutine scope and absent from the `private` clause, so every thread wrote the same row bounds; `USE_OPENMP` is ON by default, and the 2-thread timings in the deferred SLEPc plan are suspect because of it. **Reviewed, not verified — syntax checks only, nothing built or run.** Grok unavailable for every round (sandbox cannot resolve `/run/podman/podman.sock`); standalone briefing left for manual submission. Plan: `todo/arpack_small_end_configuration.md`.

- [dl20260827_dr104_side_coating_jury.md](dl20260827_dr104_side_coating_jury.md) — **Blind three-AI jury on DR-104: Christian's exclusion of the side-coating walls from the thermal problem — sound for transport and mass, incomplete for the Joule source (3/3).** Electrical necessity confirmed (φ-only buffer severs the halves; the wall is the only inter-half path). Thermal transport: the exclusion holds, but the load-bearing fact is the MgO *face* path beating the copper rim by ≥3e2–1e3×, not the quoted longitudinal cross-section ratio (~100×, +2.4% tape Cu); wall mass ~1%. The uncovered piece is the wall Ohmic power (in the magnetic stiffness, consumed by no thermal kernel — energy not conserved): ≪1% globally for the live pure-Cu wall, potentially local-first-order under the doc's 1e-6..1e-4 Ω·m edge-interface band. Jury corrections adopted: the brief's Ag/YBCO-R_ct-in-r′ conflation refuted (neither contact term is wired; live wall is Cu ρ(T,B,β)); seam-interpolated T already feeds r′(T). Recommended disposition (Christian's call): keep DR-104 open at P2 narrowed to source deposition onto seam/tape nodes; never thermally mesh the walls. Same-day follow-up settled the disposition: the adiabatic-tape comparison (no cooling BC exists; neglected bath cooling ~100× the wall share, conservative in sign) led to **Christian's ruling — no practical benefit in thermalizing the coatings.** Doc **renamed to `side_coating_wall_element.md`** with the full ruling + two conjunctive revisit triggers inline as §6.1 (stale WIP-stop by-catch fixed in the pass); DR-104 re-scoped [CODE]→[RULING], P2→P3, then **struck and archived the same day on Christian's word** — nothing is owed while the triggers do not hold. Scaling-only — reviewed, not verified. Exchange: `review_dr104_side_coating_thermal.md`.

- [dl20260827_edge_mesh_filter_jury.md](dl20260827_edge_mesh_filter_jury.md) — **Blind three-AI literature jury: "over-refining tape edges causes checkerboard-like oscillations, coarse meshes filter what machine precision can't handle" — REFUTED as stated, 3/3, every citation source-verified.** Edge refinement is the published practice (Messe 2023 tip-refined references; Alves 2022b "necessary"); checkerboarding is a tolerance artifact (ε≈1e-11, Messe §4); tape-crossing oscillations are inf-sup instabilities cured by enrichment, not coarsening (Dular 2021); the canonical sharp-gradient oscillation appears when the mesh is too COARSE (Bathe §7.4.3). Two carve-outs kept: (1) real round-off/conditioning kernel — s ≥ t − log₁₀cond(K), 1e-11 sits below the half-machine-precision Newton guideline — but no source ties the floor to edge-h, and remedies are solver-side; (2) the "coarse mesh filters high frequencies" recollection is genuine in structural dynamics (Hughes Ch. 9 spurious-high-mode filtering), transfers weakly to parabolic eddy-current problems. Executable gate if ever needed: attainable-ε vs h_edge probe. Read-only; physics adjudication Christian's. Exchange: `review_edge_mesh_filter_hypothesis.md`.

- [dl20260827_gantry_jjc_jury.md](dl20260827_gantry_jjc_jury.md) — **Gantry J/Jc = O(1000) adjudicated by a blind jury round: modeling problem, not the post-processor (unanimous).** The dump's implied jc sits exactly inside the loaded `bscco-2223.hdf5` 77 K manifold, solve and postproc share the same `bn_angle` + `JcFunctionDatabase::eval` path, and `element_rho`'s 1.072e-7 ceiling is the piecewise law's normal-state branch — the solver had already quenched the tape (115 A-class AMSC tape at 340 A, 77 K, 1–5 T). The legacy `tmp/bscco/bscco.hdf5` explained the old O(1) intuition twice over: no data above 33 K (silent clamp) *and* jc ~1000× inflated (area slip in the legacy conversion). Deck then aligned to the design paper (Rudeiros Fernández et al. 2022, doi:10.1109/TASC.2022.3158366): 12 K, 340 A, DOI + tape caveat in the comments. Predicted J/jc at the new operating point: 0.50..1.14. Rerun owed; reviewed, not verified.

- [dl20260826_dr72_increment_picard.md](dl20260826_dr72_increment_picard.md) — **DR-72 remedy A′ landed: the Picard branch of `SolverData::solve` now solves in increment form** (A·δ = A·x − b, update `x -= ω·δ`, Anderson R = −δ, residual restored from backup), so an iterative solver's relative tolerance measures the increment instead of the absolute 77 K / 300 K field offset. Full plan+audit→code+audit round, Codex+Grok, all approve; algebraically identical to the relaxed Picard update in exact arithmetic and *tighter* under inexact Krylov (lagged residual rtol·ε_nl, not rtol — parity gates judge physics, not bits). Reset path traced clean independently by Claude and Codex: the controller never reads the solver vectors on a failed iterate and reassembly re-derives them. `residual()` contract, `mFieldValues` staleness, worker-rank collectives all preserved; syntax-gated with production flags. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION 2026-08-27** in a fresh own build tree (`cmake-build-claude`, Debug, np=2): adiabatic A/B on a dr30_bulk-derived deck at rtol 1e-6 — absolute form drifts **4.4e-2 K** in 10 steps (linear, all nodes), increment form holds **1.4e-9 K** (3×10⁷ less); magnetic residuals identical to the printed digit; thermal floor −52 → **−108.7 dB = 1e-11 through an iterative solver**, which the headroom rule reserved for direct solves. `make check` 14/14. Still owed: production-deck forced parity. By-catch DR-111: first-ever smoke of 2D thin-shell thermal dies on `adV >= 0` (negative layer-element Jacobian) at first assembly — the DR-06 mechanism with a reproducer; 3D path clean under the same guard. The `.hpp` comment hunk rode into commit `c65ea8d3` via a concurrent session; the `.cpp` is the DR-72 commit proper.

- [dl20260827_dr103_weights_ruling.md](dl20260827_dr103_weights_ruling.md) — **DR-103 struck and archived: the last residue, `// todo: fix weights for second order!` (`cl_CutProcessor.cpp:97`, present since the first CutProcessor commit), investigated and ruled STALE — there was nothing to fix.** The cut constraint is a nodal T-matrix tie (`CutSet::create_duplicates`, all weights 1.0); the jump [φ] = I is constant over the cut and φ is Lagrange at every order, so unit weights are exact for TRI6/TET10 too — no facet quadrature is ever formed (`DomainType::Cut` never reaches an IWG; the `FieldList::Cut` doftable and `SideSetDofLinkMode::Cut` branch are dead code; the historical ±1 lambda weak form is long deleted). Integration-rule weights would only enter a *weak* cut constraint — a design decision, not a repair. Todo removed with a rationale comment at the weights, tracker C5 struck, the register's stale "Uncommitted" corrected (fix landed in `0db03e32`), and the genuine order-2 residue split into **DR-122 [W]** (diagonal-case midside coverage unenforced, missing hanging-source cascade in `create_duplicates`, no TRI6/TET10 fixture — all unreachable behind the deliberate order-1 hanging-edge guard). Comment-only edit on Christian's direct ruling; no vendor round. Reviewed, not verified.

- [dl20260826_dr87_dr31_strikes_and_dr107_schedule.md](dl20260826_dr87_dr31_strikes_and_dr107_schedule.md) — **DR-87, DR-31 and DR-53 struck by ruling; DR-107's gate scheduled for 02:00.** **DR-53** ("changing the default setting should be considered experimental, not for user audience") settles O1's long-open default question as *keep the flag, classified experimental*; the check that made it safe is that **the fuse flags are not deck-settable** — hardcoded `false` at `cl_ThinShellFactory.hpp:353-354`, no matching string literal in `src/`, no entry in either input-contract artifact, so the two-artifact rule does not fire and **no user can reach** the ~2e-6 residual floor or the 100 ns Δt collapse without editing and rebuilding source. Surviving the strike: the standing "never re-default to `true`" constraint (reproducer `sidecoatings`, t = 2.0825 ms), and the **physics** half of O1 (graded two-branch authority vs the HEX8TB wall element), which stays in the plan as a research question, not debt. Asked to run 87 then 107 overnight, preparing 87 turned up the finding that killed the run: **the deck is no longer in the configuration DR-87 was measured in** — the row's evidence is STRUMPACK magnetic + PETSc thermal, but `input.conf:15-18` now reads `linear magnetic { library : mumps }`, switched during the 2026-08-24 four-run comparison that closed DR-02. A run would have measured MUMPS allocator behaviour, walked into DR-106, and produced hours of numbers unable to support the strike they were run for — **a gate re-run against a drifted deck is not the same gate.** Resource objections seconded it (32 GiB available vs a 47 GiB peak, the shape that killed DR-77 at 51.6 GiB; disk at 97%), as did the fact that 87's gate had already PASSED and its residue was a **watch with no defined pass threshold**. Christian: strike 87 on existing evidence, and 31 with it. Both recorded DR-42/49-style exceptions — ID+description struck, status left unstruck, tag dropped, filed to the archive. **DR-31 is the cleanest exception yet: its gate is unrunnable BY DESIGN** — order-2 h-φ terminates at an always-active `BELFEM_ERROR( max_element_order() == 1 )` in `create_hanging_edges_and_facets`, and DR-102/DR-103 were both found and fixed on the way to discovering that; struck ≠ verified applies fully, the code has never executed. 87's watch survives as a standing caution (peak past ~53 GiB = ratchet back; PEAK not trough; rides the next STRUMPACK campaign; still do NOT use `krylov method : preonly`). Register: **41 live + 69 archived = 110, no gaps, no overlap, every live row tagged.** DR-107 scheduled as `at` job 19 (CronCreate rejected — session-only, idle-only); the script guards against a concurrent compile/solver and <10 GiB disk, and aborts before testing if the never-compiled bundle fails to build. Sole surviving memdump (t=7.1 s) backed up first — DR-89 and DR-90 both died to deleted dumps.

- [dl20260826_bc_globals_one_per_block.md](dl20260826_bc_globals_one_per_block.md) — **The exodus was writing one global variable per tape; now it writes one per boundary-condition block, plus the operating temperature.** The gantry's single `current` block with an unbracketed 464-curve list expanded to 464 conditions, each publishing its own global — 464 entries holding the identical 42.0112 A, because a block has one value function and the per-group ordinals never encoded a difference (corc wrote 6 copies for the same reason). Only the first-created member of a block now carries the label; the rest keep an empty label that `update_global()` already no-ops on, and all 464 BC objects survive untouched since `set_currents` and the cohomology need them. `temperature` publishes from `gTbulk` on rank 0, erroring rather than overwriting if a BC claims the name. Verified on three decks — and 2D_Undulator is the one that proves the design: its two `userdefined` blocks yield `current_1`/`current_2` at **+0.0467 / −0.0467 A**, so real per-block variation survives while duplication collapses. check-fast 9/9, check_doc_claims 34/34. Operational warning now documented: memdump load re-adds dumped labels and the ID-mismatch check ignores the label set, so **delete `memdump.hdf5` after any globals renaming** or the retired names come back. Commit `2320d938`.

One stale claim deliberately left un-rewritten (both plans assert `src/CMakeLists.txt:12` carries a commented FVM line; it does not) — the banner says so rather than silently editing the plan's technical content. - [dl20260826_dr30_run_gate.md](dl20260826_dr30_run_gate.md) — **DR-30 run gate executed and the row closed: the guard sits on a live path but the condition it guards never occurred, and the nominated deck could never have shown either.** Four runs (corc and a bulk-superconductor substitute deck, each serial and 2-rank, cold start, all exit 0) with a temporary counter probe. The nominated "2-rank thermal-coupled corc" gate is **struck as unreachable, not passed**: corc declares no volume conductor block, so the bulk `SuperConductor` postprocessor is never constructed, and its thin-shell blocks are excluded from the aura pass. The substitute deck put a number on it — bulk path 2-rank sum 1 478 818 vs serial 1 334 102, i.e. **+144 716 (10.8 %) redundant reads = the aura, confirming the audit's reachability claim by measurement**, while the TS path sums to exactly the serial count (zero overhead) in both decks. **Stale-element count: 0 everywhere**, including all 144 716 aura reads — the thermal group contained every element the maxwell group did. Fix is correct, harmless and defensive-in-practice; corc terminal voltages agree 1-vs-2 ranks to 1e-8 .. 1e-6. Probe removed. **The silent-keep in `link_element_maxwell_thermal` is ruled a keeper, not dead code** — it is the sanctioned fallback for a maxwell element with no thermal counterpart and must not become an assert; its comment now records that intent, the consumer-side obligation, and the measured latency.
- [dl20260826_dr30_stale_thermal_peer.md](dl20260826_dr30_stale_thermal_peer.md) — **DR-30 code half landed: superconductor postproc no longer reads a stale thermal peer's temperature.** Both `compute_superconductor`/`_ts` now use the thermal peer's T only when the peer's linked element id equals the current element's, else `gTbulk` (same contract as the block-absent branch). Full plan+audit→code+audit round, Codex+Grok. The audits' load-bearing correction: the plan called the guard defensive-only, but both vendors independently traced the live firing path — `recover_fields` walks `mMyElementIndices`, whose second pass adds the **aura** for non-TS blocks, and each kernel expands its own aura, so maxwell conductor aura elements can be absent from the thermal group **today** on ≥2 ranks; TS recovery (side-local) and `compute_element_data` (owned-only) never trigger it. Syntax-gated with production flags; **reviewed, not verified** — serial cannot exercise the fallback, the 2-rank thermal-coupled corc run is the gate (row retagged [CODE]→[RUN]). Residuals stay on the row: T not in `mPostprocessorSourceFields`; `gTbulk` bias on maxwell-only aura fringe (complete fix = expand thermal aura); pre-existing `norm(N*q)` vs assembly `dot(Nvec,q)`+clamp mismatch.
- [dl20260826_dr108_uint8_source_overflow.md](dl20260826_dr108_uint8_source_overflow.md) — **DR-108 opened, re-rooted, fixed and gated in one day: the dead gantry yoke arm was a one-byte counter, not a ferromagnetic-cut formulation defect.** `Basis::mNumberOfSources` was a `uint8_t` (`cl_Mesh_Basis.hpp:33`) and `CutSet::create_duplicates` hands `set_sources` a list of [N abstract current nodes, then the ORIGINAL node]. At the deck's 464 cuts, 465 wraps to 209: the survivors are all abstract nodes, **the original is dropped**, and `phi_dup = phi_org + Σ I_c` degenerates to the constant `209·I` — the cut stops transmitting flux and everything behind it is severed, which is literally Christian's read of the plot. The proof is a law, not an argument: **32 distinct N values from 425 to 464, every group pinned to exactly `((N+1) mod 256)·I`, min == max inside each group**. Three claims from the opening brief refuted — the "inconsistent MMFs" are the wrapped constant and differences against it, the "mixed jump signs" were an unordered-pair artifact (all 303 iron pairs are sign-coherent, so NOT another probe-4b instance), and the defect is material-blind: **205 of the 302 corrupted nodes are AIR**, so the ferro-cut mitigation flag would have hidden the iron island and left the air trunk broken. Christian's question — "what if we go up to 16 bits? I just used `uint8_t` to save memory" — got a measured answer: **the byte was never saved.** `sizeof` is byte-identical at uint8/16/32/64 for `Basis`, `Node`, `Edge`, `Face`, `Element`, because each counter is followed by a pointer and sat in alignment padding. He landed `uint16_t` plus `BELFEM_ERROR` guards (`d0f4db7d`) and re-ran; **gate G1 is clean: 0 wrapped nodes (was 302), 1371/1371 cut jumps exact, 1366/1366 cut pairs with B continuous, no dead islands anywhere, the dead arm carrying 0.46 T.** My medium-confidence read of residual O1 ("field recovery at a cut terminus") was **wrong** — it was collateral, and the run says so; kept in the file with the correction attached. Prior art in the same tree: `cl_Vertex.hpp:59` widened `mFacetCounter` to `uint16_t` "due to cohomoligies" — right fix, wrong counter. Side findings registered as DR-109 (`classify_periodic_pairs` silently skips every pair above 64 cuts) and DR-110 (`add_source` guards the counter, not the allocation). Vendor agreement was **cued** — the brief named integer narrowing among five candidate classes — so the verdict rests on the measurement; Grok also breached the read-only instruction with a 40-line fprintf probe, reverted same session. **Owed:** gantry np = 10 and `make check-fast`; DR-108 struck under the DR-42/49 exception, struck is not verified.

- [dl20260826_ferro_cut_flux_island.md](dl20260826_ferro_cut_flux_island.md) — **DR-108 registered: iron-piercing cuts leave the gantry's upper yoke arm flux-starved** — found on the first-ever completed gantry run (serial, enabled by the 08-25 facet fixes). Christian's plot read ("jump not imposed on both sides of the cut") confirmed and refined by measurement: the cut fragments the iron into 6 components incl. dust slivers; the dead arm (375 nodes) is tied but sits at |B| ≡ 0.000 / φ = const — the jump machinery works (main interface carries exactly 4×58×42.011 A) while the per-fragment MMFs are mutually inconsistent (8818/8780/10250 A). Same family as the corc in-plane sign-coherence finding. A false alarm resolved on the way: the "missing half magnet" is the deck's declared y<0 mirror symmetry; geometry complete, run numerically healthy at 12% ramp. Christian's mitigation idea recorded (input flag: forbid ferro-piercing cuts ⇒ treat yoke like a coil, condense its cut out; this geometry provably admits an iron-free layout). Brief: `todo/ferro_cut_flux_island.md`. DR-100 carry-over: strays = the postprocessor field-count handshake (value 2) from racing workers; destructive probe reverted after it deadlocked np=10; tracing probes armed for the next parallel run.

- [dl20260825_progressbar_mpi_buffering.md](dl20260825_progressbar_mpi_buffering.md) — **The progress bar arrives in one burst under `mpirun` because of TWO independent buffers, and both had to go.** Class side: `Progressbar` redraws with `\r`, never writes a newline and never flushed, so glibc released it only when the buffer *filled* — 1024 B on a pty (jerky but alive), 4096 B on a pipe, which a 3.3 kB bar never reaches (everything at exit). Launcher side, and this is the half a plausible story would have missed: **the flush alone does not fix it** — `strace -f -tt` shows the rank writing correct 76-byte frames every 20 ms while **prterun** holds the whole stream and dumps 3043 B at job end. Only `mpirun --stream-buffering 0` releases it (3 chunks → ~90; `--stream-buffering 1` does nothing, line buffering cannot help a bar with no newline). Trap recorded: the flag sets *exactly* `OMPI_MCA_ompi_stream_buffering=0` in the child (env diffed) yet exporting that by hand does **not** stream — it must be the CLI option. Class fixes: `flush()` per frame; `draw()` extracted so `mStep` is finally assigned (the throttle had always compared against 0, redrawing on **every** `step()` — one `write()` per element in the Exodus writer once flushing started); `mNumSteps > 0` and overflow/clamp guards; destructor hands the cursor back (closes the ordinary-teardown half of the DR-107 leak; **the `BELFEM_ERROR` half stays open** — `error_abort()` calls `std::abort()`, `assert.cpp` deliberately untouched, Christian's call). `examples/scripts/Allrun`: flag added on the `mpirun` path only (no `srun` equivalent), capability-probed with `--version` — validates the option without spawning ranks, ~4 ms, allocation-safe. **Pre-existing bug found in the same block and fixed:** `status=${PIPESTATUS[0]}; tee_status=${PIPESTATUS[1]}` — an assignment is a command, so it rewrites `PIPESTATUS` to one element and `[1]` trips `set -u`, killing the script *before* `exit "$status"`; at HEAD `./Allrun` exited 1 on success AND on failure, so the solver status never propagated. Now `pipe_status=( "${PIPESTATUS[@]}" )`; stub-deck verified 0→0, 42→42. `doc/parallel_execution.md` updated (flag in the recommended command line + why it is not `stdbuf -oL`). **Owed: `make check-fast` and a real `mpirun` cohomology deck.**

- [dl20260825_dr107_homology_cleanup.md](dl20260825_dr107_homology_cleanup.md) — **DR-107 executed the same evening it was registered (Christian: "if Grok and Codex agree, we can clean up"): five dead-code items out of `src/homology`, net -1201/+135, double jury (plan + code), landed uncommitted.** Gone: `clean_greedy` + the whole `mFunClean` dispatch (`clean()` calls `clean_spfa()` directly; the live greedy is `rectify_greedy_sweeps` and stays), both field ctors + `generatorsFromField` (the Homology one left `mSimplicialComplex` indeterminate, sharper than the register knew), `create_sidesets_2d/3d` + write-only `mCohomologies` + the whole `SideSetFactory` TU, dead `updatekGeneratorsFromHomology_old`; `tTransInv` becomes `Matrix<int>` (the real container already truncated through `int` in `rowExchange`; the int ring is what the kernel always computed in). Usage guide rewritten to the live `SimplicialComplex`/CutProcessor paths. Round mechanics: Codex leg died on a usage limit (reported; credits added; blind rerun), then 2/2 agreement; the code jury caught four defects in the round's own doc edits, fixed in-round. Gates: scoped grep zero refs, `-fsyntax-only` production-flags clean ×3 TUs; **owed: Christian's `make check` (homology 9/9) + commit + strike.** Routed to Christian: two real defects the blind jury found in his pre-existing Progressbar hunks (cursor-hide leak on the coarse-mesh abort; stall WARNING demoted off Default).

- [dl20260825_thinshell_facet_clobber.md](dl20260825_thinshell_facet_clobber.md) — **Gantry crash fixed: `update_facet_nodes()` was clobbering the cut-factory-restored tape facets** — and the fix's own parallel regression found and fixed the same evening. The 464-tape deck put a cut terminus on a tape whose facet master sits on the jump side; the final finalize in `CutFactory::run()` re-derived the restored facet from that master and injected the cut's unlinked MMF node (self-original, 209 tape-slave sources) → `collect_nodes` bitset assert (release build: raw heap write). Fix (full plan+audit→code+audit, Codex+Grok): `update_facet_nodes` skips `ThinShell`/`GeometryOnly` sidesets, plus a `BELFEM_ERROR` guard before the bitset write. The gantry rerun then exposed the regression: `connect_facets_to_elements` re-derives facet masters by node identity, so post-fix worker ranks abort at distribute ("Could not find master") on fresh-built meshes — bfm loads mask it. Second full audit round → the calculator now trusts inherited `has_master()` links (wires the wrapper container, deliberately no `set_master` — its relink would undo the fix; also load-bearing in release, where the abort is a compiled-out assert). Residual flagged, not fixed: `.bfm` reload reconstructs facet nodes from masters regardless. Also repaired `ask_grok.sh` (premature errexit re-arm killed retries). Gates pending: fresh-build gantry np=10 distribute+run, check-fast.
