- [dl20260825_halfcut_tie_fix.md](dl20260825_halfcut_tie_fix.md) — **The cap-corner fix landed: pure half-cut periodic ties (Option B), full plan+audit→code+audit round.** Root cause: the seam policy left the 66 half-cut edges at the tape terminals untied — free per-cap corner dofs breaking discrete periodicity exactly at the corners. - [dl20260825_cap_corner_defect.md](dl20260825_cap_corner_defect.md) — **The periodic-cap current anomaly identified: the corner pinch.** Christian's "too much of a coincidence, check the t-matrices" call drove a measured elimination chain — orientation-convention perturbation (bit-identical field ⇒ exonerated), 171k edge-on-node + 53k edge-on-edge constraint rows censused with ZERO violations, postprocessor cleared by the decay profile (5-6 rows, not row-0) — leaving one structural mechanism that fits all seven fingerprints: **unduplicated tape corner nodes pinch the sheet's φ-jump to zero at the corner points**, generating antisymmetric, cap-mirrored, σ-selected corner circulations (2.4×/0.23× at the immediate cap row). Confirmed in the φ field via the bfm duplicate map (plateau +0.1043 uniform; corner values collapse and sign-flip, cap rows strongest). Fix direction: extend duplication to the terminal corners (side-curve × terminal-curve junction policy), full audit round pending; three env-gated probes remain in-tree. Quench-onset readings adjacent to the caps should be discounted until fixed.

- [dl20260825_corc_interface_contract.md](dl20260825_corc_interface_contract.md) — **corc_solder cut forensics → the sideset-only interface contract.** Probe-verified that BOTH homology lineages wire the current constraint identically on the terminals deck (bit-identical coupling matrices/census; the 7-vs-3 divergence belongs to the retired curves BC). - [dl20260825_alpha_nonmetals_callaway_fix.md](dl20260825_alpha_nonmetals_callaway_fix.md) — **Overnight: the Callaway optical channel fixed and YBCO refitted — degenerately: the class's electronic Wiedemann–Franz term reproduces Sommerfeld 2003 to 10 % on its own, so the phonon parameters are unidentifiable (interim set documented, modelling decision → Christian). - [dl20260825_materials_doc_overhaul.md](dl20260825_materials_doc_overhaul.md) — **Three-AI overhaul of all seven `src/physics/materials/doc` files: fact sheet → blind Codex+Grok audits → rewrite → Codex prose sweep, with every file:line citation now generated from anchors (`scripts/resolve_doc_cites.py`).** The old set listed materials that do not exist, an invented `Metal` API and the field accessors in the wrong argument order; the two February alloy proposals became `alloy_homogenization.md` with a can/cannot section (solders and isostructural solutions yes; austenite, Invar, ferromagnets no) and the Fe71Cr19Ni10-vs-NIST-304 example. - [dl20260824_night_shift_dr02_dr102_dr103.md](dl20260824_night_shift_dr02_dr102_dr103.md) — **Overnight autonomous session.** Eleven register rows closed/advanced across midnight on Christian's rulings (DR-11, 13, 16, 17/42/49, 18, 19-first-gate, 22, 23-T9, 26, 27, DR-06-run). Its green gate immediately exposed **DR-103** (cut-pipeline TET10 feeds `gNoIndex`-stamped midside nodes into a bitset) — registered with mechanism, deliberately left for the morning's design call.

- [dl20260824_wachtman_elastic_review.md](dl20260824_wachtman_elastic_review.md) — **Jury review of the Wachtman elasticity commit `bcc47bcd` (Fable+Codex+Grok): by source trace the commit had not been built — three compile errors and every one of the nine metals failing at construction (stale `E_custom` overrides on empty polynomials, Nickel's never-constructed bridge Bezier, a NaN-clamped E spline, `theta(n)` OOB write, α=0 at the first ν grid point, `cp` frozen at room temperature, Cu/Ag/Pb inverting the Debye curve before E existed). - [dl20260824_iron_kohler_review.md](dl20260824_iron_kohler_review.md) — **Jury review of the material-model commit `acd4bbf0` (Fable+Codex+Grok, 3/3 convergence on every core finding), then the Iron Kohler rebuild it triggered: the committed quadratics were already the LSQ optima — the defect was structural, and Christian's flat-ended-Bezier design fixes it at zero fit cost (rms 0.0568 vs 0.0565).** Iron's `create_kohler()` was an Aluminum copy with three paste errors, never called, with the override having removed the `BELFEM_ERROR` backstop; Al/Fe evaluated the log-space saturation at `exp(w1)` instead of `w1` (underflow to exactly 0). Refit takes the transverse peak from the dataset's own quadratic vertex (w=11.17, Δρ/ρ=95.4) and lets `xi_by_x` clamping hold it to the longitudinal knot — no tail polynomial. Fixes applied on approval; executable gate (Iron ρ-database build + kohler C⁰ spot-check) owed at next rebuild.

- [dl20260823_tape_quench_deck_repair.md](dl20260823_tape_quench_deck_repair.md) — **`build/tape_quench` (Christian's restored deck) repaired through three real plugin defects, closing in on DR-02's coverage gap.** Merge-conflicted `input.conf` resolved (Christian's ruling, CustomMat reference settings); `buffer` reserved-word collision — **DR-65's exact mechanism on a second deck** — renamed to `custom_buffer`; missing `T_crit` added (92.5 K, matching the builtin YBCO material); jc/n found registered through the wrong `set_user_defined_function` overload (1-dependency generic table, never read by `jc_eval`/`n_eval`) and fixed to the 3-dependency `JcFunctionUserDefined` path, matching the existing `mu(H,T)` pattern. With all three fixed, the deck reaches real coupled Picard/Newton iteration for the first time, then hits a genuine (not plumbing) convergence wall: floor-retry exhaustion at 5-14 dB oscillation, current ramp ruled out as the cause. Open question for Christian: deck tuning or a real kernel-collapse defect — deliberately not tolerance-loosened past it.

- [dl20260823_debt_register_triage_and_run_gates.md](dl20260823_debt_register_triage_and_run_gates.md) — **Full debt-register triage (49 live rows → 14 run-only / 5 ruling-only / 14 mixed / 14 code; DR-98 + DR-99 struck), then the first run-gate batch on Christian's go:** `make check-fast` 9/9 (DR-17's four Penta6Ts tests pass by name; DR-23's cohomology suites execute under ctest for the first time), DR-75 all three terminal-pair gates PASS (named abort at `cl_ElectricalCircuitFactory.cpp:526` incl. label + bracket hint), DR-79's keyless-MUMPS gate PASS (`ICNTL(35)=0`, zero BLR statistics at `-v 4`), DR-40 both clauses verified (amplitude verbatim, no bisected cable). **New P1: DR-100 — shipped `examples/RLC_Circuit` crashes under its own `./Allrun` (np=4) in the full-matrix collect**, serial and np=2 pass, costheta np=4 passes: deck × rank-count, deterministic, < 2 min reproducer. - [dl20260823_hex8tb_hex8ts_g.md](dl20260823_hex8tb_hex8ts_g.md) — **HEX8TB + HEX8TS G-operators complete — the campaign's element work is done (9 of 10; LINE3 stays a contract decision).** HEX8TB: exact orthonormal cuboid frame, G = s(∇F)⊗∇ξ, div ≡ 0 by orthonormality, and the element's first compiled curl-sign gate — a per-dof Stokes check (the obvious (1,1,0,0) loop telescopes to a vacuous 0 = 0, a Grok catch). HEX8TS: the audited term1 contract — reference derivatives chained through the class's own per-point thin-shell nablas, tie-exact to C on every geometry, with a three-case scope docstring. Headline catch, found three ways independently: the fixture quad everyone called "rectangular" is a general quad (|FD−term1| = 0.116 there); FD + trace now run on a rotated-rectangle override. Four breaks with pre-registered signatures incl. a top-columns-only mS drop caught solely by the post-audit top-flip test; 145/145 + 14/14. The C.3 Grok audit ran as a manual dispatch by Christian from an embedded-source prompt.

- [dl20260823_tri6_tet10_g.md](dl20260823_tri6_tet10_g.md) — **TRI6 + TET10 G-operators complete — the per-point Hessian channel on genuinely curved geometry, built on two audited devices: Q-folding (per-dof coefficients recovered from the assembled E, making TET10's face-orientation tables unnecessary) and link-time constant curvature tensors (NaN-filled on the straight branch, where `NaN·0 = NaN` would otherwise poison a mathematically-zero term).** The rounds killed three silent-kill classes pre-implementation (member-`mJ` reads, unsigned TRI6 partials, Jacobian-dump stride transposes), each now with its own break gate; the fixture gained the campaign's first genuinely-curved quadratic geometry (the `aCurved` flag alone never curved anything — the Hessian channel was previously untestable in principle). First-run green, curved FD ratios 4.000/4.00001, five breaks with bound signatures, 139/139 + 14/14. **Campaign: 7 of 10 elements done; HEX8TB, HEX8TS, LINE3 remain.**

- [dl20260823_hex8_g_and_dr98.md](dl20260823_hex8_g_and_dr98.md) — **HEX8 G-operator complete (the first with the inverse-map Hessian term the curl cancels), and DR-98 found-and-fixed the same day: `EF_HEX8::C()` returned the NEGATED curl** — caught by the C.1 blind round, probe-verified twice over, invisible for years because HEX8 had zero battery coverage and `K = CᵀC` is sign-invariant. Christian approved the operand swap; the new battery closes it permanently with a both-sides-integrated Stokes test (old sign: loop = 1 / flux = −1, red). Campaign firsts: a true order-of-accuracy FD gate (ratio = 4.000 at h vs h/2, after the truncation floor rejected a roundoff-regime ratio at the default step), and a pure-Hessian control (φ=ξ, proven `G·q = H⁽⁰⁾` exactly by the audit). Three break gates red-then-green. `test_fem` 129/129, `make check` 14/14. Remaining: TRI6, TET10, HEX8TB, HEX8TS, LINE3.

- [dl20260823_coulomb_gauge_stepC_elements.md](dl20260823_coulomb_gauge_stepC_elements.md) — **G-operator Step C: TET4+TRI3 and PENTA6TS+QUAD4TS complete through C.1–C.4, every stage blind-audited by both vendors, every gate executed and quoted.** TET4/TRI3: constant link-time G, FD vs E to 1.7e-13, curl tie to 3.5e-17; mS-drop break gate red only on the flipped fixture (0.457), as designed. Thin shells: PENTA6TS is the first point-dependent G (single shared W tensor — the auditors' ∇ζ identity collapsed three to one); F₀↔F₁ break gate red (0.299/0.597) at the τ=0.4 sample point chosen for exactly that; both auditors independently refuted the first kernel-test sketch (equal dofs = Ampère mode, not a constant field) and the corrected test carries the wrong vector as its negative control. `make check` 14/14, `test_fem` 122/122. Remaining: HEX8 (DJ term owed), TRI6, TET10, HEX8TB, HEX8TS, LINE3 (contract with Christian).

- [dl20260822_coulomb_gauge_stepBC.md](dl20260822_coulomb_gauge_stepBC.md) — **G-operator Steps B + C.1/C.2 (same night, after Christian's Step A sign-off): `EdgeFunction::G()` interface landed and build-verified — pure virtual beside E/C, `mGrad` member carrying the single layout contract (`G(i+d·j,e) = ∂(w_e)_j/∂x_i`, explicit curl-tie rows), hard-fail stubs in all ten subclasses.** Audit-endorsed deviation: member named `mGrad`, not `mG` (seven subclasses own a private `mG` that would shadow it). Four more blind two-vendor rounds: B-code (zero functional defects; "and cyclic" curl-tie comment fixed to explicit rows), C.1 math for TET4+TRI3 (pair tables re-derived from `E()` by both vendors; FD-recipe traps caught — `E()` aliases `mE`, `precompute()` clobbers quadrature tables), C.2 plan (both "needs revision": TRI3 loop written out, header-inline `G()` + stub deletion, flipped-edge `mS` falsifier, pinned non-constant φ for the gradient-mode test). **Stopped at the plan-v3 sign-off gate; C.3 implementation not started.**

- [dl20260822_coulomb_gauge_stepA.md](dl20260822_coulomb_gauge_stepA.md) — **Coulomb-gauge penalty Step A: the drafted penalty audited from first principles and found (a) dimensionally inconsistent as written and (b) structurally vacuous on TET4/TRI3/thin-shell meshes — the discrete curl null space (cohomology modes included) is element-wise constant, so no element-local ∇h penalty can touch it; on TET4, G′G is exactly ½·C′C, i.e. a pure artificial-resistivity perturbation.** Five math-level probes executed (output quoted in the note); blind Codex+Grok round confirmed all decision-driving results, refuted two draft claims (mRhoMin is 0.0 by the 2026-08-10 ruling, not 1e-16 — recalled-not-reread, L-08; HEX8TS is thin-shell machinery, not a "legacy wrap"), and added the box-HEX8 ∇(xy) caveat, the ρ* averaging fork, and the missing Q6 section. Consistent alternative derived (K-channel, γ = χ·ρ*/μ², no dJdx for frozen ρ*); edge-element-compatible remedies tabled (weak-div per Monk §7.4, tree–cotree per Dular 1997). Theory note: `todo/coulomb_gauge_penalty_theory.md`. **Stopped at the Step A sign-off gate; G-operator Steps B/C not started.**

- [dl20260820_methodology_lessons_landing.md](dl20260820_methodology_lessons_landing.md) — **`doc/lessons_learned.md` lands (388 lines: a 141-line trigger-indexed tripwire layer over 18 evidence-backed cards, from 539 catalogued incidents), and `METHODOLOGY.md` grows the method description around it (41 → 106 lines: five terminology rows, four new deviations, a "The method, measured" closing section).** The lessons file is wired to be read, not merely present: a Layer 1 item in the `CLAUDE.md` compliance checklist and an entry in `doc/README.md`; run scaffolding (catalog pointers, the pre-registration `LOCKED` stamp) was stripped on the way in. Two corrections were made to the drafts before placing them: a lineage miscount that had already propagated downstream (five "rules" naming four cards), and an overclaim that the six rules without published analogue "cluster tightly" around epistemic hygiene — enumerated, three fit, one is adjacent, two are ordinary engineering lessons outside the books consulted. A conflict between two source drafts was resolved toward the stricter side: the Knight and Leveson row was placed **without** the study's figures, pending a read of the primary paper. **The Codex prose sweep was narrowed twice**: to user-facing documentation only, excluding `METHODOLOGY.md`, `doc/lessons_learned.md`, devlogs and — after the question was raised — `./todo/` files, whose standing sweep rule is withdrawn in `doc/ai_collaboration_protocol.md` §6 as not worth its cost. `check_doc_claims.py` 34/34. Pre-commit sweep same day: deviation 8 reworded to describe the proposed closure fields truthfully, the INC-538 backfill (eight index entries) and the INC-539 dangling-link removal applied to this index, bringing it to 0 unindexed / 0 dangling.

- [dl20260819_dr92_restart_step_cap.md](dl20260819_dr92_restart_step_cap.md) — **DR-92 solved and fixed: a warm restart must not let the solver be born at a large timestep.** A restart's behaviour is decided by the Δt of its FIRST step and cannot be repaired afterwards ( same process: 10851 at 50 ms, still 9888 after shrinking to 25 ms ). MPI, BDF restore, the φ0 hole, the imposed current, the step ratio and BDF order all died by measurement first. Fix: cap the re-entry step at the deck's `initial timestep`; the controller grows back under its own limiter. All four acceptance criteria passed — `it. 0` 10851 → **0.903286** / 16 Krylov, 2 iterates, and the same process climbs 5 → 50 ms with zero rejections. Reverses the Δt half of the DR-38 restart-cliff policy, keeps the BDF-history half. Christian's save-point insight killed `bdf_last_dt` as a cap and showed exact state reconstruction would not have fixed it either.
- [dl20260819_bnorm_restart_anomaly.md](dl20260819_bnorm_restart_anomaly.md) — **First run of the merged binary showed 1.5e5 preconditioned GMRES residuals and maxit-bound solves after a Δt=50ms warm restart; three-voice sweep + a dump-restore discriminator EXONERATED the binary ( DR-92 ).** `GMRES it.0` is the left-preconditioned ‖M⁻¹b‖ ( never ‖b‖ ); all code-change hypotheses refuted with citations; the anomaly follows the restored state — every healthy restore re-entered at ≤10.6 ms, the sick one leapt 50 ms off a reconstructed BDF stencil. The reproducer dump was lost to a skipped backup ( new standing rule: Claude copies endangered files himself ). Main merge unblocked.
- [dl20260818_reltol_default_strumpack.md](dl20260818_reltol_default_strumpack.md) — **Christian's 1e-8 vs 1e-10 A/B (his hypothesis, over Claude's pre-registered objection) identified the timestep-collapse mechanism: the printed nonlinear residual IS the linear exit residual, and the 1e-8 test stopped GMRES mid-dive — Picard 'stalls', Newton kicks, ω sawtooths and Δt collapses were downstream of a lying yardstick.** At 1e-10: 2 iterates/step, Δt grows, step-175 triple rejection gone, exits at ~1e-15. Follow-up audited change (both vendors, both rounds, no blocker): STRUMPACK now ALWAYS receives the class default 1e-10 — the DR-76 have-flag gate was REFINE-era and an unstated deck inherited the library's 1e-6; unmapped Krylov methods hard-error instead of silently running REFINE. The 'exact factor delivers ~2e-16' theory was found in seven live places and retired everywhere. DR-88 evidence and DR-90 traces flagged artifact-suspect.
- [dl20260818_dr38_thermal_entry_residual.md](dl20260818_dr38_thermal_entry_residual.md) — **DR-38's open question answered by a unanimous three-voice sweep: the thermal `1.000000 ( 0.00 dB )` entry residual is an unseeded-dof artifact, present on cold starts too, and NOT evidence about a restored thermal state (new row DR-91).** `ThermalFactory` seeds free dofs from `T` at `:242`, five lines before `T` is filled at `:247-248`; `initialize()` never runs again (`mInitializedFlag` is never cleared), so `x = 0` at the first assembly makes the Picard residual exactly `‖−b‖/‖b‖`. `MaxwellFactory` never initializes, so the magnetic manager IS seeded by `load_memdump` after the field restore — the whole magnetic/thermal asymmetry. No physics damage: Grok's ω = 1 argument (`x_new = x + β(G−x) = G` regardless of x) shows the unseeded zero writes the same field; Claude's ω-growth damage claim refuted (first iterate skips AIMD, ω already at its 1.0 ceiling). The fix matters because the safety is accidental — a driver starting at ω < 1 would write `T ← ω·G`. Kills the step-124 jury's thermal leg. **Fix landed same day** through the full two-vendor plan+code audit round (no blocker; reviewed, not verified): `seed_dof_values()` free-dof seeding in ThermalFactory and load_memdump; **gate PASSED, verified by execution**: the step-152 warm restart printed `Thermal Picard 1 … −91.57 dB` where every prior process printed exactly `1.000000` — inside the −73…−99 dB continuation band, magnetic unchanged. The entry state was never wrong, only the yardstick.
- [dl20260818_step124_jury.md](dl20260818_step124_jury.md) — **Step-124 jury (Fable+Codex+Grok, read-only): the old-binary rejection vs branch acceptance is best explained by H3 — restart ≠ continuation plus fresh STRUMPACK matching — NOT by the sparse fixes; "bitset_and_spmatrix_fix made 124 converge" is unproven.** Magnetic Picard-1 matched to six digits (same restored field, from the old run's own dump) but thermal Picard-1 split 0.00 vs −82.99 dB, shifting Picard-2 through the coupling and dodging the Newton-3 kick the old live process ω-crawled on (as it already had at 119; its 124 retry actually ACCEPTED). Claude retracted two claims on vendor refutation, incl. the 6892-vs-0.408 GMRES-r0 corruption reading (stale matched-system scaling). H1 sentinel = real latent release-mode OOB write, H2 base-flip = real MUMPS-deck hazard — both correctly fixed, blast radius on past STRUMPACK/PETSc results LOW. Restart-from-1800 recommendation withdrawn; DR-38 ledger annotated with the untraced thermal-entry-residual question.
- [dl20260817_dr89_abs_tol.md](dl20260817_dr89_abs_tol.md) — **The startup crawl jury-diagnosed and fixed same day: STRUMPACK's never-overridden library default `abs_tol = 1e-10` sat exactly at the −85 dB stall entry at startup's small ‖b‖, while the weekend's large ‖b‖ hid it — Christian's "it worked on the weekend" objection fully vindicated, constant-jc control killed as unnecessary by all three voices.** The old refinement OVERSHOT to machine precision; GMRES stops AT tolerance — a silent 5-decade contract change under a deck tuned to the old behavior. Fix: `absolute tolerance` deck key, STRUMPACK default 1e-14 always applied, PETSc deck-stated-only; contract pair + 3 tests + S9 gate premise corrected; ctest 14/14. Live gate = the warm restart from the 100 ms memdump.
- [dl20260816_dr69_unfolded_angle.md](dl20260816_dr69_unfolded_angle.md) — **DR-69 implemented (plan+audit → code+audit): `bn_angle` now returns the unfolded θ ∈ [0,π]; each law folds internally if even.** The audits earned their cost: both refuted the Database wrap (θ=π silently read the 0° node — in eval AND all three derivative paths; fixed with `<=`), Grok killed the width-test guard (coverage test in the ctor instead; below-range angles extrapolate, they don't clamp) and G2-as-deck (Kim isn't reachable from input.conf — became a unit test), Codex found the raw-pass-through plugin contract. Partially verified by execution: ctest 14/14, and a probe through `JcFunctionDatabase` on shipped sp-ap shows jc(70°)/jc(110°) = 1.460 — the solve now expresses the measured asymmetry; evaluated endpoints agree 1.002–1.027, so the export never broke periodicity (Codex's 35% was control-point level). Sideset sign = jc-lobe selection documented in both contract artifacts. Deck-side gates G1/G3 remain.
- [dl20260816_normal_component_trace.md](dl20260816_normal_component_trace.md) — **By ≡ 0 on thin-shell layer blocks traced blind-parallel (Codex+Grok): the SOLVE is intact — maxwell and thermal material laws consume the projected h_n end-to-end; ghost penalty and side connectors refuted as causes.** The gap is the postproc node-field write (`E*q` only, no through-thickness dof → normal row exactly zero) = DR-84; Grok additionally caught that the J/Jc lookup's b is the UNPROJECTED air average with a false same-as-solve comment = DR-85 (one-line fix, lands with DR-69's signed flip); aura-φ lag hypothesis = DR-86. Christian's Mac "Bx=0 / 1.4 T" view is not in the file — viewer-side; 1.4 matches the campaign's JJC number.
- [dl20260816_dr69_gate2_provenance.md](dl20260816_dr69_gate2_provenance.md) — **DR-69 gate 2 PASSED, verified on the raw public data:** the θ↔180°−θ asymmetry is in the raw Robinson/SuperCurrent sweep itself (figshare 10.6084/m9.figshare.4256624.v3, SuperPower AP, ratios 1.27–1.39 at 0.3–0.5 T / 80–85 K, peak near 80°), with a decisive built-in control — the same sweeps are 180°-periodic to ≤2.6% while the 90°-mirror deviates 22–39%, so it is physical pinning, not the smoothed export. Signed-β implementation now waits only on gate 1 (Christian's constant-jc control run) plus the sst-1 product-designation match.
- [dl20260816_dr82_dr83_dr72.md](dl20260816_dr82_dr83_dr72.md) — **DR-82 fixed (all four HDF5 loaders now pass `datatype<T>()` as the MEMORY type + always-active class-mismatch guard; mixed-width files convert instead of silently corrupting), DR-83 fixed (all four `BELFEM_DEBUG` sites flipped to `DEBUG` — first compilation those blocks ever had, all gates green), and DR-72 re-scoped by a Fable read-only pass:** the increment form is NOT an IWG_Timestep change — the Newton branch already solves `J·δ = r`; only the Picard branch solves the absolute system, so the real fix is ~40–80 lines in `SolverData::solve`. Parked for the release on Fable's recommendation; register row corrected so the eventual taker isn't sent down the IWG-scale path.
- [dl20260816_solver_memory_doc.md](dl20260816_solver_memory_doc.md) — **Blind-parallel doc campaign (Christian's design: two blind drafts → cross-audit → unify) produced `src/sparse/doc/solver_memory_and_compression.md` — and flushed out DR-79: every MUMPS deck without an explicit `compression scheme : off` runs silently LOSSY BLR at 1e-8 absolute today; garber pairs it with a 1e-10 nonlinear tolerance (negative headroom, the 07-06 pattern shifted two decades).** Method paid for itself: Grok refuted the brief's own mesh-replication model and the OOM count; each draft shipped a wrong-or-nonexistent knob the other's audit caught; Claude's "MUMPS auto-BLR is lossless" reassurance died to its own follow-up trace (initialize() overwrites CNTL(7) with the 1e-8 cutoff under a mislabeled comment); and both drafts had the STRUMPACK estimate line wrong (it's a SUM over ranks). Manual facts settled from the fetched MUMPS 5.7.3 guide, incl. the answer to Christian's leaf-size question: MUMPS has no equivalent, its BLR clustering is internal. Next: unification code round + MUMPS driver jury.
- [dl20260816_bdf_history_restore.md](dl20260816_bdf_history_restore.md) — **DR-78 overnight campaign: the BDF warm restart's first execution with history crashed because the dump's field history was broken on BOTH ends — saved without collecting (parallel dumps store rank-0-local rotations, sized and plausible: silent-wrong-answer class) and restored without distributing (workers assemble BDF5 from empty shells; NDEBUG turns the guarding assert into a SEGV).** Fixed end-to-end: collective history collect in save_memdump, distribute+per-rank hard verification (named error: field, rank, dump, promised depth) before restore_history_state. The serial production mechanism survived four refuted hypotheses and a mini-deck reproducer that refuses to crash even under deliberate dump corruption — unpinned, but the verification sweep names the culprit on next contact. By-catch lessons: a reproducer that resumes past its simulation horizon proves nothing; hand-launched runs skip Allrun's OMP_NUM_THREADS=2 and STRUMPACK's T=4 workspace helped OOM the study three times.
- [dl20260815_petsc_softfail_syslog.md](dl20260815_petsc_softfail_syslog.md) — **The 21:19 DIVERGED_ITS abort answered on three fronts (plan + dual audit → code + dual audit): PETSc non-convergence now soft-fails into the controller's timestep cut instead of killing the run; the tapestack3d thermal solve moved to MUMPS (direct ~1e-15 retires the rtol×T drift outright); and BELFEM_ERROR writes its message to syslog (identity `belfem`, rank in payload).** The soft-fail branch is Allreduce-hardened (STRUMPACK's lost bet not repeated), covers iteration-class reasons only (NaN/PC failures stay hard — KSP reuse unestablished), and the new linear `max iterations` key caps the Krylov budget with the 0-never-reaches-PETSc trap avoided. The syslog placement survived a real reviewer conflict: debug builds THROW by default, so the quiet-for-tests placement would never have logged in the builds developers run — own flag, disabled in all 14 test mains. Executable gate caveat (Grok): after the deck switch the quench run cannot certify the PETSc path; that gate needs its own deck.
- [dl20260815_circuit_cut_a.md](dl20260815_circuit_cut_a.md) — **Cut A executed per the dual-audited plan: circuit off the FEM include graph (PBC.hpp thinned, includes finally under the guard, forward decls), LIBLIST and include-dir hygiene (13 rows → 3), dead read_circuit/cl_Circuit.hpp includes gone, and the DR-75 terminal-pair freeze — one bracket group per pair, named setup error, three contract docs rewritten.** The claimed CMake link cycle was refuted in plan phase; the real finds were the ordinal-pairing overrun and two hidden transitive dependencies in electricalCircuit.cpp that only surfaced when the dead includes were dropped. All 15 circuit TUs gated against the reduced include set; real `make` + example-deck parses owed at next rebuild.
- [dl20260815_strumpack_refinement_stall.md](dl20260815_strumpack_refinement_stall.md) — **The quench run's hour-long hang on a CONVERGED step traced to tolerance plumbing, not the day's code (DR-76, fixed same evening on approval).** The 2026-08-13 default tightening to 1e-10 — aimed at PETSc thermal drift — was silently inherited by STRUMPACK as its iterative-refinement target; on the iterate where Newton has converged the RHS is numerically zero, no relative criterion is meetable, and refinement ground toward its library cap of 5000 (gdb: all ranks in IterativeRefinementMPI). Same disease as DR-72: a relative criterion on a numerically zero quantity, firing precisely on the best iterates. Fix splits the shared number by explicitness: SolverParameters grew a have-flag (parser + setter + copy ctor + synchronize, payload 7→8), strumpacktools applies set_rel_tol only when stated, set_maxit(50) bounds the net — deliberately second line of defense, since the wrapper escalates non-SUCCESS and capping alone would have turned the stall into an abort on a perfect solve. Deck bridge for the running binary: explicit 1e-8 in linear magnetic. Input contract updated in-session. By-catch: the 2-vs-4-thread A/B residual differences shown to be reduction-order roundoff (iterate 1 bitwise-identical, divergence growing from iterate 2), not the day's changes.
- [dl20260815_tapestack3d_j_asymmetry.md](dl20260815_tapestack3d_j_asymmetry.md) — **Overnight read-only three-AI investigation of the tapestack3d outer-copper |J| asymmetry: the "two outer coppers should look alike" premise is refuted (the rho-less magnesia buffer becomes a `DomainType::Buffer` φ-region and ghost facets skip it, so each tape is two conducting halves joined only at the coated edges), and the jagged face is a standing, slowly growing solution mode in the YBCO-side layers — inter-save correlation 0.996–0.998 — concentrated on the YBCO-side interfaces (2 µm silver + copper) and absent on the buffered face. [Corrected next morning: the overnight "1e-11 tolerance never reached" claim was a dB misreading — the log prints 10·log10, so the −108…−117 dB exits ARE ≈1e-11; the mode lives in the converged solution, with the ghost-penalty/edge-resolution/Δt knobs as candidate controls. The `_Volumes` fix was then applied on Christian's approval.]** Ruled out with evidence: EF_PENTA6TS (E/C hand-verified against the analytic curl), the 2D get_top_nodes/QUAD4TS bug pattern (PENTA facet-4 is positionally aligned; to_master_orientation TRI3 checked case-by-case), Maxwell_TMatrix (inactive on linear meshes), postprocessor layer/side handling, and all MPI mechanisms (exodus `ElementOwner` shows every tape single-rank-owned). By-catch: a REAL `_Volumes` SPR-weight misindexing bug (`cl_FEM_Kernel.cpp:944-950`, owned-only counter vs facet-ordered areas) proven inert on this mesh by reconstructing the corrupted lookup from the run's own ownership data — two-line fix queued; plus the known interface-node SPR leak confirmed painting YBCO's 2.5e10 onto the buffer faces (inner planes unusable by design, outer planes unaffected). Report, tape×plane noise matrix, and the morning verification plan (serial rerun, coating-off, tolerance experiment, BELFEM_PROBE_FUSED_ROWS spot-check): `todo/tapestack3d_J_asymmetry.md`. No edits — read-only mandate held.
- [dl20260815_bdf_warmstart.md](dl20260815_bdf_warmstart.md) — **The memdump learns the BDF integrator state: a warm restart now resumes at its earned order instead of re-anchoring at BDF1 — the restart cliff that detonated the evening's resume ( Newton promotion on a wrong-order tangent, ~40 min lost ) is gone for dumps written by the next binary.** The state is three members per equation, and the third is the trap both reviewers caught independently: the IWG's own mDeltaTime holds the last COMPLETED step ( the controller assigns the next one only after the shift; a fresh object holds 1.0 ), distinct from the dumped next-step delta_time — omitting it injects a fictitious one-second step at the first shift. Closed-gate validation, named incomplete-history errors, loud cold-start announcements at every info level, bitwise round-trip test against an uninterrupted twin that provably fails on the pre-P0 design. Four docs taught the old contract and were rewritten. By-catch DR-74: segregated hphiTrun loads dumps but never writes one.
- [dl20260815_bc_globals.md](dl20260815_bc_globals.md) — **BC globals return to the mesh: one global variable per value-imposing boundary condition, named from the deck, written each timestep — the ParaView applied-current display the legacy code had.** The campaign's plan review found the globals machinery itself broken twice over (auto-ID map keyed under 0, replication args swapped id/value — both dead only because nothing created globals), and the design was reshaped twice by audit: creation moved off the workers' factory-ctor stub mesh onto dofmgr()->mesh(), the restart story inverted (factories create BEFORE the dump loads), and the naming made injective via a two-pass section-ordinal scheme after Grok showed a legal two-block deck would abort. Circuit terminal pairs publish via fix() with rank 0 authoritative; bearing publishes nothing. Pre-existing thermal-factory silent default hardened to an error. Reviewed not verified; executable gate = np>=2 ParaView check + warm-restart round-trip on the next rebuild.
- [dl20260815_dofcount_jury.md](dl20260815_dofcount_jury.md) — **Three-juror blind sweep of a suspected rank-dependent dof count — RETRACTED same day: the premise was false.** The 8-proc figure was a combined magnetic+thermal count (2,465,712 + 979,808 = 3,445,520 exactly); there are no phantom dofs and DR-73 is struck. Kept for the lesson: the false number entered via the brief's 'established facts — do not re-litigate' section from a secondhand reading of an overwritten log, and the jurors' unanimous static conclusion that the code could not produce the phenomenon should have triggered re-examination of the premise, not a mechanism hunt. Surviving by-catch: the printed count IS the SpMatrix dimension; 4-proc magnetic count proven exact via memdump closure; uninitialized ctor member; dead PerProc tables; no MPI dof-invariance test; the dof report does not name its kernel — the enabling ambiguity.
- [dl20260814_doxygen_warning_fixes.md](dl20260814_doxygen_warning_fixes.md) — **Doxygen warning log 20 → 0, locked with a zero baseline**: 14 source/doc files fixed at the source plus `cmake/report_doxygen_warnings.cmake`; no runtime behavior change. *(Index entry backfilled 2026-08-20; see INC-538 in `doc/lessons_learned_evidence.md`.)*
- [dl20260814_bezier_cp_copper_audit.md](dl20260814_bezier_cp_copper_audit.md) — **Audit of the new `Metal::create_cp( Px, Py, Qx, Qy )` Bezier construction: the fit is excellent (sub-percent against Touloukian from 30 K to 1000 K, C1 at all four breakpoints, monotone, nine polynomials and eight switch temperatures replaced by four control-point vectors) and every defect was in the plumbing around it.** Three P0s fixed: `Copper::cp_custom` plus three shadowing members survived in `cl_Material_Copper.hpp`, so dispatch went to the old override reading a zero-length `mCpPolys` while `dcpdT_custom` correctly read the new data (cp and its derivative from two different definitions); `beta`/`gamma`/`debye0K` were never set for Copper at all, because the old `create_cp` *produced* them and the new one *consumes* them (now in `set_constants()` after `M`, since the `beta` write derives theta0 = 343.75 K from `R`); and `real y3 = Qy( 3 )` missed the `exp()` its low-side twin has, making the linear extrapolation above `T3` return 6.26 instead of 522 J/(kg K) — not academic, `create_spline` samples to 1360 K while `T3 = 1357.0 K`, so the last spline knot landed in that branch. P1: the connection-point assert compared `Py(3) == Qy(3)` instead of `Qy(0)` and therefore aborted the debug build on data that was consistent. P2: two of the three `mCpBezierLow == nullptr` checks were meant to be `mCpBezierHigh`; basis length and `Px`/`Qx` monotonicity asserted. **Christian then replaced the C1 cubic-in-`T` transition with a C2 fifth-order beam poly built entirely in log-log space, which retires the `T0 + 5` clamp and the `T1 += 1.0` monotonicity walk** (measured: the old version left a 25% curvature jump at `T0`, 0.0310 → 0.0386; the new one matches to FD precision, and the curve moves by at most 0.004 J/(kg K), so it buys a smooth `dcp/dT` for Newton rather than a better fit). Four defects in that rewrite, all in the same log-space/linear-`T` seam as the originals: an undefined `Tx`; `dydx3` used as a linear slope when it is `d(ln cp)/d(ln T)` (0.567 vs the true 0.218, factor 2.6, value at `T3` right either way); `dcpdT_custom`'s last branch still applying `exp(...)/T` to a polynomial already linear in `T`; and `T0 < T1` no longer guaranteed once the clamp is gone, now a `BELFEM_ERROR`. **Porting Silver and Indium then exposed the real prize: `Bezier::xi_by_x` bisects on `[-1.25, 1.25]`, i.e. 12.5% outside the curve's domain, where a lopsided control polygon makes the cubic fold back and gives the bisection a SECOND root — which it silently returns.** Silver's cp got an 18% jump at `T3` and a negative extrapolation slope, Indium +119% at the bottom of its Bezier range; both fits were fine. Audited all 19 Bezier bases in the tree: **every one is monotone on `[-1,1]`, five are not on `[-1.25,1.25]`** (Silver cp high, spans 0.766/1.880/**0.038**; Indium cp low, **0.009**/0.308/0.136; Iron reduced magnetization; Lead/Magnesia/WhiteTin alpha), so the bracket is the whole defect. Fixed to search `[-1,1]` with saturation outside — in-domain roots unchanged and found faster; the only behaviour change in the tree is the thermal-expansion curves at the one spline knot past `T_max` (Copper 2.0 K, Silver 0.92 K, alpha shifts 1.3e-4 relative, above melting). Copper never triggered it because all six of its spans are balanced, which is why the first audit pass came back clean. All three materials now continuous at every breakpoint, monotone densely and on the knots, no quintic overshoot. Indium's header also still carried the Copper leftovers (plus a dead `Cell<Bezier*> mCpBeziers` and its destructor loop, and a stray `#` null directive). **By-catch: `Bezier::xi_by_y` had a wrong midpoint (`0.5*(tXi1 - tXi0)`) and a swapped bracket update — replication shows it never converged for any sampled point, i.e. a guaranteed abort, not a silent inaccuracy.** **Lead and White Tin ported next; the switch-at-the-breakpoint objection answered with numbers: the defect is the solver bracket, not the query — on the Silver high curve the extrapolated cubic stops at `xi = 1.020` and plunges, giving every in-domain T in [1132, 1223] K a second root and a same-sign "bracket" (`f(-1.25) = -2.91`, `f(+1.25) = -0.078`); sharper still, `create_cp` itself anchors the quintic and the extrapolation at the exact domain endpoints, the most fold-vulnerable queries of all. `[-1,1]` is provably safe (Bernstein-positive derivative + increasing control abscissae = strictly monotone x), so the fix is exact.** Lead/WT physics all consistent: constants match literature (Pb gamma 3.05 mJ/mol K², theta0 104.1; Sn per O'Neil, theta0 199.2), quintic log-log slopes peak at 3.64/4.45 — the super-Debye signature of the Pb/Sn soft-phonon theta-dips landing exactly in the transition window; non-monotone Qy control polygons smooth out (min slope +0.07/+0.13, cp never dips); extrapolation shares 1.3%/0.0%; metal superconductivity ruled out of scope by design (HTS database — Cu/Ag/Pb/Sn/In are stabilizer/solder materials, only YBCO carries jc and the power law). The earlier Silver 20%-above-cubic flag is withdrawn — the cp(T1)/cubic ratios (Cu 1.05, In 1.04, Ag 1.20, Pb 1.84, Sn 2.45) are each metal's theta-dip strength in the physically expected order. WT still carried the shadow `mCpPolys`/`mTCpSwitch` + stale `{1,20}` write (removed); Lead's `create_debye` -> `Metal::cp_custom(12 K)` handoff verified sound. Remaining report-only item: Indium's last control point at 272.7 K leaves 36.5% of its range to linear extrapolation. All verified numerically in replication. **Compile blocker then resolved: a mechanical definitions-vs-declarations diff found SIX missing DR-07 T-leg declarations, not one (`djc_eval_dT`, `dn_eval_dT`, both overloads of `drho_powerlaw_dT` and `drho_piecewise_dT`) — all declared with doc comments distilled from the design block; all ten materials TUs + `cl_Bezier.cpp` now pass `g++ -fsyntax-only` with the build tree's flags.** Shadow sweep concluded: five ported metals clean; Magnesia legitimately owns its members (SplineLookupTable base); YBCO/Hastelloy still shadow but theirs are consumed by their own `cp_custom` — and that exposed a latent regression: they inherit Metal's new `dcpdT_custom` with empty members (not live — only `set_custom(cp)` routes there, which only Copper/Silver call — but one call away from a bounds assert); `Metal::dcpdT_custom` now null-guards on `mCpBezierLow` and falls back to the base-class finite difference. **Iron and Nickel ported (the ferromagnets): both fits stop below their Curie peaks by construction (Ni T_max=600 < Tc 631; Fe T_max=860 < Tc 1043, pre-existing resistivity cap), T3 > T_max so the linear tail is unreachable, and the critical approach is captured (end dcp/dT ~0.72-0.78 vs ~0.45 at 300 K, still rising); spot checks land on CRC/JANAF (Ni 300 K −0.3%, Fe 300 K +0.1%, Fe 800 K −0.5%), theta0 Ni 456 derived / Fe 470 explicit, both gammas literature-consistent.** Iron fixes: beta/gamma were double-set (`create_debye` overwrote the new Touloukian pair with its older gamma, 1.7% off, shifting the quintic anchor zone 1.3-1.6% below 12 K — `create_debye` now sets only debye0K) and the `mTDebyeSwitch.print("T")` probe removed. Nickel `Tcurie=672` traced (sole consumer: `compute_mred` → `rho_mag`'s magnon/spin-disorder blend), first read as the Weiss temperature and split into a new `Tweiss` property — **then Christian's challenge (sources quote ~654 K) triggered the decisive check: the reduced-magnetization Bezier is calibrated to the TRUE Tc (m(600 K): 631-scaling 0.47 vs measured ~0.45-0.50; 672-scaling 0.61), so 672 de-calibrated its own fit and was never pinnable as Weiss (reported values scatter 650-675 K); `Tweiss` removed again, numbering restored, `Tcurie=631` with the evidence in the comment.** Iron's 1043 verified genuine in both roles throughout; enum-renumber audit (no integer casts / serialization / string maps; .so ABI the one caveat) recorded for the next time; by-catch: `A_spin_disorder=3e-8` sits below its own comment's 80-120 nΩ·m range; `Iron::create_alpha()` exists but is never called from the ctor (alpha not enabled for Iron; Nickel's port wired its ctor call, Iron's looks like the same step not yet taken). **Pre-commit review of the Fe/Ni transport additions (`create_debye_and_rho`, Hust lambda sets): Iron verifies end to end — theta(295)=400.0, rho anchoring reproduces 9.8e-8 exactly and independently re-derives the old commented-out `rho_i_ref` to five digits (7.68384e-8 vs 7.68395e-8), lambda 83.2 at 300 K vs ~80 — but Nickel's lambda coefficient set is refuted through the identical validated code path: 48.5 W/(m K) at 300 K vs the handbook 90.7, low 1.5-2.6x across 100-600 K, with a high-T plateau (`T^0.181/136.4`) that analytically cannot reach the Touloukian values its own comment cites — flagged for refit, not touched.** Two structural fixes applied: `rho_0` was never set for either metal and the new `set_custom(rho)` wiring made `Ferromagnetic::rho_custom` + `Metal::lambda_custom` live on it → NaN transport for default-constructed Fe/Ni (old Iron was safe only because its `create_rho` never registered rho); both now set `rho_0 = 0` per the Indium/Lead/WhiteTin pattern. And Nickel called `set_RRR` before `rho_i_ref` existed — the regula falsi exits silently on NaN (the old "will cause an error" comment is wrong twice over), leaving `rho_0 = NaN` undiagnosed; the RRR block moved after `create_debye_and_rho`. Report-only: Ni `T_max` 600→631 makes the cp linear tail reachable (knots 604-632, smooths over the unfitted lambda-anomaly peak) and saturates alpha past its Bezier's 600 K end; `set_RRR`'s RRR definition excludes `rho_mag` (~25% vs experimental RRR for Ni). Owed: build + runs, Ni lambda refit.
- [dl20260814_doc_sweep_fixes_and_release_packaging.md](dl20260814_doc_sweep_fixes_and_release_packaging.md) — **The sweep's plan executed under Christian's rulings (`USE_TEST=ON` for release; FVM → `./nonfree`): every false documentation claim fixed against the verified source, the release front door built, and the tree's doxygen run brought to a genuine 0 warnings.** Maxwell guides lose their XML-input fiction and five invented API families for the real `input.conf`/`hphirun` patterns; four fixed-bug banners become verified-historical records (IWG "Issue 4" — hardcoded cp/λ in the standalone heat IWG — confirmed still open and now says so); mesh/sparse/comm/iwg/examples tables corrected; stale theory-doc line citations became searchable anchors (scanner: 28 broken → 1 intentional). New: rewritten `README.md`, `CITATION.cff`, `doc/getting_started.md` first in the mainpage table, `PROJECT_NUMBER` from the CMake version. `check_doc_claims.py` USE_TEST probe made bidirectional (34/34 hold). **Bycatch: four pre-existing `[README.md](README.md)` doxygen warnings that resolve against the CWD — present at HEAD, invisible to the morning gate — fixed with explicit `@ref` targets.** Gates: claims 34/34, nav clean (23 modules/85 pages), drift clean, full doxygen 0 warnings. Owed: real `make doc` + `make check` (Christian).
- [dl20260814_doxygen_documentation_sweep.md](dl20260814_doxygen_documentation_sweep.md) — **Jury round on the whole doxygen tree: the contract layer is current and strong, the module usage guides are the weak half — and the weakness is superseded prose, not the citation rot the sweep set out to measure. The Maxwell guides still teach an XML input file while `hphirun.cpp:57` takes `input.conf`.** Verified P1s: XML input docs, two files carrying "CONFIRMED BUG" banners for code that now does exactly what they prescribe as the fix, `HDF5Reader`/`HDF5Writer` classes that do not exist (live type `BfmFile`), `-DBELFEM_STRUMPACK` instead of `USE_STRUMPACK`, and an examples run-book naming `.geo` files absent from the tree (breaks the documented first run). Measured: 28/507 citations broken (concentrated in devlog-style history embedded in usage guides) and **223/327 classes + 8206 members undocumented** — the 0-warning baseline measures malformed docs only (`WARN_IF_UNDOCUMENTED=NO` + `EXTRACT_ALL=YES`) and must not be read as coverage. Packaging is the other half: README is a 16-line copyright notice, `examples/` is outside doxygen `INPUT`, no `CITATION.cff`, version 0.9.0, zero `install()` rules. **Claude's C2 partially refuted — citation health is uncorrelated with prose health**, so a citation checker would never have caught the XML defect. **Grok's USE_TEST headline refuted on verification**: the ON is an uncommitted working-tree edit, HEAD is OFF, docs match the committed tree; what survives is that `check_doc_claims.py:224-226` is one-way and goes silent exactly when the docs would go wrong. Round-shape defect owned: `cross_review.sh` framed the charter file as the review subject, so Codex audited the brief instead of the docs. No fixes applied.
- [dl20260814_input_reference_template_merge_assessment.md](dl20260814_input_reference_template_merge_assessment.md) — **Three-AI assessment of Gregory's `BELFEM_Templates` README against the input contract docs: no new key/value/enum anywhere (3/3 walks), but one genuine undocumented contract fact — unlabeled same-type sub-sections are ALL live via the ordered `mData` walk, so each repeated `current { }` is its own BC while the name map silently returns only the last duplicate.** Merge applied on approval: the two-store duplicate-section contract into reference §1/§9 + a schema `grammar.duplicate_sections` field, plus bearing rationale, bracket-group intent, the 2-D reuse rule, and the §6 labeled-layers cross-link; Codex prose pass applied; gates green (`belfem-conf drift` 125/125 clean, `check` 6/6 decks ok). Errata for Gregory written to `tmp/BELFEM_Templates/README_errata.md`. Confirmed README errors that must NOT be merged (rewrite-don't-paste rule — every long snippet carries ≥1): units/library/builtin/homology lists all under-report the legal sets, curves `@` is sideset∩sideset not edge@sideset, `coil{material}` and topology `label :` keys are inert, "homology always required" is false, and the range-notation paragraph re-teaches the missing-bracket failure mode. Scope per Christian: example decks are Gregory's to merge; only the two doc files here. Parser facts static-trace reviewed; the doc edits themselves carry the executable tooling gates above. — **All 20 `make doc` warnings eliminated at the source and the baseline locked to 0 — the old 16-warning "parser limitation" baseline was fixable after all.** Nine anonymous comparator structs named (`OpVertexDegree`, …; C++17 `inline` added where the promotion to external linkage would otherwise break the link — 2-TU reproducer), four extended friends respelled `friend class`, the `coding_philosophy.md` `***` emphasis cascade and `Cell<T>::operator()` autolink reworded, duplicate `@param normJ` header lines dropped in favor of the richer cited `powerlaws.hpp` blocks, and the doxygen-unparseable pointer-to-member member aliased via `using SwapFunction` — in **both** `cl_MeshChecker.hpp` copies, after the jury round caught that the warned postproc copy is orphaned and the built kernel twin carried the identical construct. Codex approved as-is; Grok's two completeness conditions (kernel twin, baseline 16→0 in `report_doxygen_warnings.cmake`) both confirmed and incorporated. Gates: doxygen over the applied tree = 0 warnings; ~20 affected TUs compile clean under `-Wall -Werror -pedantic-errors`. Owed: the next real `make doc`. By-catch debt, not touched: the two diverged `MeshChecker` headers share one include guard and the postproc `.cpp` is built by no target.
- [dl20260814_nedelec_test_battery.md](dl20260814_nedelec_test_battery.md) — **The volume Nédélec circulation/curl battery landed (`tests/fem/test_EdgeFunctions.cpp` + `cl_EF_TestVolume` fixture, 22 tests over TRI3/TRI6/TET4/TET10) and caught defect D3 on its first full run: rows 8-9 of the TET10 `mNeta`/`mNzeta` shape-derivative tables were crossed, making the η/ζ Jacobian columns identical (singular J, nablas ~1e14).** Third instance of the naive-node-map trap, four entries, fixed same day; `mNxi`/`mD` audited clean against `tet10_lagrange.m` (whose node table was always correct). Masking lesson baked in: `link()` takes its Jacobian from evaluation point 0, and a boundary point there (η=0) hides the defect, so the battery pins an interior point at column 0. **46/46 green** (24 pre-existing Lagrange + 22 new Nédélec) via a standalone probe against the prebuilt libs with the fresh TET10 TU; negative control against the stale pre-fix library reproduces the D2 signature (2/3, −1) exactly. Formal `make check` gate + 3D run remain (defect tracker R3).
- [dl20260814_tet10_table_fix.md](dl20260814_tet10_table_fix.md) — **D2 fixed MATLAB-first: the `EF_TET10` eta/zeta table exchange traced to ONE line in the table generator (`tmp/tet10/tet10_generate.m` used the naive node map its sibling `defelement.m` avoided), fixed at the source, re-pinned, ported, and gated by parsing the edited C++ back into sympy — all four checks exact (tables, derivatives, circulation identity, curl chain).** Christian named the underlying booby trap: the EXODUS TET node map must swap eta and zeta (node 1 carries zeta) or `det J < 0`; the naive triangle-extension intuition caused both tet defects while the triangles stayed clean. Octave/SymPy and Christian's parallel MATLAB run agree (all residuals zero); `g++ -fsyntax-only` with build-tree flags clean. Still owed: rebuild + circulation regression test + 3D run (R3); TET10 stays PoC until then. Reviewed and symbolically exact, not yet run in the binary.
- [dl20260814_nedelec_tex_extraction.md](dl20260814_nedelec_tex_extraction.md) — **The pre-BELFEM LaTeX theory notes are extracted into the module docs (weak forms → `maxwell/doc`, Nédélec derivation → `interpolation/doc`) with a drift record in the notes repo, through two blind Codex+Grok audit rounds; the convention check surfaced a CONFIRMED edge-function defect.** 18 transcription errata found and fixed where extracted; drift led by the missing cohomology machinery (manual thin cuts vs the automatic thick-then-thin pipeline, deferred to Gregory's paper/thesis) and the missing hanging-node concept (λ-interfaces vs condensation). **By-catch D1 (HIGH, confirmed by hand on three sides): `EF_TET4::E()` edge 2 codes `η∇ξ − ξ∇ζ` instead of `η∇ξ − ξ∇η` — circulation 1/2 on its own edge, −1/2 on a foreign edge, E and C disagree within one class; D2 suspects the same slip in `EF_TET10`.** Report-only, tracked in `todo/nedelec_edge_function_defects.md`, awaiting ruling. All static + hand algebra; reviewed, not verified.
- [dl20260814_r2d_wont_do_ruling.md](dl20260814_r2d_wont_do_ruling.md) — **R2d converged-at-clamp diagnostic ruled won't-do (Christian, against Claude's proposal) — DR-02 is purely a run gate again.** The tapestack3d printout decided it: the production quench run never clamps, the path into clamping is already voiced by `print_thermal_stall_warning` on degraded convergence, and a converged-at-ceiling state shows as a `T_max` plateau in the exodus T field. Two observability channels exist; a third was not worth perturbing the freshly-stabilized Controller. The unconsumed `T_clamped()`/`rho_clamped()` accessors stay. No source touched.
- [dl20260813_sparse_default_tolerance_test_fix.md](dl20260813_sparse_default_tolerance_test_fix.md) — **The first-ever `USE_TEST=ON` suite run surfaced one failure, and it is not a debug/release difference: `DefaultParametersValid` pinned the default relative tolerance at 1e-8 while the source moved to 1e-10 two campaigns ago.** The drift (1e-6 → 1e-8 → 1e-10, `45264244` then `5aae789b`) was invisible because the suite had never executed — exactly the finding class the gate exists for. Test updated to pin 1e-10 with the history in a comment. Also diagnosed: `test_homology` "not built" is no wiring defect — the target and ctest entry are fully generated; the binary just needs the next `make` pass.
- [dl20260813_python_conf_tool_cross_review.md](dl20260813_python_conf_tool_cross_review.md) — **Three-AI jury audit of the new `python/belfem_conf` input-file checker (28 findings confirmed, no P0, ten P1s), then all fixes applied same-day under granted autonomy.** Required sections/keys at every level, runtime-refused enums, BC types, the circuit contract, unit-token/layer-thickness dimensions, and the C++-exact value shape are now enforced; drift scans whole files and is **clean** (124/124 anchors) after `metis nodendp` and the quoted `read_signed_sidesets` anchors were fixed in the contract docs. Regression probes caught a parser bug all three reviewers missed — comments leaking into statement keys. Gate: roundtrip 6/6 byte-for-byte, all examples ok, under python3.14 and 3.9.
- [dl20260813_dr23_cohomology_layer_tests.md](dl20260813_dr23_cohomology_layer_tests.md) — **DR-23's layer half landed: new `tests/homology/` suite drives the production cohomology sequence on a programmatic annulus — six cases (TRI3/QUAD4 × three coreduce variants), probe-run 6/6 green in 45 ms.** Asserts betti numbers, the unit-coefficient rectification contract, and the winding pairing of the generator with the inner-ring cycle. No mesh files needed. Two load-bearing fixture lessons: `aComputeConnectivities` must stay `true` (false silently empties every edge coboundary — the coreduction collapses nothing and the SNF degenerates to one generator per edge, which briefly looked like three separate framework defects), and the `reduce_complex*` calls live in the `mSuggestHomologies == false` branch production never takes — dead and doubly broken when forced (zeroed SNF; unbounded iterator walk in `pGeneralizedCombine`), filed as **DR-71**. **Stage 2 landed the same day**: `test_CohomologyPeriodic.cpp` adds two 3D periodic-bar fixtures with hand-wired periodicity matching the infinite-tape topology (sidecoating/tapestack3d reference) — solid torus with the free axial generator paired ±1 through the fold, and annulus × S¹ with a unimodular pairing matrix against the axial + encircling cycles. Slave-plane faces must be re-based to slave role (production's private `fix_face_slaves`, replicated — its absence was the first probe's segfault at `create_complex:414`), and `updatekGeneratorsFromHomology`'s 3D branch turns out to require terminal in/out pairs, so the periodic tests assert the constructor path. Suite 8/8 green in 7.2 s at probe tier; still owed: a layer-tier forced-repair case and the T9 corc run.
- [dl20260813_debt_register_currentness_sweep.md](dl20260813_debt_register_currentness_sweep.md) — **All 24 open register rows verified against the tree by six parallel read-only verifiers: DR-28 closed as already-fixed, twelve rows amended, three rows recommended for closure.** DR-28's step-1 feasible-vs-infeasible diagnostic has been the default path since `clean_spfa` landed. Three amendments change what the row means: **DR-30 is refuted as written** (T *is* rank-consistent after every thermal solve; what survives is a stale-*element* path and T's absence from the postprocessor source fields), while **DR-02** (the R2d converged-at-clamp diagnostic never landed; `T_clamped()` has zero consumers) is **worse** than recorded — the register's second pattern-break. **The sweep also produced a false positive and retracted it the same day:** it reported multi-rank `edge coating` as broken because the Distributor ships no connector record; Christian challenged it while running exactly that, and the code refutes it — the record is deliberately absent because the coating block types and wall→facet links ship by two other explicit channels, one of which carries a comment naming that design. Absence in one function was promoted to a functional gap without tracing the alternative routes; the retraction and its lesson are kept in both the row and the devlog. Recommended for Christian's ruling: DR-16 (by-design), DR-17 and DR-31 (gate-only strikes). DR-26 and DR-13 are now ready-to-execute cleanups with pinned change sets. No source edited, nothing compiled.
- [dl20260813_debt_register_scaffolding_rulings.md](dl20260813_debt_register_scaffolding_rulings.md) — **Scaffolded-code rulings: DR-52, DR-64, DR-66 struck as done (run gates retired with the rows, DR-66's suite gate surviving in its status column); DR-67 and DR-68 closed as keep-by-design; DR-53's physics position documented.** The sideset-jacobian branch and the bubble machinery are dormant capability, not dead code — documented at the `cl_IWG.hpp` flag (any future writer owes the loop an `is_active()` guard) and in the interpolation module README (wrong enrichment space for the dropped iron experiment; compile-gate before deleting). DR-53: fusing is believed mathematically more correct but converges slower for no better result — suspected overconstraint, like weakly enforcing a B·n = 0 the formulation already fulfills; both flags stay false, recorded at the flag site and in the O1 plan. Comment-only source edits, both TUs syntax-checked clean.
- [dl20260813_dr66_gesvd_workbuffer_floor.md](dl20260813_dr66_gesvd_workbuffer_floor.md) — **DR-66 ruled and fixed: `gesvd` floors its query-derived work size at the reference-LAPACK minimum (`lwork = max(query, formula)`), making the reuse path reachable under MKL; buffers now only grow.** "Trust the query as acceptance floor" was rejected as incoherent — the acceptance test must decide without a LAPACK call, so the floor can only be a formula. Backend-conditional handling was explicitly on the table and deliberately not used. Probe-verified on this tree (MKL+Blaze): raw query re-measured at lwork=7 vs reference 15 for 4×3 double, and one buffer holds 15/15/15 through a 4×3→4×3→3×2 sequence with 1e-15 reconstruction error. Tests tightened to assert length invariance; suite gate (`make check-fast`, `USE_TEST=ON`) still never run. Pattern-setting for the unified LAPACK interface.
- [dl20260813_dr69_beta_angle_ruling.md](dl20260813_dr69_beta_angle_ruling.md) — **DR-69 ruled: table-based Jc switches to the signed dot ([0,180°], the range the databases natively span); Kim-type laws stay folded; metals and bulk untouched.** The only genuine sign choice is the orientation of the tape normal itself (θ↔180°−θ), which graduates to load-bearing deck input; the metal Kohler channel is a different angle (B↔J) and Pippard's cos²/sin² blend is even about 90°, so its fold is provably lossless. Session probe: sp-ap and sst-1 share an identical 0–180°-in-1°-steps grid, and **sst-1 is also asymmetric about 90°** (raw-coefficient level, ~95% of points >20%; order-2 control points, so qualitative until spline-evaluated). Implementation stays gated on the constant-jc control run and the Robinson raw-data provenance check. No source edits.
- [dl20260813_thermal_joule_probe.md](dl20260813_thermal_joule_probe.md) — **The tapestack3d quench showed no heating after 5 s and sat 20–50 mK BELOW its 77 K start; a three-AI round plus an in-kernel `#CHECK` probe cleared the Joule path and turned the puzzle into one real physics finding and one real defect.** Verified clean: peer link, `compute_j = C·q`, layer thickness on both the magnetic (edge-function `det_J`) and thermal (`det(J_Lagrange)` over extruded nodes) sides, clamps, the `+Δt·f` residual sign, the empty BC factory, and air's absence from the thermal mesh. The probe then reconstructed the power law exactly — loaded elements sit at `ρ·J ≈ Ec`, i.e. **at** Ic, and a cold element matches `(J/jc)^(n−1)` to the printed 4.7e-37. The finding: the sp-ap table gives jc ≈ 3.3e10 A/m² at 77 K, **3.3× the 1e10 the deck comment assumed**, so the stack was never above critical and below the knee produced no heat at all; the plotted 1.44 is the signed-angle lobe (DR-69) of the same table. Both auditors also caught the deck having drifted back to `power-law` with no `density correction`. **The cooling is a separate, still-unexplained defect — new DR-70, cross-linked to the long-open DR-06 (−1.9 K dip, `dV(k) < 0` suspect, whose guard is debug-only)** — inherited through `memdump.hdf5` rather than freshly produced, with a fresh adiabatic run now in flight as the discriminator.
- [dl20260813_drhodt_tleg.md](dl20260813_drhodt_tleg.md) — **The dρ/dT T-leg lands: the quench-feedback tangent the thermal Newton was missing, implemented in a full two-phase three-AI round the night the quench study first stalled on it.** The `dbdT = 0` placeholder omitted the entire superconductor contribution (≈ 2ρ/K near 77 K); the retired `compute_drhodT_hts` reconstruction was additionally wrong twice — sign-flipped (b < 0) with a dadT weighting divergent at the flux-flow crossover, and structurally invalid for piecewise. Shipped: closed parallel factors (∂c/∂a = (c/a)²), branch-consistent piecewise derivative with a frozen-knot Bézier partial (Grok's ρ1 term, independently re-derived by Codex), 8 wrappers, per-law dispatch, and the mTClamped guard (Codex). Knot motion staged out — flux-flow remains the tangent's weak regime. Reviewed not verified; the live gate is the running tapestack3d study after rebuild.
- [dl20260812_gantry_deck_revival.md](dl20260812_gantry_deck_revival.md) — **The 2022 gantry-dipole deck ported to the current input contract: 464 hand-written cohomology cuts replaced by `generalized pellikka`, `RoxieIron` by `SAE1010`, and the midplane BC reversed from what the old deck declared.** `gantry.msh` has no physical groups, so gmsh entity tags *are* the ids; the classification was re-derived from the mesh (yoke = surface 1, air = 2:932, midplane = curves 3:15 with 14 the segment inside the yoke, and 928 vertical 2.5 mm curves = the two halves of 464 BSCCO tapes in eight 58-turn pancakes). Tape sidesets still start at 207, the same id the 2022 deck used, so entity numbering has not drifted in four years and the old file remains a usable cross-check. **The old per-tape amplitudes were cumulative cut values, not transport currents** — 20137.6 A = 58 × 347.2 A, the sequence repeating once per pancake — so the revived deck drives 347.2 A into all 464 turns, one `curve` per tape built from its two half-sidesets, relying on `get_id_groups` expanding an unbracketed `1:464` into 464 separate conditions. **Midplane:** the old deck said `antisymmetry`, which is B·n = 0 and also the default for an undeclared one-sided air face, i.e. it declared the default; but the lower half is the mirror image with the current *preserved*, giving B×n = 0, so it is now `air symmetry` / `ferro symmetry` (φ = 0 Dirichlet, legitimate because φ is odd about the plane). **The jury round refuted the reason given for that being safe, and the refutation was load-bearing:** `unflag_symmetry_sidesets` does *not* steer cuts away from the plane — it deletes the symmetry simplices from the complex, making the midplane an *attractive* landing, which is exactly where the 2022 `Circle` arcs went. What actually saves the MMF is that the cut jump is a hanging-node interpolation, not a difference of nodal values: `CutSet::create_duplicates` gives each duplicate the sources `{abstract nodes, original}` at unit weight so φ_dup = I + φ_org, `set_currents` fixes the abstract dof, and `impose_dirichlet` pins only the originals the midplane facets carry — landing on Dirichlet gives 0 / I / jump = I. Both citations re-read against source rather than accepted on report. BSCCO stays one homogenized 0.396 mm layer spanning the full cross section — placeholder `jc = 2.5e8 A/m²` (Ic = 495 A) and `n = 15` pending measured Ic(B,T). No geo change was needed: the eight `Circle` cut arcs and the mid-height gap splits survive as `Inactive` air–air interfaces. **Deck only, not run.**
- [dl20260812_cli_verbose_and_solver_passthrough.md](dl20260812_cli_verbose_and_solver_passthrough.md) — **GNU-style `-v/--verbose[=N]` now sets the logger info level in every executable, and the requested PETSc/STRUMPACK command-line pass-through turned out to already exist — the deliverable there was documentation, not code.** The parsing lives in the `belfem::Arguments` base constructor (new `Logger::set_info_level`), so `hphirun`, `hphiTrun`, `electricalCircuit`, `material`, `gastable`, and `gas` all share identical semantics; bare flag → `Everything`. `gastables` version flag flipped `-v` → `-V` per the GNU pairing. PETSc gets the full argv via `PetscInitialize` (all `-ksp_*`/`-pc_*`/`-options_file` options work, `-options_left 0` silences the noise); STRUMPACK re-reads `--sp_*` flags after `input.conf`, so the command line overrides the file. Documented in the sparse and core usage guides. Syntax-checked only — no build run.
- [dl20260812_tapestack3d_setup_and_terminal_guard.md](dl20260812_tapestack3d_setup_and_terminal_guard.md) — **Four aborts in sequence bringing up the 8-tape soldered stack, three of them case configuration and one a source robustness gap — and the last two produced the *same* message from two unrelated causes.** (1) `topology{curves}` intersected every tape with the **air** end face, whose boundary only runs along the two outer tapes; for the six inner tapes the intersection collapsed to the single tip node, giving the `1: 0 2: 0 1+2: 1` counts in `CurveFactory::sort_end_nodes`. The face that carries an inner tape's edge is the end face of the solder volume below it. (2) The `.geo` had no `Periodic Surface` statement, so gmsh meshed the two periodic planes independently — 5300 facets against 5110, **zero** coincident centroids on the air faces, and the solder faces agreeing on only half their centroids despite matching counts. (3) Eight current conditions were declared where the topology provides two generators: the tapes are thin shells and the volumes between them are solder, so the conductor is one connected slab and `H^1` of the periodic air region has rank 2 — one cut plus the free axial generator. **The terminal also had to change form, not just count:** the *key* selects the algorithm, `input curves` making `suggest_Homology` build the generator as the plain sum of the listed curves, `input terminals` taking the boundary of the terminal 2-chain — the envelope encircling tapes *and* solder. The curve form was wrong twice over here, missing the solder current and naming six curves that lie *inside* the conductor and so are not in the air complex at all. (4) The corrected `input terminals : 42:48` then reproduced (3) verbatim, because `Section::get_id_groups` flushes every id outside a bracket as its own group — **including each member of an `a:b` range** — so seven sidesets meant seven conditions, not one terminal. **Source change:** `updatekGeneratorsFromHomology` sizes `tTransInv` as `n x n` and writes one row per condition, so an over-specified deck walked off the end and surfaced as a raw Blaze bounds assertion naming neither the responsible input key nor the offending quantity; the guard that covers the related rank failure sits *after* that loop and never got the chance to fire. Added a `BELFEM_ERROR( aNumConditions <= n, … )` before the loop, naming both counts and stating the three things the user needs — one condition per galvanically connected conductor, one further generator spent on the periodic axial loop, and the bracket rule. `BELFEM_ERROR` not `BELFEM_ASSERT`: setup code, input error, must survive release. **The Codex audit then refuted the claim that this was the complete bound, and the refutation was load-bearing:** `smithForm` returns `t = rank(F)`, and the free-cut loop appends `tQ_.n_cols() - t = n - t` further rows, so the real no-overrun condition is `aNumConditions <= t` — a deck with few enough but *dependent* conditions still overran one loop further down, with the check that names linear dependence still unreachable for it. Re-derived from `fn_Smith.cpp:144-148,185-196` before acting rather than accepted on report; a second guard now sits after the SNF. The two are kept separate because "more conditions than the topology carries" and "the conditions are not independent" have different remedies. The existing rank check stays and is dead under neither guard. **Open at medium confidence:** the bulk branch also folds every `topology{curves}` curve whose edges the terminal sideset flags into the generator, i.e. all 16 tape curves on top of the envelope; whether that is the intended handling of a thin shell piercing a bulk terminal or a double count turns on sign conventions in `Chain::getBoundary()` and `Segment::edge_direction()` that were not resolved statically — the symptom would be a factor on the imposed current, not a crash.
- [dl20260812_material_data_search_path.md](dl20260812_material_data_search_path.md) — **A material data file named in an `input.conf` is now looked for in `$BELFEM_DATA/material` when it is not in the run directory**, closing the gap that forced every run directory to carry its own copy of `sp-ap.hdf5` and `bhdata.hdf5` or spell a machine-specific absolute path. The gas tables had solved this years ago through `gastables::data_path()`; the materials module handed the input string straight to `HDF5` or `dlopen`. New `material::data_file()` tries the path as written, then the same relative path below `$BELFEM_DATA/material`, then **the file name alone** there — the third step is what lets `bhfile : MatData/bhdata.hdf5 ;` resolve in a run directory with no `MatData`, and it is why the undulator and `examples/sidecoating` decks now run unedited. The run directory deliberately wins, so a local copy is never made inert; an unresolved path is returned unchanged rather than raised, because the opener already names it and because two of the four call sites are `dlopen`, where a hard error would have removed the working `$LD_LIBRARY_PATH` search. All four input keys route through it (`bhfile`, HTS `file`, `custom { file }`, `defect { file }`), which also fixed the `MaterialFactory` constructor bypassing its own `create_bh_curve()`. The shared data directories were renamed with it: `fluidprop` → `fluid`, `matprop` → `material`, absorbing the repository's old `database/` HDF5 files. **Reviewed, not verified:** syntax-only compiles plus a standalone probe exercising all five resolution outcomes from two working directories; no solver run, and the two `dlopen` sites are untested.
- [dl20260812_alloy_rrr_input_path.md](dl20260812_alloy_rrr_input_path.md) — **A builtin alloy declared with an `RRR` key could never be constructed:** `MaterialFactory` applied RRR *after* construction via `set_RRR()`, but `Alloy` derives `rho_0` from RRR inside its constructor and — deriving from `SplineLookupTable`, not `Metal` — has no `set_RRR()` at all, so the pure-metal path silently did not generalise. Three further defects fell out of the same trace: a `!= BELFEM_QUIET_NAN` guard that is always true, which stored a NaN RRR and turned the intended "requires RRR > 1" error into a debug-only assertion (making debug and release fail differently on the same input), and a case-sensitive `"RRR"` compare that rejected the lowercase in-formula spelling its own detection accepted. RRR is now forwarded to `create_material()`, which also halves the rho-database builds for pure metals.
- [dl20260812_ferroair_interface_removal.md](dl20260812_ferroair_interface_removal.md) - **There is no ferro-air interface weak form, in 2d or in 3d — the interface nodes are duplicated for visualization only, and the kernel that appeared to implement one was a debug stub calling `exit( 0 )`.** Tracing D3 (`InterfaceCondFerro` admitted to the magnetic equation with no `link_to_group` case) surfaced the larger finding: `phi_phi_2d` printed its own name, dumped the dofs and exited — with no MPI finalize and **exit code 0, which a driver script reads as success** — while everything below it was the bubble-enrichment body behind `#ifdef BELFEM_ENRICH_PHIA`, a macro defined nowhere in the tree; `phi_phi_3d` was `"not implemented"`. Residue of the dropped experiment to enrich the phi elements of the iron, whose enrichment space was the wrong one (bubble rather than hierarchical — Dular et al. 2021); `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp` already said so in a comment, and `mUseEnrichment` holds its `false` initializer with no writer. **Unreachability established rather than assumed:** under h-phi the sideset is `Inactive` before groups are built and both live sideset loops skip inactive groups; under any other formulation the `else` backstop raised first; and the one unguarded `link_to_group( tSideSet )` sits behind `compute_jacobian_on_sideset()`, whose flag is `false` at `cl_IWG.hpp:103` **with no writer anywhere in the tree** — so the entire sideset branch of `DofManager::compute_jacobian` is dead for every IWG in the framework. The `exit( 0 )` could not fire today; it was one bypassed deactivation away. **Fixed:** the `InterfaceFerroAir` case collapses to `BELFEM_ERROR( "must be disabled!" )` matching `InterfaceCondAir` (the `if HPhi / else` went with it — the `else` was that morning's R6 backstop, and the branch it guarded was the dead half); `mt_maxwell_phi_phi.{cpp,hpp}` deleted with its CMake entry, no `phi_phi` reference left in `src/`; `InterfaceCondFerro` gains the same error case; and **R9/D3 closed** by setting `InterfaceCondFerro` `Inactive` after its hanging-edge condensation, mirroring `InterfaceFerroAir`. **Deactivate, not de-admit** — the first proposal was to drop it from the whitelist, but the tree changed the recommendation: the whitelist runs *before* the condensation pass and the fem groups take the **live** mesh type, which is exactly how `InterfaceFerroAir` and `ThinShell` are already made dormant; following the existing idiom removes any dependence on admission ordering. `Maxwell_FieldList` needs no change — it has cases for both snapshots. **Reviewed, not verified:** `-fsyntax-only` on both TUs under the build tree's own flags, no build or run; the gate is a deck with a conductor block touching ferro, which could not run at all before this. The bubble machinery itself (14 files plus four call sites on a permanently-false flag) is left standing — retire it, or keep it as scaffolding for a future hierarchical enrichment.
- [dl20260812_ferro_undulator_d1_fix.md](dl20260812_ferro_undulator_d1_fix.md) - **The ferro-undulator day, end to end: D1 fixed with the audited short-circuit (R1), the `InterfaceFerroAir` fall-through closed (R6), a same-day jury round confirming both edits — and then Gregory's answer refuting the entire D2 reconstruction: his error was `fn_check_facet_orientation.hpp:78`, and his model has no coils.** The in-house trace had already proven the three "must be disabled!" errors unreachable (whitelist never admitted the coil trio in any commit) and surfaced **D3** (`InterfaceCondFerro` whitelisted, dof-carrying, fully active, but with NO `link_to_group` case → "Not implemented" abort for any conductor touching iron). The real defect **D4**: `check_facet_orientation` implements only the 3D shared-edge test — two distinct 2D line facets can never share a node *pair* — so `fix_facet_masters`' BFS (whose 2D adjacency is node-based and crosses sideset chains at point contacts, Gregory's "blocks that were not even neighbors") aborts on any 2D cohomology model where a same-type facet touches a cross-type seed: his air|air HTS tape lines meeting the ferro-air pole interfaces. His comment-out makes the BFS flip every reached facet, which preserves the element-ID-based relative orientation — tape consistency inherited from gmsh numbering, not established; it-works-by-accident, mesh-dependent. **R10 applied on Christian's go-ahead and jury-audited the same evening (round 3): both auditors accept, no P0** — the dimension-aware check keeps the ≥3-node shared-edge loop and gives two-node facets the chain rule on `original()->id()`, with all three load-bearing properties (truth table incl. coincident twins, node order = master traversal reversed by `flip()`, originals as comparison space) confirmed independently three ways. The round's catch, raised by both auditors: a seed at an *interior* node of a same-type chain tears it in the BFS — endpoint contacts (Gregory's case) are safe; reworded O4, latent P1, pre-existing. R12 flags the 3D loop's raw-id comparison as potential duplicate-blindness. **The R8 run then cleared that abort and hit the next one — D5, "No surface found on tape/boundary" — turning the auditors' warning live within hours:** the 2D cut pipeline silently requires a *uniform master side per tape sideset*, an invariant nothing establishes and that only gmsh's per-surface element numbering ever supplied; R10's selective flips break it, both sides then receive duplicate nodes, and the terminal-curve search finds no original-node facet. **This also corrects the earlier verdict on Gregory's comment-out: in 2D it was harmless** — flip-all is a uniform reversal that *preserves* the invariant, which is exactly why his run passed where a pairwise-correct rule failed; only selective flips destroy uniformity. Fixed as R13 (Christian's option B): a four-site `tPropagate = (dim == 3)` guard disabling same-type BFS propagation in 2D, where no consumer ever benefited from it and no model ever ran it successfully; cross-type seed normalization untouched, 3D bit-identical, one connectivity build saved. O5 left open: make the uniform-master invariant explicit in CutFactory so the next violation is a one-line diagnostic instead of a cryptic downstream assert.
