All four fixes reviewed-not-verified (syntax gate only); R8 remains the single gate. `MaxwellData`'s constructor called `constant_property( mu )` unconditionally, whose `BELFEM_ASSERT( is_constant )` fires for every material with a B-H curve (loading the curve writes `BELFEM_QUIET_NAN` into the constant slot), so every ferro run died in a debug build while release was accidentally correct (`NaN == mu0` is false). The flag is now `tIsConstantMu && …` — always initialized, assert never violated, release value unchanged on every path. Explicitly *not* Gregory's variant, which leaves the flag uninitialized on exactly the ferro path and is read in the bulk branch at `:297`, i.e. UB that can silently turn the iron yoke into vacuum; telling him (R2) is still owed and matters more than the code change. Second edit: `link_to_group`'s `InterfaceFerroAir` case assigned `mFunMKF` only under HPhi with no `else`, inheriting the previously linked group's kernel for any other formulation — now an `else` + `BELFEM_ERROR`, matching the HPhi-only solving coverage in `maxwell_usage_guide.md` and the admission-tier error policy. **Reviewed, not verified:** both files pass `g++ -fsyntax-only` under the build tree's own flags; no build or benchmark run — the undulator debug-build gate (R8) is open, as are D2's admission route (R3–R5, blocked on Gregory's error message and deck) and O1–O3. - **The multi-AI method written up as an experience report for an external reader: `doc/ai_workflow_best_practices.md`.** Third of three — `METHODOLOGY.md` maps our terms onto published vocabulary, the protocol is the normative rule set, this one pairs each practice with what changed *after* adoption and what it costs. Selected from the 2026-03 → 2026-08 corpus (248 entries) with introduction dates taken from git rather than prose: protocol 03-16, `ask_codex.sh` 06-11, per-topic sharded exchange 06-18, `ask_grok.sh` 07-23, cross-review tooling + evidence ladder 08-05, campaign pages + debt register + falsification battery 08-06. Two negative results are given equal prominence: three-model unanimity has repeatedly been refuted by a source read, and mechanical claim-checking cannot supply a premise nobody wrote down. Documentation only.
- [dl20260811_workflow_best_practices_doc.md](dl20260811_workflow_best_practices_doc.md) — **`doc/ai_workflow_best_practices.md` written**: the experience report on the multi-AI method for an external reader — dated facts rated high, the selection of "what worked" flagged as judgement; documentation only, nothing compiled or run. *(Backfilled 2026-08-20; INC-538.)*
- [dl20260811_mumps_escalation_and_edge_synch.md](dl20260811_mumps_escalation_and_edge_synch.md) - **Two silent wrong-answer paths closed in source: MUMPS factorising a matrix it had quietly dropped entries from, and the parallel postprocessor synching quadratic edge fields at the wrong stride.** **DR-45(b):** `INFO(1) = +1` means MUMPS found `IRN`/`JCN` indices outside the matrix, **dropped those entries** and factorised anyway — nothing downstream can notice, because the residual is measured against the assembled operator. Christian's read was that it should be fatal and I agreed *before* any auditor ran, which is exactly why a blind `--jury` round was run instead of a straight implementation; the pre-registration named the two findings that would have reversed it and declared them wanted. Both auditors returned **escalate**, neither able to construct a benign producer. The evidence is structural, not the absence of a grep hit: fixed dofs are not discarded through a sentinel index but live in a separate matrix (`mDirichletMatrix` free×fixed, `mSystemMatrix` free×free, `cl_FEM_DofMgr_SolverData.cpp:350-405`), and with `ICNTL(5)=0`/`ICNTL(18)=0` and `irn`/`jcn`/`A` bound only on the master (`mumpstools.f90:321,340-348`) `+1` is **host-local by construction**. Both `solve()` overloads now route positive `INFO(1)` through a new private `MUMPS::check_warnings()`; `free()` stays report-only because it is teardown after `JOB=-2`, where aborting masks the original failure. **The scope guard is the real hazard and both auditors raised it independently:** the check tests the **bit** (`mInfo(0) & 1`), never `> 0` — MUMPS sums warning flags and `+8` (refinement not converged) is *expected* on ill-conditioned HTS systems, so a `> 0` test would have reproduced that morning's `61bbfa11` failure, where an always-active guard on a normal distributed state aborted every parallel run. `BELFEM_ERROR` not `BELFEM_ASSERT` (the latter vanishes under `NDEBUG`, leaving release quietly solving the wrong matrix); never `soft_fail()`, since a retry re-factorises the same wrong matrix. **The round corrected three things in my own work:** a *premise refuted* — `InfoLevel::Minimal` does not hide the message, it is the tier most likely to print (`Minimal=1`, `Default=2`, gate `aInfoLevel <= mInfoLevel`), so an absorbed `+1` was **visible but unread**, not silent; an *auditor disagreement settled by source* — Codex wanted an `MPI_Allreduce` rank-uniform verdict citing STRUMPACK, but that precedent is the **soft-fail** path which returns into further collectives, whereas a hard error terminates via `MPI_Abort` and needs no reduction; and a *defect shipped hours earlier* — `5a2ddf81` claimed to fix "both `INFO` reads" when there are **three** sites (`solve` is overloaded) and the Matrix-RHS one was still rank-0 gated, raised by Grok, confirmed by direct read, closed in the same commit. **DR-31**, sitting at `unverified` with "needs a layout check before calling it a bug": the check was done and **the suspicion held**. `DofManager::create_fields` sizes an edge field as `edge_multiplicity() * number_of_edges()` and `IWG_Maxwell` sets that to **2** at element order 2, storing edge *i*'s dofs at `2i`/`2i+1` — how `Calculator::nedelec_data_quadratic_2d/3d` reads them back — but `synch_source_field` copied **one** value per index, and the index lists hold *entity* indices, not dof positions. So it read slot *i* out of a `2N` array: the second dof of every edge never crossed the wire and the value that did belonged to a different edge; `face_h` identical. **No crash** (indices stay in bounds) — silent wrong H/B/J on aura entities near partition boundaries, in a run that looks converged. **Not opt-in:** `mElementOrder = mMesh->max_element_order()` with the flag `== 2`, so an order-2 mesh on >1 rank is enough. Stride now recovered from the field's own length rather than assumed, derived from the field rather than queried from the IWG so the two cannot drift; P2 → P1, blocking-1.0 left to Christian since it turns on whether order-2 meshes are in release scope. **The cross-cutting result:** three slips in one day are one defect class — stale `file:line` anchors, "DR-24" naming an incident that has no register row, and a prose enumeration naming two of three `INFO` sites. Each pointer was correct when written and never re-followed; none is a reasoning error, and the DR-24 mislabel survived a jury round, a row edit and a second reader. **Both fixes are reviewed, not verified** — clean under `-DNDEBUG` and `-DDEBUG` with `-Werror`, never executed; DR-31's serial path never calls the changed function, so only a ≥2-rank order-2 run exercises it. Commits `78ea534d`, `0671b29a`.
- [dl20260811_ndebug_error_path_tests.md](dl20260811_ndebug_error_path_tests.md) - **Five of thirteen test binaries aborted under `USE_DEBUG=OFF`, and the obvious reading — a flaky or false-positive test — was wrong in exactly the way that costs coverage: `MPI_Abort` kills the process, so 28 of `test_ode`'s 43 tests and 44 of `test_mesh`'s 46 never ran, and a truncated suite reports as a single failure.** `BELFEM_ERROR`'s *check* is compiled into every build; its *reaction* was not — both macros funnel through the `assert::error` template, which threw under `DEBUG` and called `error_abort()` → `MPI_Abort` otherwise (`assert.hpp:98-102`). So `EXPECT_THROW` on a `BELFEM_ERROR` was a debug-only construct, and 17 tests across five binaries did exactly that. **The tests were correct by their documentation; the documentation was wrong** — `tests/doc/tests_12_ode.md` §12 and `tests/doc/tests_11_io.md` §4 both inferred "always active" ⇒ "always throws" and instructed writers *not* to guard. `doc/coding_philosophy.md:625-629` had the mechanism right all along, which is why this survived: the authoritative document agreed with reality and the derived one did not. A quieter second defect: `assert::error` is a template instantiated in the *calling* TU, so the throw/abort choice is fixed with the **library's** flags — `-DDEBUG` on a test target would not have restored throwing for `cl_BDF.cpp:27`, and a `#ifndef NDEBUG` guard in a test file reads the wrong TU. **Five options went to a blind three-AI jury; all three converged on a runtime switch**, rejecting always-throw (a throw escaping `main` terminates one rank while peers block in a collective, and `coding_philosophy.md:747` already says not to rely on `throw` in library code), death tests (they `fork()` after `MPI_Init`), and guarding-out-of-release (rejected by the requirement — release is what ships). **Unanimity was not the evidence**: agreement is the weakest rung and MPI process-death is a safety boundary, so the choice was ratified by Christian. **The one auditor disagreement was load-bearing and changed the design.** The pre-registered proposal defaulted the flag to `false`; Codex refuted it — `USE_DEBUG` defaults `ON`, debug throws today, and **52** `#ifndef NDEBUG` blocks depend on that, so a blanket `false` breaks all 52. The flag is therefore initialized to `BELFEM_ASSERTIONS_ACTIVE` (a new macro exported by `assert.hpp`, since bare `#ifndef NDEBUG` is not equivalent when both macros are defined), lives as a single definition in `assert.cpp` — never a header static, or each TU gets its own copy — and is flipped on by all 13 test mains. Kept deliberately as **two** predicates: after this change a release binary in throw mode can catch a `BELFEM_ERROR` while `BELFEM_ASSERT` still expands to nothing. **Verified in both configurations**: release 13/13 (1541 tests, 0 failures) with all 17 error-path tests passing and 17 dragons printed, proving the paths executed rather than being skipped; debug 13/13. The five release failures had been predicted by name beforehand and matched exactly, including abort site — which also settled the round's one source-trace-only finding, Codex's catch that `test_HDF5.cpp:195` is a real `BELFEM_ERROR` path. **Stated rather than papered over:** a test now verifies that the check fires, *not* that the process aborts; release and test runs take different reactions by construction, which is the price of not forking after `MPI_Init`. Not done: the 52 bare guards still work and their migration is hygiene, not a fix.
- [dl20260811_todo_sweep.md](dl20260811_todo_sweep.md) - **Second currentness sweep of `todo/` in three days, and the first one to find a register row that was *worse* than recorded, not better.** Four prior corrections in this directory all went the same way — DR-43's four gasmodels defects, DR-19's ticked items, B6's four unticked weeks, DR-52's already-fixed P0 — which quietly builds the expectation that a stale row is a pessimistic row. DR-45(a) retires it: `5a2ddf81` fixed the worker-warning rank gate in `MUMPS::free` and in `MUMPS::solve`, but there are **two** `solve` overloads and only the `Vector`-RHS one was fixed; the `Matrix`-RHS overload still discarded worker warnings at `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp:563`, including the `INFO(1) = +1` dropped-index warning that DR-45's open half is entirely about. Raised by Grok in the in-flight jury round, confirmed here by reading both overloads rather than counting votes, and closed by the owning session in `78ea534d` — the row stays **open** because nothing has executed and its risk is a false abort on a healthy deck. Two plans closed on run evidence (`bfm_stale_cache_detection`, `example_deck_and_material_db_repair`), two deferred on decisions already taken (`coreduce_…`, `slepc_…`), DR-21 struck, DR-65 added so a closed plan's open question survives the move. Also: R4 was done-and-unticked in `instruction_doc_currency` (DR-63 closed by *amending* the convention rather than manufacturing 22 reference sections), the rho/lambda doc inventory was re-baselined and **grew** by ~15 sites, and `fix_facet_masters` drifted +129 lines in two days. **The result that outlived the sweep:** three slips that look unrelated are one defect class — stale `file:line` anchors, a wrong register ID (DR-24 for an incident that has no row), and a prose enumeration naming two of three `INFO` sites. Every one was a pointer correct when written and never re-followed; none is a reasoning error, and the DR-24 mislabel survived a jury round. `CLAUDE.md`'s "anchor by a searchable token, never a line number" rule already exists for the first — the open question, handed to Christian, is whether it should cover any reference a reader confirms by recognition. Bookkeeping only — nothing compiled, nothing run.
- [dl20260811_ferro_history_scalar_sign.md](dl20260811_ferro_history_scalar_sign.md) - **The ferro nonlinear-mass Newton tangent contracted its history with a magnitude where the isotropization requires a signed projection — a Jacobian sign flip exactly at AC zero-crossings; fixed at both Maxwell sites.** `phi_ferro_newton` and `h_newton_mu` filled `dMdX_times_h` with `dmudh·|hist-field|`, but the slot contract is the signed `(dM_ik/dx_j)·h_k` (the thermal producer's `dot(Nvec, qhist)` already honored it), and the isotropized scalar is `dμ/dH·(αH − ĥ·Hvec_hist)`. The magnitude agrees only for parallel, co-oriented history; under reversal it flips the tangent's sign. Both sites now compute `dot(Hvec, Hvec_hist)/H` via two calculator scratch vectors (no per-intpoint allocation — the φ site actually loses temporaries); at `H ≤ BELFEM_EPSILON` the history scalar takes the zero subgradient, degrading the tangent to the Picard matrix, identically at both sites. Residual untouched, so its roots cannot move — the gate is Newton iteration counts on a ferro AC deck through a zero-crossing, with a monotonic-ramp control that should show ~nothing. Syntax gate green under the tree's own flags. DR-64; `bdf_nonlinear_mass_verification.md` B8.
- [dl20260811_dr35_dr36_build_claims_verified.md](dl20260811_dr35_dr36_build_claims_verified.md) - **Two `[seeded — confirm]` rows carrying five build claims between them, never checked since the 2026-08-05 seeding; four fall to inspection, and the fifth turns out bigger than its row said.** **DR-35 closed.** STRUMPACK is **8.0.0** in all three SCLS toolchains (`libstrumpack.so.8.0.0` under `/opt/scls/{mkl,gcc,debug}`), corroborated by PETSc's own build record (`petscpkg_version.h:301-303`) — comfortably past the 7.1.2 the row wanted for the small-problem hang fix. The requested "BLR `Krylov_iterations==maxit` warning hardening" is **not needed**: STRUMPACK 8.0.0 has a dedicated `ReturnCode::NO_CONVERGENCE` (`StrumpackParameters.hpp:55`), BELFEM already decodes it (`strumpacktools.cpp:27`), and the wrapper routes *every* non-SUCCESS code through the soft-fail contract or a `BELFEM_ERROR` quoting the message — serial (`:262-283`) and distributed (`:336-373`), the latter reducing the flag across ranks so a rank-local SUCCESS cannot mask a peer's failure. **Confidence split honestly rather than averaged:** the handling is *high* (read at the cited lines); whether STRUMPACK *emits* NO_CONVERGENCE at maxit rather than SUCCESS is *medium* — source not installed, `solve()`'s own docs silent, only the string "maximum Krylov iterations" in the binary. Bounded anyway: BLR compression is OFF by default. **DR-36 shrinks from three claims to one.** "Dynamic MKL link untested" is **refuted — it is the daily driver**: `config_mkl.cmake:38-39` links dynamically by construction and `readelf -d` on a binary linked this session lists all five MKL libraries as `NEEDED` shared objects out of `/opt/intel/oneapi/redist/lib`; the binary ran. "PETSc `-lX11`/`-lmpfr` removal unconfirmed" is **confirmed removed**: commented out at `config_petsc.cmake:33` with its reason, and the full `ldd` closure of a PETSc-linked binary carries neither. **The third claim was reframed by Christian, and the first write-up of it — mine — was wrong:** *"We have not validated BELFEM for MPICH actually. So far, PETSc always crashed when calling it. I always compiled openmpi with intel when I used it."* OpenMPI is the **only validated MPI**; MPICH and Intel MPI (MPICH-derived) are an unvalidated configuration carrying a **known PETSc crash**, and even the Intel-compiler route goes through a self-built OpenMPI. So the two hardcoded sites — `config_mumps.cmake:26` (`-lmpi_mpifh` and friends) and `config_mkl.cmake:60-62` (`-lmkl_blacs_openmpi_*`), both `NEEDED` in the binary — are **consistent with the support posture, not defects**; `config_mkl.cmake` picking OpenMPI BLACS even on the Intel path corroborates it exactly. That retires the fix I had proposed: adding `-lmpifort` and an MKL MPICH BLACS variant would advertise support that never existed and move a user from a link error to a *later* crash inside PETSc. **The real gap, found only by checking the correction: the requirement is written down nowhere.** `README.md`, all of `doc/` and `CLAUDE.md` contain **no occurrence** of "OpenMPI", "MPICH", "Intel MPI" or "impi", and `config/system/find_mpi.cmake` inspects only whether `MPI_HOME` is set and inside SCLS — never which implementation it names. A hard requirement whose only trace is two comments buried in the linalg configs. **Both fixes landed the same session on Christian's go-ahead, neither of which is "add MPICH support".** `doc/mpi_support.md` states the requirement, names the PETSc crash as the reason rather than leaving it folklore, gives the Intel-compiler guidance, and records the two hardcoded link sites as *correct* — with an explicit "do not add `-lmpifort` or an MKL `blacs_intelmpi`/`blacs_mpich` variant" so the retired fix is not re-proposed by the next reader who finds those flags and sees a portability bug; indexed in `doc/README.md`, pointers from `src/comm/doc/README.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. The guard in `config/system/find_mpi.cmake` preprocesses a probe against `mpi.h` and reads the macros **the header itself defines** (`OPEN_MPI` vs `MPICH`/`MPICH_VERSION`/`I_MPI_VERSION`), so nothing depends on wrapper flags or compiler names; MPICH aborts with a message naming PETSc and the override, `-DALLOW_UNTESTED_MPI=ON` proceeds with a warning, mirroring the existing `-DALLOW_NINJA=ON` pattern. **The asymmetry is the part worth defending: an implementation the probe cannot identify WARNS rather than errors** — a failed probe is not evidence of an unsupported MPI, and erring hard would turn a detection miss into a broken build for someone whose setup works. All five paths exercised through a standalone `cmake -P` harness including the real file (real mpicxx → silent; `g++` → warning; synthetic MPICH → FATAL_ERROR; with override → warning; unrecognized → warning), the shared build tree never touched, `check_doc_claims.py` 32/32 after the `CLAUDE.md` edit. **Method postscript worth keeping:** four of five claims fell to the CMake config plus `readelf`/`ldd` with no BELFEM build — the **code → claim** direction `check_doc_claims.py` automates. The fifth is the instructive one: it *was* checkable, every fact I reported about it was correct, and I still drew the wrong conclusion, because the missing premise ("MPICH is unvalidated, PETSc crashes there") is not visible from CMake files, link lines or `ldd`. A mechanical check catches stale facts; it cannot supply a support posture nobody ever wrote down.
- [dl20260811_dr33_bdf_ramp_tests.md](dl20260811_dr33_bdf_ramp_tests.md) - **DR-33's reproducer had been deleted five days earlier and the register never noticed.** V1 was the whole row — "BDF2-vs-BDF1 Newton-iteration count on `thermalTest`" — and `src/executables/thermalTest.cpp` was removed on 2026-08-06 in `e4c02ac8`, alongside `periodictest.cpp`, as dead-code cleanup; `src/executables/` now holds only `hphirun`, `hphiTrun` and `electricalCircuit`, and nothing in `src/` or `config/` still names it (the stale CMakeFiles dirs in both build trees are what made it look present). **Reviving it verbatim would revive dead code:** recovered from git, it hardcodes `costheta.msh` and `SolverType::UMFPACK` (opt-in, OFF by default — the DR-49 trap again), starts from T = 0 K, and carries its own `// todo: implement cp and lambda in material definition`, so the ρ(T) nonlinear mass the item is *named* for was never configured in it. The deletion was right; the register's pointer was the defect. **So DR-33 is blocked on a decision, not a run** — which live deck replaces it (`sidecoatings` is the only live thermal exerciser per DR-02's own inventory). **The item splits, and the half needing no deck is now closed:** `tests/fem/test_BdfTimestepMethod.cpp`, six cases in the `fem`/`fast` suite, exploiting that `IWG_Timestep` is concrete, under no pure virtuals, and constructs with no mesh/kernel/dof manager. Pinned: the constructor's BDF1 default, BDF1-5 round-trip, the **startup ramp holding step one at order 1** — load-bearing, because `mH` is all zeros before the first shift, so a broken ramp runs into `compute_bdf_coefficients()`'s own *"Invalid BDF history step size"* assert — the non-BDF gate protecting the eigenvalue `MassOnly`/`StiffnessOnly` switch from stale order state, and the **CN/Galerkin hard rejection, which had never been tested at all** despite being the sole basis on which that clause of DR-33 was struck rather than fixed. **Scope correction recorded in place:** an earlier note claimed the coefficient formulas could be pinned the same way — they cannot. `compute_bdf_coefficients()` is mesh-free, but `mAlpha`/`mBeta`/`mH`/`mStepCount` are private with no accessor and the only writers of the history are `shift_fields`/`reset_fields`/`restore_savepoint`, all of which dereference `mField` and call `DofManager::initialize()`; covering `compute_bdf_coeffs_2..5` needs a fixture or a deliberate seam, i.e. a production-header change and Christian's call. **One guard decided by evidence, not habit:** `BELFEM_ERROR` throws under debug but calls `error_abort()` under release, and the behaviour is baked into `libbelfem_iwg.a` at library build time — running the CN case against the release-built library **took the process down via MPI_ABORT**, so without its `#ifndef NDEBUG` guard a release `make check` would lose the binary rather than report a failure. Compiles clean (Blaze, NDEBUG and DEBUG, `-Werror`); all five non-debug cases executed green as a standalone probe against the prebuilt libraries. **The run half is PARKED, not pending:** asked which deck replaces `thermalTest`, Christian's answer was that we do not have one — a nonlinear-mass ρ(T) thermal transient does not exist in the tree in any form, so V1's run is not schedulable and carrying it as an open action would be noise in a register whose purpose is the release lens. The unblock condition is written down instead (any deck with temperature-dependent ρcp, run at BDF1 and BDF2 at fixed Δt, Newton counts to ±1; `method :` is already an input key). **What that leaves standing, stated in both directions: nothing in the tree exercises BDF2-5 end to end** — bounded, because BDF2-5 is opt-**in** since the 08-07 rollback so no deck in use depends on it and the all-NaN defect is fixed with its ramp now tested; but real, because `compute_bdf_coeffs_2..5` is verified by no test and no run. With no deck available a seam was the *only* route to that coverage — **and on Christian's go-ahead it was built the same session:** one `friend class BdfCoefficientProbe ;` line in `cl_IWG_Timestep.hpp` (zero runtime cost, no widened public API, probe defined only in the test and used nowhere in `src/`) plus §4's three cases — constant-step reduction to the published BDF2-5 constants (Hairer & Wanner II.4, an independent reference not a restatement), **polynomial exactness at ragged step size** (the property that actually defines BDF-p, and what a mistyped cumulative sum would break while leaving the uniform case intact), and the ramp using reduced-order coefficients (a BDF5 run two steps in must give BDF2's α, since `collect_qhist` truncates history to `mOrderActive`). **Running it corrected the test itself, which a static check could never have done:** written first with realistic 3e-4 steps it was near-vacuous — a wrong-order scheme misses by O(h^p) ≈ 1e-13 there, indistinguishable from a correct answer's roundoff, and BDF5 was measured reproducing d/dt of t⁶ to 2e-13, so it would have gone green for a scheme it exists to reject. Steps rescaled to O(0.1) (separation now fifteen orders of magnitude: ~1e-15 exact vs 16–73 % wrong) and the negation at degree p+1 asserted rather than merely observed. Nine cases total; clean under **both** backends × NDEBUG/DEBUG under `-Werror`, production consumers unaffected by the header change, all non-debug cases executed green against the prebuilt libraries.
- [dl20260811_dr42_gesvd_spline_column_tests.md](dl20260811_dr42_gesvd_spline_column_tests.md) - **Both halves of DR-42 written: the gesvd branch that had never run, and the spline overload that was being used as its own reference.** `fn_gesvd.hpp` splits on the caller's `Work` length — query-and-grow, or trust the buffer and derive `lwork` from it — and **both** existing tests enter with a default-constructed `Vector`, so the second branch was dead in the suite. It is the delicate one: one real-valued buffer holds LAPACK's `work` in its head (two reals per entry for complex `T`) and the complex-only `5*min(m,n)` `rwork` in its tail, where an off-by-one overruns rather than failing loudly. Four new typed tests over all four flavors: exact-minimum length (asserting the buffer is **not resized**, which is the observable proof the other branch ran), minimum + 137 (moves the tail and puts the complex split where the minimum case cannot reach), a one-buffer-three-calls reuse including a **smaller** third problem where `lwork` comes from the old length while the tail is sized from the new `min(m,n)`, and `jobu = jobvt = 'N'` (the `ldu = ldvt = 1` / empty-`data()` path). All pass `AbortOnError = false` and assert on `info` — deliberately, because the test mirrors the wrapper's own `lwork` formula and so is no second opinion on it; **LAPACK gives that verdict**, returning `info = -13` if the formula is understated, which turns a would-be abort into a red test. On the spline side, §3.4 was asserting the extrapolation contract *using* `eval( x, 0 )` as its trusted reference while nothing tested the column overloads at all. New §8 covers all five (`eval`/`deval`/`ddeval`/`entropy`/`dentropy`): pinned-column values against a hand-written coefficient table, evaluated at the knot, the midpoint, **and far outside the interval** — the last is what separates "uses column k" from "clamps like `find_col`" — plus `EXPECT_DOUBLE_EQ` (not `NEAR`; same arithmetic, same order) agreement between the column-free and column forms, and the range and entropy-mode asserts. §8 is built through `matrix_data()` and the empty-container constructor so **no solver is in the loop**, putting it outside the `BELFEM_SUPERLU` gate — DR-49's premise was that these very tests would otherwise be invisible, and removing the dependence beats betting on the gate. Evidence beyond a review: clean compile under Armadillo *and* Blaze × NDEBUG/DEBUG with the tree's `-Wall -Werror -pedantic-errors`, and both groups **executed** as standalone probes — gesvd 4 flavors × 4 cases green against LAPACK (the complex exact-minimum case accepting `lwork = 10` with a 15-entry tail), spline exact to 0.0, `find_col` agreement bitwise, 7/7 asserts fired. `make check-fast` is Christian's and is the only thing left on the row.
- [dl20260811_claudemd_stale_content_sweep.md](dl20260811_claudemd_stale_content_sweep.md) - **The proprietary `literature/` file tree was being reproduced inside the open-source `CLAUDE.md` — and had drifted besides (`felippa` still under `books/`, `lectures/`, `papers/topology/`, `gross` and `cormen` missing entirely), so it was deleted rather than repaired: `literature/README.md` already carries a current tree, and the open repository should not state that inventory at all.** The `paperN`/`F0` aliases went with it, being opaque to anyone without the proprietary library; author-year citations replace them everywhere, with `doc/literature_references.md` kept as the decoder for older records and the key-papers list refreshed to include the **Arsenault et al. 2026 erratum** that corrects the 2023 air-domain coupling, Denis 2026, Lucchini 2025 and the Dular 1997/1999 pair the `circuit/` module rests on. **The sweep's real yield was §3: two read-only auditors found nine false claims, four of which would have produced wrong code.** `share( Cell<T>& )` exists at `commtools.hpp:1648` — the file (and `coding_philosophy.md:525`, from which it was copied) said no such overload existed; `aligned_alloc(64,…)` was taught as the SIMD pattern though `src/` contains no aligned allocation anywhere and no backend gives 64 bytes; `BELFEM_ERROR` was prescribed for convergence failures, which `coding_philosophy.md:621` calls a design error because it converts a retry-able state into an abort; and the sweep introduced a Blaze/Armadillo default inversion that the audit caught (`CMakeLists.txt:61-67` — Armadillo everywhere but Apple). Five more were wrong but harmless: `-O3 -fno-exceptions` (actually `-O2`), `make tests` (actually `make check`, `USE_TEST` OFF), "dipole/helix/magnet" executables (actually `hphirun`/`hphiTrun`/`electricalCircuit`), `fvm/` listed as built though `src/CMakeLists.txt` comments it out, and "both static and shared" though libraries are STATIC-only. "Every module README provides external references with DOIs" held for **1 of 23**. Two omissions with operational weight were closed: the nonfree AI-exchange ban (protocol §2 — vendor leak surface) was absent while the checklist told sessions to *use* the exchange, and the "reviewed ≠ verified" evidence ladder had no presence in the bootstrap file. **Two edits landed outside `CLAUDE.md` because precedence demanded it:** `ai_collaboration_protocol.md:171` mandated the retired `paperN` format *and* named `CLAUDE.md` as its source, so retiring the aliases in one file alone would have been inoperative. Left open: `doc/literature_references.md` still omits the topology papers and lecture notes; legacy aliases survive in live source comments (`cl_FEM_Controller.hpp:120` and two others), untouched pending approval. Documentation only; nothing compiled or run.
- [dl20260811_spfa_solver_test_port.md](dl20260811_spfa_solver_test_port.md) - **The difference-constraint solver behind the thin-cut rectifier was production code with ZERO checked-in coverage, and the harness that once tested it lived only in a swept scratchpad (DR-23, T8 part one).** Ported as `tests/math/test_GraphSpfa.cpp`, 16 tests in the `math`/`fast` suite. **The tests verify the answer, not the verdict:** a feasible result is accepted only after re-testing every constraint against the returned theta; an infeasible result only after checking the certificate is a genuine closed walk of **strictly** negative weight. Two cases are regression guards for defects the original harness caught — a zero-weight cycle must read **feasible**, and large feasible instances must survive the spurious `cnt > n` trigger. Randomized instances are feasible **by construction**, so an infeasible verdict there is a false negative rather than a fixture bug. **DR-23 stays open and blocking-1.0, scope sharpened not closed:** the `Cohomology` entry points still need a mesh fixture and T9 is a run. Syntax-checked only; **not executed** (`USE_TEST` is OFF).
- [dl20260811_bfm_stale_cache_detection.md](dl20260811_bfm_stale_cache_detection.md) - **A `.bfm` cached the ENRICHED mesh (cuts, thin-shell layers, coating walls, periodicity, hanging entities) but was reused on a checksum of the RAW geometry alone, so editing a layer thickness, a cut algorithm or the coating flag silently reloaded the old mesh with nothing printed (DR-21).** Fixed with a second, orthogonal stamp: the checksum stays exactly as it is — it IS the `.msh` identity and the memdump guard, and it catches a changed base mesh under an unchanged deck, which no options tag can see — while a new config tag catches the reverse. Validity is the conjunction. **Two designs died before the built one:** a checksum of the *completed* mesh (Christian: the point of the file is to avoid rerunning cohomology, so a stamp needing that rerun saves nothing — it survives only as a possible integrity check), and hashing the deck text (`100 um` and `0.1 mm` are the same mesh; a reindent is too). Built instead is Christian's "just check the numbers": thin-shell count, layers per shell, SI thicknesses, coating flag — plus the entries two blind jury rounds proved also change the cached mesh, `homology { algorithm }` and the terminal ids, both of which feed `CutFactory`. FNV-1a over `%.12g` text rather than `std::hash`, because a `.bfm` may be mailed to another machine and `std::hash` is unspecified across standard libraries; the canonical text is stored beside the tag so a mismatch names the changed line. `Mesh::save()` builds its own `BfmFile`, so the tag rides on the `Mesh` instead of through a setter, and `BfmFile` gained `config_tag()`/`config_text()` probes because the decision happens before `load()`. **The section worth reading is §3: running it found two defects compiling could not** — an id list `1,3,5,7,9,11` collapsed to `1` (first-character numeric test sent it through `to_real`, which stops at the separator, so `...,11` and `...,13` tagged identically — a silent miss inside the feature built to prevent silent misses), and the terminal filter matched only `terminal` while corc writes `input curves`, so corc's cut ids were absent entirely. Six discrimination tests now pass across three decks: thickness, extra layer and terminal-id edits change the tag; a unit change (`50 mum` vs `0.05 mm`) and a solver change do not. Also recorded: one alarming "thickness change does not move the tag" result that was my own `sed` failing to match (`mum`, not `um`), nearly acted on. Not built into the tree; `make` and the deck-edit reproducer are Christian's.
- [dl20260810_debt_register_small_cleanups.md](dl20260810_debt_register_small_cleanups.md) - **Three register items cleared without a run: DR-49, DR-47, DR-24 — one bigger than its row said, one smaller, one scoped back to what is honestly checkable.** **DR-49:** every spline construction/evaluation test sat behind `#ifdef BELFEM_SUITESPARSE`, which is **off by default** (legacy, not BSD-3 clean) — so §3–§7 compiled out and **spline coverage in the fast gate was zero**, with DR-42's planned `aCol` tests set to be invisible too. The gate was wrong on its own terms as well: production `Spline::update_data` solves with **SuperLU** (`cl_Spline.cpp:427-429`, whose own comment explains SuiteSparse "cannot be relied on"), so the tests were gated on a library the code under test had stopped using. Re-gated on `BELFEM_SUPERLU` at all four sites, headers corrected from "require UMFPACK", and the reason recorded in-file so it does not come back; `tests/sparse/test_Solver.cpp` keeps its SuiteSparse gates because those really are UMFPACK solver tests. **DR-47:** `Hex8TbUnitCirculation` re-enabled — its blocker (no Lagrange factory case for HEX8TB) landed 2026-08-08. Dimensions were verified before flipping, not assumed, because the test's comment names four wall dofs while the helper loops `number_of_edges()`: the fixture returns the **HEX8TB** wall (it also builds a HEX8TS layer beside it — easy to misread), `number_of_edges(HEX8TB)` = 4, and `EF_HEX8TB` sizes `mE` as 3×4, so loop bound, column count and the 4×4 assertion agree and the `HEX8TS` argument only picks the corner table. An initial worry that the helper would over-index a 4-column matrix was **refuted**. Its `EXPECTED: pending sign-off` caveat is unchanged and shared with the enabled Penta6Ts/Hex8Ts siblings — first green run is the sign-off, and it is currently the wall element's only automated check. **DR-24:** `set_currents` fixes `aI(k)` onto abstract node dof `k` while the caller builds `tI` in current-BC order, with nothing tying the two together. Split honestly: the checkable half is now checked (`BELFEM_ERROR` on `aI.length() > mAbstractNodeDofs.size()` — the old loop silently discarded the surplus — plus a null-dof assert), while the *ordering* correspondence is established by the factory with no key on the dof side to re-derive it from, so it is documented rather than faked; asserting it for real means threading BC identity through to the dofs, a design change. `BELFEM_ERROR` not `ASSERT` because it is O(1) once per timestep. **Nothing compiled or run** — three source files changed, `make check-fast` (USE_TEST=ON) is Christian's, and two of the three changes make previously-silent tests execute for the first time, so a red result there is a finding rather than a regression. **Left open: DR-59** — the retired `populate_rho_database_parallel` bodies are now unreferenced dead code in both Metal and Alloy carrying a never-advancing receive counter and an index-mismatched packing; delete or keep-and-fix is Christian's decision.
- [dl20260810_examples_and_rho_database_repair.md](dl20260810_examples_and_rho_database_repair.md) - **The two defects standing between a fresh checkout and a first successful run, both fixed and committed (`bc578b5e`) — and the finding underneath them: the parallel rho-database build never worked, and never needed to exist.** A first run on ≥2 ranks aborted in METIS before the mesh was even read, because `populate_rho_database_parallel` forced contiguity (`partition( tCommSize, false, true, false )` — third argument, passed explicitly) on the non-contiguous 95×35×37 tensor grid. Un-forcing it was necessary, kept, and insufficient: it exposed the Distributor crashing on empty worker sets, and a master-only-build second attempt then **deadlocked**, because the "serial" builder's `Database` constructor is collective at `comm_size > 1` — a design error whose root cause was a pre-flight "no hidden collectives" check that read the function bodies but not their callees. The final fix **removes the `comm_size` branch entirely**: `Projector::project` was already a designed collective pair and `populate_tensor_mesh` already populates only the master, so every rank runs the former serial path in lockstep, the master evaluates, and workers hold an empty-but-valid grid and receive the projected table inside the constructor (enabled by an early return in `TensorMeshFactory::create_bsplines` for element-less meshes — the main blast-radius question, flagged). **Both plan-stage auditors independently killed the original rationale:** `Partitioner` applies METIS's result to owners only inside `if( aSetProcOwnerships )`, which the *second* argument disables, so this path always computed everything on rank 0 — dropping CONTIG stops an abort, it does not create parallelism, and "each rank computes its own nodes" must not be repeated. Second defect: a stale cache silently shadowed regeneration (gate was `file_exists()` alone, no version stamp) and then died with *"Dataset RRR of type double does not exist"* — now an `RRR`-marker probe (`fn_rho_database_is_current.hpp`, raw `htri_t` tri-state so a probe *error* lands on the rebuild side), MPI-symmetric by master-probe-and-broadcast, warn-and-regenerate, in **both** `Metal` and `Alloy`. Third: `examples/README.md` written — five of six examples ship a `.geo` and no `.msh`, and nothing said so. **The DoD's "bit-identical h5diff" was amended by evidence rather than quietly dropped:** two builds differ at ~1e-7 *even serially* (MUMPS/MKL multithread nondeterminism — serial-vs-serial 8.7e-8, 2-rank-vs-serial 7.3e-8), while two `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` builds are bit-identical; the single-threaded statement is the achievable one and it holds. Post-implementation jury caught three more, fixed same session (worker-side null-`SpMatrix` reference bind in the Projector, work-mesh leak in both serial builders, the `htri_t` hazard). **`examples/corc` closed the same day, by replacement rather than repair** — Christian swapped in his working 6-tape model (now byte-identical to `cmake-build-debug/corc`), verified from a clean dir: reads 61,418 nodes / 341,712 elements, passes `create_periodic`, **builds both rho databases in-run from the empty directory**, runs 12 timesteps to t = 300 ms and warm-restarts from its own memdump — which is the first-run fix above demonstrated on a second, larger deck. **§8 records a correction earned the hard way:** the original diagnosis blamed the gmsh 2.2 format ("no `$Entities`, therefore no vertices"), Christian objected that his corc runs, and the evidence killed it — BELFEM builds vertices from **type-15 point elements** in either format, the working mesh is *also* 2.2 with ten of them, and the example's lone vertex is the *last* element in `$Elements` (appended by the Python tool that wrote the mesh, serving `bearing { nodes : 9 }`). The two dirs are different models entirely, 3-tape vs 6-tape, so "corc runs" and "shipped corc aborts" were both true. The quoted error message was wrong too: `BELFEM_ASSERT` is compiled out here, so `Mesh::vertex`'s readable assert never fires and you get `Map::operator()`'s always-on fallback *"Key not found in map"* — no id, no map name, a diagnostic the debug sibling carries and the release path drops. The repair that finally landed was none of the ones debated — replace the pair, not patch the mesh. The retired `populate_rho_database_parallel` bodies survive as unreferenced dead code carrying two landmines (a receive loop whose read counter never advances; dense packing read back by `node->index()`) — DR-59.
- [dl20260810_arpack_driver_configuration_fix.md](dl20260810_arpack_driver_configuration_fix.md) - **"ARPACK is slow" investigated as a SLEPc-replacement question and root-caused as a driver misconfiguration sitting on top of two live out-of-bounds stack writes (uncommitted, not compiled).** `slct` was declared `slct(nev)` — one element at `nev=1` — while `dneupd.f:330` declares `select(ncv)` and `dneupd.f:548-551` unconditionally writes `ncv` of them: three logicals past the end of a stack array, every call, since the file was written; `z(n,nev)` violates the documented `N by NEV+1` and overruns whenever a complex-conjugate pair straddles the cut, which for a non-symmetric `dnaupd`/`dneupd` driver is the expected case. Both were masked because every work array was sized to an expression equal to the requirement *only* at `ncv == 2*nev+1`, pinning the whole block to a Krylov subspace of dimension **3** (Remark 4 of `dnaupd.f` gives `2*nev+1` as a floor, not a target) — and the arrays were automatic, i.e. on the stack under `-fopenmp`, so raising `ncv` to a useful 20 would have overflowed it. `mNumMaxIter = mN*mN` clobbered the sane default of 300 on a counter of *restarts*, turning non-convergence into a grind instead of a failure. **The decoder had been swallowing ARPACK's own diagnosis:** `arpack::check` decoded both routines against dneupd's table, so `dnaupd info = 3` — *"increase the size of NCV relative to NEV"*, the exact bug — fell through to "Unknown Error", while the benign `info = 1` was reported as "Shur reordering failed". Fixed: allocatable work arrays sized per the real contracts, `ncv = min(n, max(2*nev+1, 20))`, `ido` split out of `info(2)`, `NCONV` now reported and checked, split `check_naupd`/`check_neupd` with each routine's true table transcribed from arpack-ng 3.9.0. Also made the `nev` setters functional (they resized the λ buffers while `nev` stayed a hardcoded `const 1`, so asking for five eigenvalues computed one), extremum now scanned from the converged set rather than read off slot 0, eigenvalues exposed through `compute_{smallest,largest}_eigenvalues()` + accessors, and `arpacktools.cpp` gated on `USE_ARPACK` (it was compiled unconditionally while the `.f90` was gated → link error instead of clean degrade). **Left open by ruling:** `'SM'` is still structurally hopeless in mode 1 — shift-and-invert needs a solve-only wrapper path (MUMPS drives JOB 5/6, so every matvec would refactorize); the SLEPc port stays open, and if taken up the target is `SVD` not `EPS`, since `EPS_SMALLEST_MAGNITUDE` without a spectral transform would be exactly as slow. Separate finding: `compute_conditioning()` returns `|λ_max|/|λ_min|`, which is κ₂ only for normal matrices — the h-φ Jacobian is not — while the MUMPS branch returns an Arioli/Demmel/Duff system estimate, so the two branches of one function report different quantities; this bears on the live "conditioning-dominated" residual-floor hypothesis in `todo/timestep_collapse_residual_floor_plan.md:101-106`.
- [dl20260809_gasmodels_make_check_failures.md](dl20260809_gasmodels_make_check_failures.md) - **Five gasmodels `make check` failures root-caused over two rounds, two of them live physics defects. (a) The cubic entropy reference: commit `1c04933f` removed `mSref` as a "double count", but it was cancelling the standard-state entropy that `cl_GM_EoS_Cubic.cpp:628` anchors into the departure spline — so every PR/SRK gas returned an absolute entropy one standard state (6702 J/kg/K for air) too low, measured as `Cubic_Entropy` at r2 = −56.4 with an RMS residual of 6648. Fixed by anchoring the departure spline's entropy at 0.0, which restores the pre-commit expression term for term while keeping the clearer `realgas_s`; the commit message's "13.6 instead of 6.86 kJ/kg/K" was reasoned from the code, not measured. (b) A sign typo in methane residual term 34 (`mN(33)` = `+1.387292044e-2`, Setzmann & Wagner Table 35 term 34 is negative), the only one of the 40 terms disagreeing with the published set.** It leaves p(Tc,ρc) correct but breaks the critical constraints the equation was fitted under (∂p/∂ρ = −16129 instead of 0), so every isotherm up to ≈197 K carried a spurious van der Waals loop — which is why `Helmholtz::v()` aborted with "Too many iterations" at 190.595 K / 50 bar: the density Newton had three roots and a ∂p/∂v > 0 stretch, not a robustness problem. Exact below ~170 K against the paper's own ancillary equations and −16 % at 190 K, hence invisible to the campaign's far-field validations. Verified with a numerical mirror against CoolProp 8.0.0 (same EoS): agreement goes from 14 % error near critical to **5.7e-6 uniformly**, that residual being BELFEM's `Rm/M` vs the paper's rounded R. Tracked as D16/D17 in `../todo/closed/gas_correctness_fixes.md`. The rest are test-side: `PrandtlMeyerPerfectGasLimit` assumed argon is calorically perfect over 200–4000 K when the NASA table only delivers cp = 2.5 R exactly on 250–1000 K (retargeted to T₁ = 950 K, Ma ≤ 3; tolerances then set by the ~1e-9 noise a spline-derivative cp carries, amplified near sonic); `Methane_Cp_FD_NearCritical`'s ±0.05 % step truncates by 1.2 % at the 193 K cp spike — CoolProp scores 1.682e-4 on the identical measurement against BELFEM's 1.679e-4, which is itself the cleanest confirmation the fixed EoS is right; and the disabled Gibbs test stopped compiling because `Matrix::col()` views convert implicitly to both `Vector` and `Matrix`, making `r2` ambiguous. Not compiled; build/test gate is Christian's.

- [dl20260807_doxygen_markdown_integration.md](dl20260807_doxygen_markdown_integration.md) - **The `*/doc/*.md` documentation is now a browsable Doxygen page tree, and the warning count went 607 → 16 (uncommitted, not compiled).** The markdown was already being rendered — as a flat alphabetical "Related Pages" dump at mangled URLs — because `LAYOUT_FILE` was a relative path that never resolved against the build directory, `doc/` was missing from `INPUT`, and there was no main page. - [dl20260806_side_edge_fusing_authority.md](dl20260806_side_edge_fusing_authority.md) - **D8 rim branch-mixing fixed at the source (R1–R3 of `todo/side_edge_fusing_cut_aware_plan.md`, uncommitted, not compiled): the thin-shell side-edge/node fuse now resolves every rim station ONCE against the cut-composed air volume nodes — the same input the outer-interface anchors read — instead of the branch-blind mid-surface temp/curve nodes.** R1 pinned the mechanism: `create_temporary_edges` keys by original-normalized pairs and `unique()`s them, so branch identity is destroyed by construction; the anchors have no cut logic of their own (CutFactory relinking runs first, `create_cuts()` → `create_thinshells()` → anchors). - [dl20260804_2d_thinshell_layer_alternation.md](dl20260804_2d_thinshell_layer_alternation.md) - **Root cause of Gregory's 2-D thin-shell N>1 layer-alternating current (read-only, 3-AI convergent).** `EF_QUAD4TS::E()` negates the top-edge dof on a false `mS[1]=-mS[0]` assumption (the factory builds both carried edges same-direction, so `mS[0]==mS[1]`); adjacent same-material layers share the interface Edge object, so the basis is anti-continuous through the thickness and h_t/J flip sign per layer. Curl parameters `+0.5,+0.5` make per-layer J ∝ (h_bot+h_top) instead of (h_top−h_bot)/t — Ampère telescoping broken. Same E/C feed assembly and JJCz postproc → solved field wrong, not viz. N=1 hides it (no shared interior edge); 3-D PENTA6TS/HEX8TS use plain `+mS` (correct). Latent extras: mCoeffs index typo (axis-aligned-only correctness), double 0.5 curl scaling (likely the |J/Jc|max≈0.87), factory-vs-EF normal convention (global sign only), 4× dV overweight (quad weights sum to 4, dV_ts returns EF det_J). Fix APPLIED and Codex-audited: 3-D `+mS` convention, true ∓0.5 curl parameters, geometric ∇η, mDetJ = area/4; h_ghost interaction traced (3-D bit-identical, 2-D ghost becomes consistent, greg2 has no ghosts). Old 1-layer runs evaluated ρ(|J|/2) — quantitative changes expected; validate against analytics, not old output. Post-fix smoke exposed a SECOND masked sign defect: the top-surface edge→φ tie was flipped (`get_top_nodes` QUAD returned facet-2 traversal {2,3} vs the position order the hang functions pair against) → net shell current ≈ 0, odd ±J screening profile while the air-side Norris solution stayed correct; the two errors had canceled at the surface pre-basis-fix (why 1-layer looked right). Fixed by the corner swap in `meshtools.cpp get_top_nodes`; Codex-audited; validated by Gregory (uniform same-sign J, physical global sign).

- [dl20260803_blaze_lazy_inv_defect.md](dl20260803_blaze_lazy_inv_defect.md) - **The `cl_EF_QUAD4TS.cpp:50` abort is a Blaze 3.9.0 defect, not fallout from the LAPACK/Blaze interface work: `B * inv( A )` silently transposes the destination whenever `B` is non-square.** `blaze::inv()` is lazy (`DMatInvExpr.h:405`); Blaze restructures `B * inv(A)` into `trans( solve( trans(A), trans(B) ) )` (`MatInvExpr.h:190`), `solve()` resizes the destination through a `DMatTransposer` (`LSE.h:254`), and `DMatTransposer::resize( m, n )` forwards the extents **unswapped** (`DMatTransposer.h:353-355`). Measured: `2x2*inv(2x2)` correct, `3x2*inv(2x2)` → dest becomes 2x3 in BOTH debug and release (no assertion — silent), `2x1*inv(1x1)` → abort. The left form `inv(A)*B` is unaffected. Two live sites: QUAD4TS (aborts) and `cl_FVM_Factory.cpp:783` (silently transposed). Fix = eager backend wrapper in `fn_BZ_inv.hpp`, with three load-bearing details each caught by an auditor rather than by inspection: the parameter must be the **raw backend type** (a `Matrix<T>` overload is never a candidate, since `fn_inv.hpp` calls `inv( aA.matrix_data() )` → ADL → lazy `blaze::inv`; the first attempt left the abort in place), an `IsMatrix_v` guard is required (Blaze's scalar `inv()` is gated on `IsScalar_v`, true for unknown types, so an unconstrained template swallows `belfem::Matrix`), and the return type must **pin the storage order** (`evaluate( inv( product expr ) )` came back row-major, against the column-major invariant). Cost: the lazy `inv(A)*B → solve(A,B)` rewrite is gone; the one real non-debug site is `cl_Gradient.cpp:206` at 1.19x in `-O3` (~23 ns/integration point, postprocessing) — the "only debug" claim was false. Second fix: QUAD4TS's 1x1 Gram makes `J+ = J^T (J J^T)^-1` collapse to `trans( mJ ) / mGram( 0, 0 )` — **bitwise identical** over 720 geometries. Scope is the 1D-manifold facet only; `cl_EF_PENTA6TS.cpp:61,64` (materialises `mInvGram` first) and `cl_EF_HEX8TS.cpp:380-384` (hand-rolled 2x2 adjugate) need a genuine 2x2 inverse and were never exposed to the defect. Third fix: eager `inv()` costs exactly **one `posix_memalign` per call** (measured with an interposed allocator), i.e. a per-element allocation inside `link()` — so all six sites in the edge functions now write into pre-sized members (PENTA6TS ×2, QUAD4TS ×2 incl. a local `Matrix tJ` promoted to member `mJ2`, TRI3, TRI6), verified **bitwise identical** to `blaze::inv()` over 20000 draws. Fourth fix, which the third one forced: **`inv2`/`inv3` now test rank, not scale.** Their `|det| > BELFEM_EPS` assert was an absolute bound on a dimensional quantity, and it misclassified both ways — it rejected a perfectly conditioned PENTA6TS Gram (cond 3.0) for being small, since `det(G) = 4 area²` scales as h⁴ and drops under machine epsilon for any facet finer than **0.13 mm**, while happily accepting a 1e-9-radian sliver (cond 2e9) for being large; the same triangle flipped verdict purely by moving the mesh scale. Replaced by the **Hadamard ratio** `|det|/Π‖rᵢ‖` — in [0,1] by Hadamard's inequality, 1 for orthogonal rows, 0 exactly at rank deficiency — compared in squared form so no `sqrt` is needed, and free in release since the whole expression sits inside `BELFEM_ASSERT`. Chosen over a condition-number test because thin-shell Jacobians are *deliberately* anisotropic (tangent row vs thickness row) and κ would penalise the intended shape. Tolerance is `BELFEM_EPSILON` (10× eps) not `BELFEM_EPS`: across the tree `BELFEM_EPS` is a seed/floor **value** (10 uses) while `BELFEM_EPSILON` is the **decision threshold** constant (187 uses) — these two asserts were the only places `BELFEM_EPS` served as a comparison tolerance. Deliberately loose: the check catches collapsed/uninitialised matrices, not bad elements — quality policing stays in `MeshChecker`. Payoff: all four hand-rolled adjugates (incl. the pre-existing `cl_EF_HEX8TS.cpp:380`) collapse back to `inv2`/`inv3`. Verified: good shapes pass h=1 … 1e-8, anisotropic thin-shell Jacobians pass to aspect 1e8, collapsed/dependent/1e-17-ratio cases assert, `inv2`/`inv3` still bitwise identical to `blaze::inv()`. Thread `tmp/ai_exchange/blaze_inv_pseudoinverse.md`.
- [dl20260731_hex8tb_edge_function.md](dl20260731_hex8tb_edge_function.md) - **HEX8TB edge function derived by 3-AI consultation (Q1–Q3 unanimous, identical formulas from Claude/Codex/Grok) and implemented same day; campaign doc landed in `src/fem/maxwell/doc/side_connector_wall_element.md`.** Exact-cuboid metric (block width/thickness + midpoint length — the outer nodes are a drawing proxy and must not feed the metric), constant Jacobian/Nabla built once in `link()` with right-handed b = n×t frame, E = s·F·(2/L)t, C via constant curl factors P = −4/(wL)n / Q = +4/(dL)b. Key physics: **j_t ≡ 0 exactly** (transport lives in the shell; the wall carries only cross-section currents) and **h_t is the stream function of the cross-flow** — the transferred current between boundary points is their Δh_t, which IS the r′ stencil. Q4 recovery (h_n from adjacent shells' compute_hn, h_b from shell in-plane field, never new dofs, material-law-only) documented, deliberately not implemented. Q5 settled: [-1,1] vs barycentric [0,1] edge spaces are compatible — dofs are ampere-valued unit circulations, traces constant at lowest order. Implementation audited clean by Codex+Grok (all signs verified); new R5 gates: physical_tag gets clobbered by Maxwell material assignment (D4), ζ-gap semantics undecided (D5).
- [dl20260731_kernel_theory_docs.md](dl20260731_kernel_theory_docs.md) - **Theory reference docs for the solver machinery: variable-step BDF1-5, Anderson-accelerated Picard, and the nonlinear Controller — written, Grok/Codex-audited (math independently re-derived; 7 factual fixes; 13 prose edits), landed in `src/fem/kernel/doc/`.** Includes the paper1 Eq. 14 arctan sign erratum, the beta-asymmetry argument behind flush-on-reject, and the implicit-overestimation-of-runaway-modes note. Two code follow-ups surfaced and FIXED same day (Codex+Grok audited): the Newton-branch residual recompute read stale assembly-time field values (now refreshed in the update loop), and reset_fields could not restore mH(3) because the shift destroys it (now backed up via mHDropped, savepoint-transported).
- [dl20260730_cmake_generator_pinning.md](dl20260730_cmake_generator_pinning.md) - **The recurring "build tree corruption" that forced full cache wipes was a Ninja/Makefiles generator collision in `cmake-build-debug/`; the project is now pinned to Unix Makefiles.** CLion 2026.2 configures with an explicit `-G Ninja`, and its cache reset deletes `CMakeCache.txt` + `CMakeFiles/` but leaves the top-level `Makefile`. A terminal `make` picked up that orphan, whose `cmake_check_build_system` rule re-ran the full configure (the TPL discovery banner), which regenerated *Ninja* files from the cached generator — then make recursed into `CMakeFiles/Makefile2`, which Ninja never writes. Two guards added after the in-source check: a `FATAL_ERROR` pin rejecting non-Makefile generators (`option( ALLOW_NINJA ... OFF )` escape hatch, ordered first so a rejected configure deletes nothing), and a cleanup pass that drops the other generator's entry points (`Makefile`/`Makefile2`/`Makefile.cmake` under Ninja, `build.ninja`/`rules.ninja`/`.ninja_deps`/`.ninja_log` under Makefiles). No false positives — a healthy tree of either kind never holds both; object files untouched since both generators share the `CMakeFiles/<target>.dir/` layout. Both branches verified against the real source in a scratch tree. Remaining manual step: flip the CLion generator dropdown — it cannot be scripted, since CLion rewrites `.idea/workspace.xml` from memory and passes `-G` explicitly.
- [dl20260730_anderson_picard_implementation.md](dl20260730_anderson_picard_implementation.md) - **Anderson(m) mixing for the Picard branch implemented (opt-in `anderson depth` key) + the ts17 controller hygiene bundle: ω active-store carry at 9 sites, thermal flip latch, retry state resets, full flush-trigger wiring.** Type-II mixing in standalone `fn_FEM_anderson_mixing.hpp` (column-normalized `gels`, drop-oldest retry, ‖γ‖∞ guard), stage/commit/discard handshake between Controller and SolverData, reject ⇒ full window flush (β=live-ω leaves the ΔXγ term undamped otherwise), rank-consistent controller-side commit counters gate the AIMD growth (O2). 4 gtest cases, hand-verified affine convergence. Uncommitted, unbuilt — R8 tape A/B validation pending (Christian). Plan: `todo/anderson_picard_acceleration_plan.md`.
- [dl20260730_lapack_unified_interface_review.md](dl20260730_lapack_unified_interface_review.md) - **Unified backend-agnostic LAPACK interface landed in `src/linalg/lapack/` (gesv, posv, gels, gemm, getrf, getri, gesvd, all four datatypes) — five waves in one day: 3-AI concept review, implementation, per-routine audits, final 3-AI audit, 52-test typed suite.** One generic body per routine + backend-aware `lapack::leading_dimension()` (Armadillo `n_rows()`, Blaze padded `spacing()`; a storage property — never trans-dependent); per-backend `fn_AR_/fn_BZ_` gesv wrappers and `fn_LAPACK_*` deleted. Key design settlements: `int_t` IS the LAPACK integer (SCLS one-width guarantee; `USE_MKL_64BIT_API` → `BELFEM_INT64`, `BLAZE_BLAS_IS_64BIT` synced in `blaze_config.hpp`, sizeof static_asserts as backstop — old ILP64 builds were ABI-broken before this); `cplx_float_t`/`cplx_double_t` glue matches every vendor's own declarations (`MKL_Complex8/16` only for MKL-without-Blaze, since Blaze's clapack protos stay active even under MKL; real-pair otherwise) — `std::complex` prototypes are conflicting C-linkage declarations under Blaze; hidden Fortran char-lengths exactly where Blaze forces them (posv, gesvd — gesvd also needs real-valued `S` and the complex-only `rwork`, both ABI traps in the draft); preconditions `BELFEM_ASSERT`, `BELFEM_ERROR` only on `info != 0`, suppressible via `AbortOnError=false` + `int_t` info return for Anderson-type callers. gemm made op()-aware in m/n/k and asserts. Pivots are `Vector<int_t>` end-to-end. Accepted constraint: `mkl_lapack.h`/generic `lapack.h`/un-shimmed `slu_ddefs.h` must never share a TU with these wrappers. Tests: `tests/linalg/test_lapack.cpp`, gtest typed suite, 13 tests × 4 types, verified on both backends.
- [dl20260730_gels_least_squares_wrapper.md](dl20260730_gels_least_squares_wrapper.md) - **LAPACK `gels` least-squares wrapper added for both linalg backends (real + complex); Blaze `polyfit` moved off the normal equations.** Blaze ships no `gels.h`, but no copy into a temporary buffer is needed: BELFEM configures Blaze `columnMajor` (`blaze_config.hpp:55`) and the SIMD column padding is precisely what LAPACK's `LDA` handles — `DGELS` validates only `LDA >= max(1,M)`, `LDB >= max(1,M,N)` and threads both unchanged through `DGEQRF`/`DORMQR`/`DTRTRS`. New `fn_gels.hpp` + `fn_AR_gels.hpp` + `fn_BZ_gels.hpp` expose `gels(A, B, Work)` for `float`/`double`/`complex`, with caller-owned workspace grown once to the LAPACK-optimal size. Two traps recorded: `ldb` must come from the allocation, never from `max(M,N)` (the underdetermined branch writes rows `M+1..N` and overruns an `M`-row buffer), and the size check on `B` must be `BELFEM_ERROR` rather than `BELFEM_ASSERT` because Blaze padding can make the stride hide an under-sized logical `B` in release. Blaze `polyfit` now solves the tall Vandermonde directly instead of accumulating `VᵀV` + Cholesky: max relative coefficient error on a degree-8 fit drops from 7.1e+01 (no correct digits) to 1.5e-07. Armadillo `polyfit` left on `arma::polyfit` deliberately (SVD-based, tolerates rank deficiency). Verified numerically on both backends; `gelsy`/`gelsd` noted as the addition if a rank-deficient consumer appears. `gesv` and `posv` gained their complex flavors for symmetry (`cgesv_`/`zgesv_`, `cposv_`/`zposv_`) — Armadillo only, since the Blaze wrappers delegate to `blaze::gesv`/`blaze::posv` which were already templated over complex; note the complex `posv` is **Hermitian** positive definite, not complex symmetric.
- [dl20260730_hphi_ts17_controller_strategy.md](dl20260730_hphi_ts17_controller_strategy.md) - **hphiTrun ts17 controller stall: 201-iteration grind diagnosed by 3-AI analysis (read-only); root causes = re-enabled thermal update gate, hysteresis-free Picard↔Newton chattering, ω min-carry ratchet, dead stall guard, target-iterations 100.** The input deck's `update gate : 1e-2` re-activates the knob disabled by default after this exact ts17 pathology; thermal chatters across its 1e-4 switch with the promote/demote ω carry `min(ωN, ωP)` (`cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:678/:687`) ratcheting every Newton entry to the floor; stall band 0.001 dB can never fire on the observed ±0.02–0.1 dB plateau; `mOmega0` hard-coded 1.0 and `mNumIterationsDiv` shared magnetic/thermal + never reset; `initialize_timestep` leaves thermal algorithm and `mThermalFrozen` stale across retries. Ship order: config (drop gate, target 100→20) → slope/projection progress watchdog → thermal flip latch + ω-carry fix → retry hygiene → Newton memoryless line search + Aitken Δ² for the coupling (answer to the α-β-γ tuning question: the AIMD form, not the constants, is the limit). No source edits made.
- [dl20260729_side_connector_fixes_hex8tb.md](dl20260729_side_connector_fixes_hex8tb.md) - **Side connectors: three Claude+Codex+Grok audit/fix rounds on the fuse and SideLayer construction; degenerated HEX8TB wall element landed with Phase-1 mesh wiring.** Order-aware `compute_side_edge_indices`, edge-driven duplicate-safe SideLayer ctor with in-ctor hanging (weights deferred to DofData, verified), periodicity-by-deletion design (hanging outer nodes inherit constraints through sources), HEX8TB = plain-HEX8 node convention with 4 dof edges all +curve, edge-driven element assembly with per-station orientation flag. Memory ownership verified clean 3/3 (mesh owns block, block owns elements, thin shell non-owning). MeshCheckers error loudly on negative-volume HEX8TB instead of node-swapping (would corrupt slot-tied edges). - [dl20260728_side_connector_revival.md](dl20260728_side_connector_revival.md) - **Side-connector campaign restarted: legacy factory code recovered, degenerate 4-virtual-dof wall element concept, side-curve fusing physics settled by 3-AI consensus.** `sideconnectors` branch moved from the stale 2026-05-13 backup (kept in `sc_backup`/`sc_bakup`) onto devel; `create_side_connectors` + 9 subroutines, ThinShell accessors, and the Connector domain types recovered verbatim (uncommitted; MaxwellFactory consumer wiring deliberately left out; defect analysis deferred). Theory verdict (Claude + Codex + Grok, unanimous where it matters): free interior side-curve traces are the natural zero-impedance lateral short (r′→0), NOT insulation — the uncoated slit edge is an essential condition (ALL station side traces = g = φ₀−φ₁), so fusing is required physics, not overconditioning; coated stations instead fuse interiors to one new internal dof `h_in` facing only the wall element's r′ stiffness (never also tied to g). One shared station enumerator, two fuse targets. Buffer-adjacent traces are φ-hung via `create_buffers` (`cl_ThinShellFactory.cpp:1731–1784`), value-at-side-curve probe pending (R0.3). - [dl20260728_tape_user_material_abi.md](dl20260728_tape_user_material_abi.md) - **Tape example's `ybco` user material tagged Buffer instead of HTS: ABI-stale plugin, confirmed by disassembly + git + Codex/Grok audits.** `MatData/build/libmat.so` (built 2026-04-07) registers jc/n/rho through Material vtable slot 22 — which was `set_user_defined_function(MatFunc1)` in April but is `mu(H,T)` today (11 virtuals inserted since: `ae3a6754`, `b524fe08`, `82a1efc1`) — so all registrations are silent no-ops, `have(rho)` stays false, and `ThinShellFactory::create_buffers` (sole criterion, `:1758`) tags the block Buffer. - [dl20260722_coupled_newton_hardening.md](dl20260722_coupled_newton_hardening.md) - **Coupled magneto-thermal solver: from 110 all-Picard iterations to sustained dual Newton, serial + 4-proc.** Root causes closed: the deck's `tolerance switch : 1e-18` sentinel (Newton promotion could never fire), lagged Newton residual reporting (new `mRhsBackup` preserves b across the solve for a true post-update recompute; first attempt without it produced residual≡1.0 — instructive failure recorded), thermal-kernel parallel aura crash (unguarded block lookup for air aura elements from facet neighbors; `key_exists` guard added, secondary continue-skips-slave find pending), PETSc parallel auto-AIJ default. - [dl20260721_thermal_kernel_collapse.md](dl20260721_thermal_kernel_collapse.md) - **Thermal kernel collapse complete (R11 + R12 thermal half): dispatch → `T_h_picard`, 13 legacy `T_h_*` deleted.** `IWG_MaxwellThermal::link_to_group` no longer consults any material at link time — Conductor+ThinShell dispatch the MaxwellData-delegating `T_h_picard` unconditionally (D2 crash class gone), Buffer/Ferro/Air keep `T_phi`; after Christian's verification gate, `mt_thermal_h.cpp` went 1339 → 55 lines (straggler sweep clean) and the §6.1 sign split closed (last legacy `−E·q` mirrors gone). - [dl20260716_spfa_warmstart_cycle_scan.md](dl20260716_spfa_warmstart_cycle_scan.md) - **SPFA churn on double-layer corc gen 6 fixed (solver-side); helix confirmed working.** Christian's field runs: helix clean_spfa works end-to-end (random-looking isolated cut patches are cosmetic — B physical, static condensation adds no DOFs → SPFA is a net win regardless of corc); corc gen 6 churned at 6.39M dequeues / ~37k queue steady state with no verdict. Fixes in `fn_Graph_spfa.cpp`: **BFS spanning-forest warm start** (tree arcs satisfied tightly at init; queue = violated-arc tails; work scales with excess, not range) + **amortized periodic parent-graph cycle scan** (every 4·n+1024 updates, O(1) amortized; catches a cycle within one interval of forming); probe gated to every 65536th dequeue. - [dl20260707_fvm_defect_fixes_and_datastructures.md](dl20260707_fvm_defect_fixes_and_datastructures.md) - **FVM defect fixes D1/D3/D4/D6/D7 landed (Codex-verified) + global-solve data structures decided.** Execution start of `todo/fvm_implementation_plan.md`: slave normal flipped outward in both branches of `compute_facet_normals` (D1) with `SubEdge::master_normal()/slave_normal()` accessors as single source of truth; injective facet key `tA·N+tB` (D6); distributed lookups → `mMesh->facet(id)` (D7); distributed tangent + non-root `set_size` (D4) **plus two NEW latent MPI bugs fixed in the same block** (owned-column copy-loop misalignment; `tMyIDs` request-list reuse without resize — OOB when not-owned > owned); `add_subdirectory(fvm)` + malformed fn deleted (D3, 3rd-order `Tensor` ctor by Christian). Flux measure verified two-source: **`S = t·l` with `l = L/2`** = half the full edge (Aavatsmark 2002 (F0) Eq. 67; Agélas face-measure split `m_σ/card(V_σ)`) — never `|r|` (lives only in `G`). - [dl20260706_side_connector_bridge_formulation.md](dl20260706_side_connector_bridge_formulation.md) - **Side conductivity of HTS tapes: problem statement + edge-wall impedance-bridge formulation (read-only, 3 AI review rounds).** The quench current-escape path around the φ-only buffer (CORC current sharing) cannot be a volume-ρ modification — a φ region has no curl-curl channel and one-layer ρ⊥ cannot cross the buffer — nor XFEM+Nitsche (uniformly-cut edge row has no healthy neighbor; ghost facets verified normal-only), nor extra edge DOFs (approximation-error tool vs a conditioning/topology problem). Recommended model: collapse the plated edge wall into `∫ r′·[[h∥]]·[[v∥]] dy` on the side curve — in the h-formulation a resistor is `R·I(h)·I(v)` with the current a **jump/circulation of H** (Ampère; Nédélec DOFs are circulations — no discrete curl, no new DOFs, so the HEX8TS unwetted-DOF failure cannot recur). Physics reframe: injection into the jacket is via the broad top Ag/Cu interface, not the 20 µm sliver; r′ is slit-edge-interface-controlled (10⁻⁶–10⁻⁴ Ω·m vs 10⁻⁸ metal floor) → calibratable parameter; buffer-pinhole path parameterized independently (same kernel family, on conductor traces — never buffer φ). **Codex-audit-confirmed: `contact_impedance_theory.md` §3.4 keeps the wrong TSA term for transfer-⊥-sheet physics** (R_ct = ρt lives in the dropped in-plane-curl term; the 1/h + h/6 matrices are printed in Alves 2022a; harmlessness for rint is *conditional* — λ_ct spans µm–hundreds of µm against metal sheets — feeds Milestone C). Key discrete finding (audit-hardened, **O1 = BLOCKING**): current BELFEM makes a naive side-curve jump kernel inert — closed side curves deliberately not node-split (`cl_CutFactory.cpp:1546-1598`), air-adjacent shell boundary edges hang on air φ-node differences (`cl_FEM_DofMgr_DofData.cpp:3555-3637`), conductor-air interface kernel disabled (`cl_IWG_Maxwell.cpp:398-401`) → an explicit side-wall trace pair must be plumbed first. 1D transmission-line benchmark: λ = sqrt(r′/(R′₁+R′₂)) ≈ 0.5 mm–10 cm across plausible r′. Plan: `todo/side_connector_effective_resistivity.md` (rev. 3).
- [dl20260706_strumpack_blr_default.md](dl20260706_strumpack_blr_default.md) - **STRUMPACK BLR compression off by default — rank-dependent Newton stall root-caused (experiment-confirmed).** Same 72k-DOF h-φ timestep: clean at 2 procs, mid-Newton explosion at 1 proc, stuck at ~4e-4 floor at 4 procs. Cause: old N≥50k auto-BLR heuristic + STRUMPACK BLR default rel_tol **1e-4** + `solve_internal` returning SUCCESS unconditionally → silently-stagnating GMRES injects ~1e-4 noise into Newton; distributed BLR tile grid makes it rank-dependent; the 1-vs-2-proc paradox was relaxation-controller path divergence seeded by that noise. `compression scheme : off` (works since the S2 fix) restored rank-independent convergence. Change: `AUTOMATIC`/`OFF` → no compression for STRUMPACK; explicit `blr` gets leaf-size heuristic + `set_rel_tol(compression_cutoff()=1e-8)` (first use of that member). MUMPS untouched. Same day: `hatch_small_turtle` removed (8 procs @ 9k DOFs/proc runs cleanly — warning empirically refuted; OpenMP oversubscription turtle stays). Exchange `tmp/ai_exchange/strumpack_mpi_interface.md`.

- [dl20260701_user_material_backend_boundary.md](dl20260701_user_material_backend_boundary.md) - Read-only investigation of why standalone user-defined material compilation is currently not backend-agnostic. Found that `cl_Material.hpp` directly imports `cl_Spline.hpp`, exposes `Vector<real>` in the polynomial API, and inline-dereferences `Spline`; `Spline` exposes `Vector`, `Matrix`, and `SpMatrix`; `SpMatrix` also exposes `Vector`; and `cl_Vector.hpp` only defines `Vector` under `BELFEM_ARMADILLO` or `BELFEM_BLAZE`. The MatData CMake target imports include paths but not backend definitions or third-party usage requirements. Recommendation: short-term propagate the exact BELFEM build configuration to the material target; longer-term split a scalar/plugin-facing material ABI from spline/linalg-heavy framework internals.
