Adopted: shared `feasibility_solve()` core (diagnostic = production minus write-back), `aPeriodicity == true` as hard precondition, `BELFEM_ERROR` guard for the dead null-complex field constructor (zero call sites), corc reclassified as unit-coefficient periodic regression (Phase 0 needs a real coarse-CCT input — Codex catch, verified). Left open: greedy `clean()` termination on feasible inputs (Grok simulation: 0 cycles in ~1700 instances; Codex: monotone-relaxation heuristic; agreed: a hang does **not** certify Regime 2). Plan file updated; no source edits. Same session: `src/homology/doc/thin_cut_nonunit_rectification.md` gained **§1.1–1.3** (geometry of the non-unit pushed object — both sheets land on the *same* conjugated facet, giving one facet with multiplicity 2 and a non-uniform jump `2I`/`I` that breaks the single-duplication scheme; watertight as an integer 2-chain via `dc = 0` with a branch line where sheets separate, not an embedded 2-manifold; per-regime resolvability — Regime 2 resolves only as a multiplicity chain `q_k = p + k·I`, topologically exact but locally blind in the throat, hence refinement-first). Decision (Christian): fix the greedy `clean()` first — **ordered step tracker T1–T9 added** to the plan (solver core → diagnostic → gate → write-back → guards → messaging → error split → tests → validation). Risk-list review: **3 more items closed** (domain scope + numeric width by T1/T4 design; higher-order elements — cochain is corner-edge-only, orders 1–2, `flag_corner_nodes`/`grab_nodes` verified), **1 narrowed after Codex refuted Claude's closure** (Cochain side-state IS read post-clean by `updatekGeneratorsFromHomology`, `cl_Cohomology.cpp:586` → T4 side-state maintenance now mandatory, closes at T4 review). Exchange thread `thin_cut_plan_update_audit` (swept).
- [dl20260701_bfm_parallel_facet_duplication.md](dl20260701_bfm_parallel_facet_duplication.md) - **`.bfm` reload fixed end-to-end: facet triple-reference (D24) + topology-map divergence (D25) — reload now runs serial/2/4/8 procs.** D24 fixed by move-not-copy (`reset_facet_container` in `collect_facets`), dropping the redundant `mThinShells` loop, `create_sidesets(aKeepEmpty)` for BFM load (empty shell husks survive for `read_domain_types`' unguarded id lookup), and Exodus count/populate filter alignment. D25 (found via serial-reload postproc crash "is penta6, expect tet4" + parallel dof "Key 39726 not found"): the load branch ran `Topology::run()` on the *enriched* mesh — buffer block 23 entered the map (Air postproc rule Air∨Buffer mixed PENTA6 into TET4), `detect_sideset_types` mutated cut sidesets Default→Inactive, `groups(Buffer)` went live in the fresh-dead node-flag loop → hanging/dof divergence; fixed by `Topology::run_on_enriched_mesh()` — exclusion sets from `mMesh->thin_shells()` (layer+buffer blocks, tape/ghost sidesets), no re-detect (Christian's synthesize-don't-persist design; φ rule is a pure function of block domain types). Original D24 diagnosis: Worker SIGABRT "container is already allocated" (`cl_Vertex.cpp:216` ← `ProtoMesh::create_facet_extra:1234` ← `Distributor::run:215`) on the CORC `.bfm` load path. **Chain (each link verified):** shared shell↔tape facets serialize once per sideset membership (`populate_facet_data` iterates sidesets) — h5py on `tmp/examples/CORC/corc.bfm`: **16761 facet records, 12285 unique**, tape block byte-identical to the shell block → `create_facets` materializes clones per duplicated ID (`mFacetMap[id]` keeps the last; positional `tGeo` stamp keeps rank-0 asserts green) → distributor ships all copies (index-based bitset) → worker's ID-keyed map double-resolves → second `allocate_facet_container()` aborts. Serial load silently wrong too (clones instead of aliases). **Follow-up (Christian's DIAG in `collect_facets_from_sidesets`):** CutFactory's cohomology doubling/stitch (`duplicate_and_relink_facets`/`restore_thin_shell_sidesets`) **exonerated** — the DIAG series reconstructs exactly (+7582 double, +4454 cuts, −7582 restore). Real cause = **triple reference** from `ThinShellFactory::create` (all in `2dd4f090`, 2025-09-11): stale copies left in `shell_NN` (`collect_facets` copies, never clears) + aggregate pushed to `mSideSets` (`:337`) + `ThinShell` wrapping the same sideset re-added by the `mThinShells` loop in `collect_facets_from_sidesets` (B−A = A−C = 7582). Fresh MPI tolerates the triplication only by accident (last-position-wins index + bitset dedup). Ghost/periodic facets freshly created — no aliasing. `unique()` band-aid insufficient (`.bfm` still saves per-sideset duplicates → crash survives) and hazardous (`unique(Cell<T>)` **sorts** → `Facet*` by heap address → nondeterministic facet order/indices). **Recommended fix at source:** clear `shell_NN` containers in `collect_facets` (move, don't copy) + drop the redundant `mThinShells` loop + revert band-aid; loader-side ID-dedupe kept as fallback. Tracked as **D24** in `todo/meshfile_refactor_plan.md` §4.0.
- [dl20260630_bfm_maxwell_integration.md](dl20260630_bfm_maxwell_integration.md) - **`BfmFile` ↔ `MaxwellFactory` `.bfm` save/load integration — tri-AI audit + fix sprint (D13–D21).** Audited the newly-wired `.bfm` restart path (`hphirun`→`MaxwellFactory::read_mesh`/`create_magnetic_kernel`) across three threads (thin-shell, vertex/curve, maxwell-load/save; Claude+Grok+Codex). **Design decision (D21):** input file stays source of truth; the `.bfm` is *only* an enrichment cache (cohomology/cuts, thin shells, hanging, periodicity) — regular block→material rebuilt from `input.conf` every run; thin-shell *layer* materials ride in the `.bfm`; physical fields + time cursor go in a **separate** restart file (`Mesh::save_fields`/`load_fields`). **Fixed:** D13 `tLabels(n, )` compile blocker; D14 ctor auto-`load()`/`save()` double-execution + `checksum()` null-deref (ctor passive + self-contained `checksum()` + `get()` releases `mOwnMesh`); D15 `save_group_data` dropped `tIDs.push` → empty `"ids"` → 0 blocks/sidesets; D16 `read_mesh` non-void fall-off on first run (restructured, source-load lifted out of `if(exists)`) + D16b base-checksum cached before enrichment; D17 block materials unassigned on load → `assign_materials` "Key not found" crash (ungated `create_block_to_material_map`, called on load path); D18 `create_thinshells` double-create / materials-missing (early-return + hydrate `mMaterialBlockAssignment` from loaded `ThinShell::materials()`) + D18b `++b` decouple; D19 curve duplication on load (`curves().size()==0` guard) + bonus periodicity guard. **D20 retracted** (false positive — `Mesh::checksum()` caches the base value before enrichment; Christian caught it, both auditors missed it). Codex's standout catches: the `Cell(n)`+index-assign reserve misuse, the ctor double-load/save, the curve duplication. Two stale-state corrections went *to* Claude (removed block-material map; D20) — re-read beats memory. **State:** D13–D21 resolved, `.bfm` path structurally complete; not yet exercised by a build+round-trip (R12 next) — globals + field-restart call-site wiring (R10/R11) still open. Exchange threads `thinshell_bfm`/`vertex_curve_bfm`/`maxwell_loadsave` (swept).
- [dl20260628_circuit_docs.md](dl20260628_circuit_docs.md) - **Circuit module usage guide + ngspice parser plan (docs only, no source changed).** Traced `src/circuit` from `ElectricalCircuitFactory` (`hphirun.cpp:60,78`); wrote `src/circuit/doc/circuit_usage_guide.md` (+README) and `todo/ngspice_parser_plan.md` (+index). **Module facts:** MNA solver, **last** node index = ground (`cl_ElectricalCircuit.cpp:31-46`); unknown-current elements are only voltage sources + switches; nine components (resistor/capacitor/inductor/voltage source/current source/switch/diode/superconductor/terminal pair); the `terminal pair` (`FEMTwoTerminals`) is the FEM bridge — relays FEM current, runs **no** BDF, its `order` key is silently ignored, and the factory always tags it `CircuitVoltage` (`:481`; the 2-D `Voltage`/`length` branch `:523` is dead, `mCircuitCurrentBCs` never populated); `input curves:[..]` reference FEM `topology{curves{}}` ids; circuit solves **rank-0 only**, only the OK flag broadcast (`cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:131-161`; `set_circuit` at `:1749`). **A FEM-free runner already exists** — `src/executables/electricalCircuit.cpp` (hardcoded), recomputing MNA only on Δt change and `shift()`ing at step end. **Parser plan answers:** (1) pull lumped R/L/C/V/I/D from a `.cir` via the existing `create_*` (raw SI) — `terminal pair`+`superconductor` need a `* belfem:` extension; (2) minimise `circuit{}` to `file:`; (3) make `electricalCircuit.cpp` file-driven. **Tri-AI audited (Codex+Grok):** both refuted claim F (`solve_circuit()` = Newton loop only, not the per-step driver) → runner section rewritten around the real loop; fixed `set_circuit` citation `:332`→`:1749`, the 2-D duplicate-inputs path, "results broadcast"→OK-flag-only, resistor-floor/BDF/`order`/time-invariant-MNA wordings; extended parser risk list (node-count not max-index, implicit/aliased ground, `MIL`/`A` suffixes, deg→rad phase, SIN `Td`≠`time_offset`, `//`+`.end`, hard-error on `E/F/G/H/B/K/T`). Exchange `tmp/ai_exchange/circuit_ngspice.md` (swept).
- [dl20260627_periodic_bfm.md](dl20260627_periodic_bfm.md) - **Periodicity save/load in `BfmFile` — audited (Claude+Grok+Codex) and completed (R7 done).** Christian wired `save/load_periodicity_data` + per-entity helpers into `BfmFile` and the `ProtoMesh::create_periodicitiy` → `from_proto` reload. Three **CRITICAL** defects, all confirmed by both auditors and fixed: **C1** wrong loader dispatched (`load_hanging_faces()` instead of `load_periodic_faces()`, `:1669`) — and because `group_exists`/`dataset_exists` are both `H5Lexists`, the guard passes on the periodic `"faces"` dataset then `H5Gopen2` fails; **C2** plane matrix `Matrix(3,2)` indexed `(0,k)/(1,k)` for `k<3` → column OOB at `k=2` (plane is master/slave × 3 pts ⇒ `Matrix(2,3)`); **C3** plane loader wrote into default-empty `Cell`s with no `set_size(3)`. Then **Codex's HIGH** (the substantive one): `from_proto` restored the pair *lists* but never set per-entity `periodic()` back-pointers or entity-type flags, while `set_entity_dependencies` hard-requires `A->periodic()!=nullptr` (`cl_Mesh_Periodicity.cpp:88`) and Maxwell calls `update()` unconditionally (throws on restored, guard `:369-370`); **Grok's HIGH**: the `create_periodicitiy` gate keyed on node counts though planes save unconditionally. **Resolution:** pairs now stored by **authoritative partner** (`entity->id(), entity->periodic()->id()`) so loaded `master(k)↔slave(k)` are true partners (sidesteps the independently-ordered-lists hazard, `periodicity.md:88`) ⇒ `mMaster/SlaveSideSets` not serialized; reload via **O1 option (i)** — `from_proto(...,aCrosslinkEntities=true)` → `crosslink` sets bidirectional `set_periodic()` + flags NODE/EDGE/FACE/FACET; periodic DOF constraints (`add_source`) ride back through the hanging-entity data; Maxwell read-side gate now runs `update()`+`set_entity_dependencies()` only when `!node_pairs_restored()` (`cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:481-489`). **Write-side still pending** (R10) — the enriched `.bfm` must be saved *after* `set_entity_dependencies()` so slave sources exist to persist. Also landed: abstract+orphan node save/load (ID-keyed, `:147-211`). Plan: R7 done, O1/O2 resolved, inventory rows 8+15 done. Exchange `tmp/ai_exchange/periodic_bfm.md` (swept).

- [dl20260626_controller_newton_escalation.md](dl20260626_controller_newton_escalation.md) - **Picard-breakdown → Newton escalation in `Controller` (VERIFIED working).** CORC at Δt_max=50ms: at the current-ramp advance the magnetic Picard iteration takes one good step then stalls at a residual ~5 decades above `mEpsilonSwitch`, so the normal Picard→Newton promotion never fires; the controller then halves Δt every retry with the residual floor barely moving (×128 in Δt ≈ 5 dB — **Δt-insensitive**, a structural/nonlinear-stiffening wall, not a timestep-size problem). Tri-AI consensus (Claude+Codex+Grok, `tmp/ai_exchange/corc_controller_stall.md`); reconfirmed `residual 9.000000` is a display clamp (`std::min(ε,9)`), true value = dB column. **Fix:** new `try_escalate_to_newton()` pins the solver to the configured Newton tangent once per attempt (`mForceNewton`/`mNewtonEscalated`, ω restarted from `mOmega0`) before cutting Δt; handoff condition (`iterate_coupled`+`iterate_magnetic`) gains the `mForceNewton` override; both reset sites + the `magnetic_stagnation_forces_reset` Picard branch route through it. First test showed the banner but Newton never iterating — **two further guards** (both gated on `mForceNewton`): the in-loop line-search reset no longer guillotines Newton's first over-relaxed overshoot (damps via backtracking, resets only at `tBacktracks>=8`), and the post-loop "+10 dB past grace" reset is suppressed so Newton gets several damped iterations (termination falls back to the stall guard + `mMaxNumIterations` + NaN). Christian confirmed on his working tree: damped Newton now engages at the same Δt and carries the step. Only active when `algorithm:Newton`. Builds clean (`-Werror -pedantic-errors`). Claude's local repro stayed blocked by a pre-existing periodic-mesh setup crash (`PeriodicityFactory::map_facets`, facet 49349 no partner) on this bfmfile tree — unrelated. Deferred: `mDeltaTimeMin` floor + Δt-insensitivity detector in `reset_timestep()`. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260622_picard_backtracking_linesearch.md](dl20260622_picard_backtracking_linesearch.md) - Added a **revert-if-worse backtracking line search** to `Controller::iterate_coupled()` to tame the HTS power-law Picard oscillation (60–72 iters/timestep, residual blow-ups to ~5e4–4e7). **Literature (non-Messe):** confirmed the no-resistivity buffer is a φ-region by design (`cl_IWG_Maxwell.cpp:257-258` routes Air+Buffer to `phi_*`; Arsenault 2021) and *not* the nonlinear culprit; the real cause is power-law non-coercivity (Dular 2021 §V: differential resistivity → 0 as J → 0), and the fix is globalization (Bathe §8.4 line search / Deuflhard damped-Newton monotonicity). **ρ_min analysis:** BELFEM already has Dular's two-limit regularization (floor `mRhoMin`/`gRhoMin`=1e-16, parallel-ρ_n ceiling + `clamp(·,gRhoMin,gRhoMax=1e10)` at `mt_maxwell_h.hpp:35`); with the run's real `J_c=1e10, n=20, E_c=1e-4` (from `input.conf`) the floor crossover is **0.785·J_c** (just below the loss band, `E_floor<1% E_c`, ~0.5 decade headroom) → floor is well-placed and *not* the lever; the oscillation is the intrinsic `ρ∝J^19` sensitivity in [0.8,1.1]·J_c. **Change:** snapshot `field_data` **and** `dof->value()` (the latter is the relaxed-update `x_k` baseline at `cl_FEM_DofMgr_SolverData.cpp:2157`, which a field-only revert misses), accept when `log10(ε) < log10(mEpsilon0)+0.3` (tolerate ≤2× worse — Picard is non-monotone), else revert both + halve ω; self-terminates as ω→ω_min, 8-trial cap → existing `reset_timestep()`. **Debug arc:** the first two attempts were silent no-ops — `tLogEpsilon` seeded to `REAL_MAX` made trial 1 always accept (revert branch dead). Tell-tale: a `residual 9.000000 ( 47.62 dB )` line is self-inconsistent (`10·log10(9)=9.5dB`), proving `9.000000` is a display clamp while the blown-up step (~5.8e4) was printed/accepted — a working revert would suppress it. Fixed baseline→`mEpsilon0`, reject-if-worse, cap, removed a counter-walking `--mIteration`. `-fsyntax-only -Werror` clean; needs `make reset` + runtime check; `0.3` is the knob. Uncommitted.
- [dl20260622_mumps_error_messages.md](dl20260622_mumps_error_messages.md) - Overhauled `MUMPS::error_message` against the **5.9.0** user guide and wired in a proper warning path. **Bugs fixed** in the old `-1..-53` table (transcribed from an older manual, wording otherwise kept): `-2` (was "matrix size"/tested `INFO(4)`; it's NNZ-out-of-range, N is the separate `-16`), `-5`/`-7` (now apply the negative-`INFO(2)`⇒×1e6 size rule), `-9` (read `INFO(3)`; the missing-entry count is `INFO(2)`), `-11` (non-positive branch left a literal `%i` unsubstituted; `LWK_USER`→`LWKUSER`), `-22` (corrected the pointer-array label table — `SHUR`→`SCHUR`, `LISTVAR_SHUR`, stray `R`, `IRN_loc/JCN_loc`→`…or A_loc` — and added entries 17/18). **Factual** identifier/index fixes: `-27` `NRHS+1`, `-28` `IRHS_PTR(1)`, `-30` `SCHUR_LLD`, `-32` `NRHS`, `-36` `ICNTL(25)`, `-41` `LWKUSER`, `-44` `ICNTL(31)`, `-45` `NRHS`, `-47`/`-48` reworded; prose typos swept. **Added** codes `-54..-90` + `-800` (simplified per the deliberate "only expose ICNTL 4/7/10/11/14/29/33/35" policy — `mumpstools.f90:269-324`); **deliberately omitted** `-92,-100..-107` (GPU/cuBLASXt/XKBlas/rank-revealing — unreachable; improved `default:` prints the real `INFO(1)`). **New `MUMPS::warning_message(int_t*, Cell<string>&)`** bitwise-decodes the summed positive warnings (`+1/+2/+4/+8/+16`) one line each, suppressing the `INFO(2)` detail when both detail-bearing bits (`+1`,`+16`) are set (ambiguous attribution). Call sites in `free()`+both `solve()` overloads now split error vs warning cleanly. **Codex caught** that `mumpstools_free_solver` returned only scalar `INFO(1)` (leaving `mInfo[1..]` stale → garbage INFO(2) in free-phase messages) → fixed the Fortran wrapper to return full `INFO(1:40)` (`dimension(40)` + separate `ierr` for `MPI_BARRIER`, mirroring the solve wrapper), C decl `int_t&`→`int_t*`, call site `mInfo(0)`→`mInfo.data()`. Codex audited both the messages and the interop change — high confidence, no blockers. `mpicxx -fsyntax-only -Werror` passes with/without `-DBELFEM_MUMPS`; needs a full `make reset` to rebuild static libs. **Addendum:** the user's post-rebuild convergence win (warning `+8` gone) was traced **not** to this diagnostics work but to the staged-but-never-compiled `ICNTL(14)` memory-relaxation `0→30` fix (`init_defaults`, the `dl20260620` `-9`/`+8` remedy) — HEAD=0, index=30, our edits unstaged on top; plain `make` never rebuilt the static lib, so this task's `make reset` finally activated it. That `ICNTL(14)=30` is still uncommitted (not in HEAD).
- [dl20260622_powerlaw_merge_resolution.md](dl20260622_powerlaw_merge_resolution.md) - Resolved the `periodic_new`→`devel` merge conflict in the HTS power-law resistivity functions. The branches diverged in **opposite** directions: `periodic_new` outsourced the bodies to a new `powerlaws.hpp` (cleaner asserts + a fixed copy-paste assert message) and ends `cl_Material.hpp` with `#include "powerlaws.hpp"`; `devel` kept them inline and carried the one genuine logic change (`cf4d8c76`, "user-defined Jc(B,theta)"). The botched working-tree resolution had kept `devel`'s inline bodies **and dropped the include**, orphaning `powerlaws.hpp` (included nowhere → no duplicate-def error, but the whole refactor silently discarded). **Per-function whitespace-insensitive 3-way diff** (ancestor `d2e6306d`) proved the math is identical everywhere — so "let `cl_Material.hpp` supersede wholesale" would have re-introduced the assert bug + deleted doxygen. **Fix:** keep `periodic_new`'s `powerlaws.hpp` as base; port *only* `cf4d8c76` = (A) 3 missing overloads `n(B,θ)`/`jc(B,θ)`/`jc(B,θ,x,y,z,t)` + (B) 8 field-dependent functions switched `n = constant_property(n)` → `mNFunction->eval(normB,angleNxB)`; rebuild `cl_Material.hpp` = `periodic_new` + 3 declarations + restored include. **Null-safety hardening** (each new overload mirrors its T-sibling): `jc(B,θ)` now uses the `?: constant_property(jc)` fallback — removes a real release-mode null-deref (`have(jc)` is true for a plain-constant jc where `mJcFunction==nullptr`, and the as-ported `assert(have(jc))` compiles out). Verified: no markers, braces balanced, decl↔def parity 38/38, `mpicxx -fsyntax-only` exit 0. Merge left **open/uncommitted**.
- [dl20260622_thin_cut_nonunit_note_and_plan.md](dl20260622_thin_cut_nonunit_note_and_plan.md) - **Docs + planning only (no source changed).** Turned the 2026-06-12 non-unit thick-cut analysis (`|c(e)| ≥ 2`, coarse CCT) into a shareable peer note `src/homology/doc/thin_cut_nonunit_rectification.md` and a phased implementation plan `todo/thin_cut_nonunit_rectification_implementation.md`. **Literature:** reconciled the note against the newly-added topology cluster + Gross & Kotiuga + CLRS + Haken + Dular 1997/1999; **fixed a wrong library DOI** (`dular1999` → `10.1109/20.767308`). **Dular cross-check:** confirmed the continuous foundation (`c − dθ` ↔ curl-kernel freedom; jump = transport current, binary `q_i`; Dular cuts are cohomology-lineage, *not* XFEM). **Two-AI audit:** Codex caught a real §3 error (feasibility L∞ test conflated with the weighted-L1 objective — "feasibility is the `w ≡ 1` special case" was false; Grok missed it); both + user flagged the undefined `{0,13,…}` 6-bit edge bitmask (now decoded with a table) and the mis-stated non-unit failure path (magnitude-blind `determine_cut_case_3d`; `weight()=0` drop); added a Notation table (kept `θ` distinct from physical `φ`; defined `d` = coboundary). **Implementation design:** the rectification is a graph algorithm on the **1-skeleton only**, run on mesh `Node`/`Edge` + `Cochain` directly (not `graph::Vertex`), with solver state in **external index-keyed arrays** (`Vertex` untouched — rejected `mLevel` reuse and an `mData` swap), periodic **quotient** via `periodic()` fold; phase 0 = a read-only feasibility diagnostic (QB). Codex prose-polished the plan. **Addendum (same day):** design iterated (no compaction/quotient map — key by `node->index()`, inline `periodic()` fold, no `reinterpret_cast`) and the plan **audited by Codex + Grok** before coding — core design held; adopted realization fixes (generator loop, `original_edges()` domain filter, master∪slave quotient adjacency, edge-ID certificate + folded-self-loop = length-1 cert, Cochain side-state, `BELFEM_ERROR` guards, `Cell`/ring-buffer + int64). No source modified.
- [dl20260622_meshfile_refactor_plan.md](dl20260622_meshfile_refactor_plan.md) - **Planning only.** Investigation + plan (`todo/meshfile_refactor_plan.md`) for the broken `.bfm` mesh save/load, refactored around `ProtoMesh`/`Protoshell` to enable restart-from-last-timestep with a fully enriched mesh (skip cohomology/cut/thin-shell factories on reload). **Findings (tri-AI, verified):** the enriched BFM write is *commented out* (`cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:469-478`) and the reload path half-built (`save_thin_shell_facets` writes a `tapes` group nothing reads); the `ProtoMesh` ID-keyed reconstruction engine already exists (MPI-only today) and is the right spine; **element type is not deterministically rebuildable** (TET20/HEX20 collide at 20 nodes `meshtools.cpp:1181-1184`, HEX8TS unhandled) → must-save; **periodicity reload is a hard error not silent loss** (`PeriodicityFactory.cpp:320,359-370`); the current writer is pervasively **index-keyed** and only round-trips because serial load preserves order. Deliverable: 23-row gap table (a/b/c + citation each), 13-step ordered todo (R0-R12), 8 open questions, proposed ID-keyed HDF5 schema. No source modified.
- [dl20260620_side_connector_removal.md](dl20260620_side_connector_removal.md) - Removed the dead **side-connector** thin-shell feature (deemed unphysical 2026-06-05; code removal had been pending). Stripped `DomainType::LeftCoating`/`RightCoating`/`InterfaceTsConnector` + all their switch-cases across ~11 files, the `h_penalty` IWG, and the now-orphaned `h_tb`/`h_tb_t` temperature-bulk matrices. **Kept** (working / needed later): `compute_binomial_vectors`, the ghost-facet stabilization (`h_ghost`/`create_ghost_facets`/`DomainType::Ghost`), `cl_EF_HEX8TS`, `cl_EdgeCutter`. 0 references remain; all touched TUs compile. (Mid-task correction: ghost facets are *working* — the side connectors were the dead end, not the ghost facets.)
- [dl20260620_periodic_parallel_source_closure.md](dl20260620_periodic_parallel_source_closure.md) - **Parallel CORC resolved.** Continues the 0618 distribution work. **(1)** Re-diagnosed `element id 0`: NOT a distribution drop — the buffer thin-shell layer (Block 23) is tagged `Buffer` not `ThinShell`, so `compute_element_volumes` loop A double-counts it with loop B (`owned-in-UNTAGGED` exactly matched the mismatch). Fix (Christian): loop A skips `ThinShell || Buffer`. The 0618 array-sizing fix was reverted as a band-aid. **(2)** The real distribution bug (5b): the pre-aura edge/face `select_sources` loops in `select_entities` were dead (gated on `is_flagged()` before any element was flagged), so hanging EDGE/FACE/FACET sources were never ghosted (29 missing on procs 1/3). Fixed with a **source-closure fixpoint** (`select_sources` on all set node/edge/face/facet until convergence) — with the key correction that the geometric aura stays a **single pass** (putting it in the fixpoint walks the whole mesh and hangs; a source only needs to exist as a basis, not its element neighborhood). 0 missing after; parallel runs 58+ timesteps and tracks serial. **(3)** MUMPS `-9`/`+8` were red herrings: ICNTL(14)=0 left MUMPS's too-small default (→ force 30%), and the wrapper aborted on positive=warning INFO (→ log-and-continue, error only on `<0`). The **serial baseline stagnates identically** with buffer+ghost-stab → the remaining convergence stagnation is a **formulation issue, not distribution**. Tri-AI audited (sound for CORC); `#DIAG-5A` converted to a debug-only regression invariant; `#DIAG-B*` stripped
- [dl20260618_periodic_parallel_distribution.md](dl20260618_periodic_parallel_distribution.md) - Two mesh-distribution fixes that get the z-periodic CORC reproducer through MPI on 4 procs (serial solved earlier today). **Fix 1** (`cl_Mesh_Distributor.cpp`, Codex+Grok VERIFIED high): a periodic slave facet hangs on its master facet (a FACET-typed source), but the owned-facet loop never called `select_sources(tFacet)`, so the master facet wasn't ghosted → `invalid facet id 87025` in `create_t_matrices`. Added the one call. (Auditors also refuted my related claim that the facet/face positional pairing was buggy — `map_facets` builds those arrays index-aligned and `match_nodes` relies on it; left unchanged.) **Fix 2** (`cl_FEM_Kernel.cpp`): `compute_element_volumes` sized the per-proc exchange arrays from pass-1 block+thin-shell counts but filled them from `tMesh->elements()` (no thin-shell elements) → trailing zero ids sent to master → `invalid element id 0` (diagnostic-proven: rank 1 alloc 34392, fill 32518, zeros from pos 32518). Sized the arrays from the fill enumeration; fixed master-side `tProcVolumes` sizing. Behavior-preserving. **Now:** parallel reaches `MUMPS::solve()`, fails `-1` on proc 3 — a separate distributed-solver issue; the wrapper only surfaces rank 0's INFO so proc 3's real code is hidden. Next: print MUMPS INFO on all ranks
- [dl20260618_audience_tiering.md](dl20260618_audience_tiering.md) - Refactored the AI-collaboration rules so **audience** (reader) is the organizing axis: AI-only artifacts (the exchange — ephemeral, machine-parseable, disposable) vs AI+human artifacts (`./todo/`, `./devlog/` — durable, curated), with a **distillation** step lifting signal into the devlog before the AI-only scratch is swept. Edited `doc/ai_collaboration_protocol.md` (7 changes incl. a new Audience column in §10), `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and both wrappers (`ask_codex.sh`/`ask_grok.sh` now resolve `<slug>` from `$AI_EXCHANGE_SLUG` → `sess_<CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID>` → `scratch`, verified to propagate to the subprocess; create-on-demand; instruct the auditor to read the existing thread). `tmp/ai_exchange/` git-ignored; migration forward-only (legacy file frozen). - [dl20260618_periodic_corc_solves.md](dl20260618_periodic_corc_solves.md) - 🎉 MILESTONE: the z-periodic CORC reproducer now runs the full pipeline (cuts → cohomology → thin-cut duplication/relink → periodic rebuild → assembly → nonlinear solve) and produces a physical HTS J/Jc distribution. Step 9 (Option 2) validated end to end: key periodic facets/edges by `node->original()` (induced-via-sources dups never need to be matched periodic nodes), plus the `set_entity_dependencies` fix from positional edge pairing to the authoritative `A->periodic()` pointer + original-aware orientation assert + release-active guards. Joint effort — Christian (physics + key calls), Claude Opus (diagnosis/probes/impl), Codex (precision audits, caught the M-existence and edge-pairing flaws), Grok (third-voice verification), Fable (last week's salvaged Step-5 drafts)
- [dl20260617_periodic_facetmap_rootcause.md](dl20260617_periodic_facetmap_rootcause.md) - Root cause (Codex+Grok CONFIRMED, high confidence) of the Maxwell-pass `create_facet_map` "Node N is not flagged" crash: the periodic master seam facets carry 178 distinct **unpaired** cut-duplicate nodes (the one-sided-jump dups from 6b's exactly-one-bit branch — created with no `set_periodic`/no backup), and `restore_node_pairs` rebuilds `master_nodes` purely from the **paired** node backup, so those 178 are absent from `master_nodes` and the facet-map invariant correctly fires. It's a list-overwrite, not a relink; 6c (`mPairVerdict`) is built but never consumed. This is the facet-level manifestation of the deferred one-sided-jump policy. Fix is a design call (A: exclude unpaired-dup seam facets from matching / B: cut-aware pairing space / C: implement 6c). Diagnosis only — no behavior changed; probes 5j/5f/5g/5i left in place. Christian had parked the facet-based `match_nodes_and_edges` refactor and reverted to backup-restore + old `match_edges` with a `check_health()` detector

Builds clean (debug+release); the new error never fired. VALIDATION BLOCKED: the handed-over `input.conf` is a non-periodic variant (no `topology{periodic{source;target}}`), so the run (clean to timestep 33) never exercised the periodic path (#DIAG 5f 0 calls). Also cleared a chain of input/material parse failures (bearing `node`→`nodes`; copper/silver/ybco missing `builtin` keys; hastelloy/magnesia colon-form not registering) that look like an `fd46a497` materials-refactor regression vs the config. - [dl20260614_b3_kernel.md](dl20260614_b3_kernel.md) - Read-only source-grounded whitepaper for Tier B3 (FEM kernel: assembly, DOF management, static condensation, nonlinear/time solve). Headline findings: the kernel fully implements consistent-tangent Newton-Raphson (assemble_newton adds IWG dJ/dx, condensed as tTdJT; Maxwell computes the tangent) but it is DORMANT for the magnetic equation (every timestep forces Picard; the residual "tolerance switch" promotion is commented out; no runtime re-promotion - Codex-confirmed tree-wide), so the magnetic solve runs effectively Picard; static condensation applies T^T*{J,rhs,dJdx}*T per-element via one shared T-matrix for hanging-node AND cohomology-cut/abstract DOFs, but the multi-interface edge cascade is linear-edge-only behind NDEBUG-compiled-away asserts; zero ctest-level kernel coverage. Independent cross-check by CODEX (budget allowed)
- [dl20260614_b3d_homology.md](dl20260614_b3d_homology.md) - Read-only source-grounded whitepaper for Tier B3d (homology/cohomology cut layer): H^1 cohomology of the phi-region (Air+Buffer+Ferro) as integer edge 1-cochains via Smith Normal Form (Kaczynski-Mischaikow-Mrozek), four CutAlgorithms (Pellikka/CCR/PellikkaGeneralized use SNF; BeltedTree is a separate spanning-tree path), thick->thin cut conversion with node-duplication phi-jump, "conjugate edge" as doc-only term realized as cut-case->opposite facet, periodic slave-collapse, zero topological-correctness tests, and fragile/dead paths (clean() greedy, |coeff|>1 unsupported, clean_bfs dead). Independent cross-check by GROK (not Codex - budget); BeltedTree/Generalized-Pellikka provenance gaps flagged
- [dl20260614_b3b_shape_functions.md](dl20260614_b3b_shape_functions.md) - Read-only source-grounded whitepaper for Tier B3b (FEM interpolation layer: nodal Lagrange + edge Nedelec shape functions, reference->physical mapping, integration-data assembly); independent cross-check confirms end-to-end max usable Nedelec/h-phi order = 2 (symmetric mesh+interpolation cap; order-2 elements unvalidated/untested), nodal Lagrange covers full order 1-4 with HEX64 cubic not quartic, EF_LINE3 dead and HEX8TS live-but-deprecated, and no Heaviside enrichment anywhere in src/fem

- [dl20260324_test_suite_bugs.md](dl20260324_test_suite_bugs.md) — All 15 bugs found and fixed during the test suite implementation campaign (modules 1–10)
- [dl20260805_gasmodels_departure_and_lowT_generator.md](dl20260805_gasmodels_departure_and_lowT_generator.md) — gas models return to `src/physics`: `*dep0` established as a deliberate convention (decision still open), own 68-species `thermo.inp`/`trans.inp` built from CEA with fitted cryogenic intervals, deuterium for fusion, `USE_GASMODELS` decoupled from `USE_NONFREE`, gas tests revived (15/19) and five `src` defects fixed; jury round on the Spline ExtraMode fix
- [dl20260816_spmatrix_parent_child.md](dl20260816_spmatrix_parent_child.md) — SpMatrix parent/child structure sharing for the SolverData matrix pairs: three jury rounds (7 P0s found and fixed), verify-only linked load, int_t consistency through the solver `initialize` vtable and the PARDISO shim, 14 new unit tests; DR-74/DR-75 opened
- [dl20260818_jacobian_init_quadratic.md](dl20260818_jacobian_init_quadratic.md) — `DynamicBitset::where()` scans the full bitset once per DOF inside `populate_graph`, making Jacobian init O(N²) on rank 0; measured 37.8 s/pass at 2.1e6 DOFs vs 0.65 s for a test-and-set rewrite; same pattern found in `Kernel::partition_mesh` and the `symrcm` disconnected fallback
- [dl20260818_dynamicbitset_summary_bitmap.md](dl20260818_dynamicbitset_summary_bitmap.md) — two-level summary bitmap makes `DynamicBitset::where()`/`reset()` O(k); 59.3 s → 0.394 s per `populate_graph` pass at 2.1e6 bits; `flip()` underflow, two ctz-fallback bugs and the lock-hash sentinel fixed; mutable `data()` privatised; §2.12 test suite added
- [dl20260818_spmatrix_accessor.md](dl20260818_spmatrix_accessor.md) — SpMatrix Phase A+B: `index()` int_t truncation, sentinel slot (real ASan-confirmed heap overflow), base-agnostic matvec (1.83x), de-virtualized `position()` (1.3x); the spec's hybrid linear search measured and rejected
- [dl20260818_doxygen_site_structure.md](dl20260818_doxygen_site_structure.md) — three-AI sweep of the generated Doxygen site and the R1–R17 campaign that followed. `IMPLICIT_DIR_DOCS`, `MARKDOWN_ID_STYLE` and a hardcoded `HAVE_DOT` — three defaults that moved under a 1.8.16-era template — had detached all 24 module index pages, killed 222 in-page links and left every diagram unrendered; **1471 errors + 232 warnings → 0/0**, 0 → 2737 graphs, a 30-group API topic tree built from nothing, class descriptions 26/318 → 146/318, `src/linalg` documented for the first time (36 headers), the logo linked to belfem.lbl.gov via a build-time-generated header. Three Codex prose sweeps caught nine technical errors including a source bug; two further code defects came out of reading bodies to document them; `eigen` gained a tested `aAbortOnComplex` flag and an `eigen_sym` sibling. `make check` 12/12
- [dl20260820_alpha_from_cp.md](dl20260820_alpha_from_cp.md) — alpha(T) rebuilt on cp(T) through the Grueneisen equation of state across nine materials: the `y0 == y1` third-law constraint on the expansion Beziers forced alpha proportional to T, wrong by 10.5x at 20 K for copper and 18.6x for aluminum; replaced below a split temperature by alpha = C(T)*cp(T) with a `dln(C)/dT > 0` guard and a turning-point fallback. Ten defects fixed in the first implementation (four fatal) plus a wrong `Bezier::d3ydx3`; the logged Grueneisen diagnostic found Lead's frozen E(T), Indium's 27%-low expansion curve (refitted, rms 16x better), Nickel's factor-of-ten `ref_density` and Iron's never-called `create_alpha()`. Chromium's exclusion imposed then lifted on evidence; Aluminum and Chromium added as unregistered materials. Not compiled
- [dl20260825_gasmodels_const_correctness.md](dl20260825_gasmodels_const_correctness.md) — const accessors and mutable scratch across the whole gasmodels hierarchy; the compiler used as the oracle in both directions, 10 unnecessary `mutable` found by a removal sweep, a malformed Nitrogen initializer list fixed and `cl_GM_EoS_Nitrogen.cpp` shown to be unbuildable at HEAD and absent from CMakeLists. All 14 module and 19 test TUs syntax-clean; `make check` not run (USE_GASMODELS=OFF in both trees)
- [dl20260825_gastables_const_correctness.md](dl20260825_gastables_const_correctness.md) — `RefGas` made const-correct (100 signatures) with **zero** `mutable` needed: unlike the mixture layer, the species layer does not memoize. `find_*_poly` now returns const poly pointers. Plus a recorded read-only audit of `archive/{channel,combustion,boundarylayer,engine}` for callers relying on `Gas::M()`/`R()` returning a live reference — none exist; search terms and false-positive traps written down so the negative survives. Then, on that evidence: `Gas::M`/`R` switched to returning `real` by value, paired const/writable overloads added for `Gas::data` and the three `RefGas` splines, and a `const RefGas *` read-only handle convention applied inside the const methods; positive and negative compile probes confirm the const is load-bearing
- [dl20260825_nitrogen_eos.md](dl20260825_nitrogen_eos.md) — `EoS_Nitrogen` completed, wired into the factory and built for the first time. Six defects in the residual derivatives (two of which compiled and were silently wrong, one out by ~600x) fixed and verified against finite differences at R²=1. Saturated liquid density was +0.47 % too dense with vapour density and vapour pressure both correct; localised by measurement to the high-delta terms, then confirmed against Span et al. Table 17 as a single digit — `mJ(19)` was 4.0 and should be 6.0. After the fix, agreement with the paper's own ancillary is 0.004 %. 24/24 gas tests pass (31/31 after the 2026-08-26 test round, which also put methane back on the CEA reference scale)
- [dl20260825_lemmon_jacobsen_transport.md](dl20260825_lemmon_jacobsen_transport.md) — viscosity and thermal conductivity for N2 and O2 after Lemmon & Jacobsen 2004, as **one** class parameterized by fluid (the correlation covers N2/Ar/O2/air with a single functional form) rather than one class per species. Architecture follows `HelmholtzTransport_Methane`, equations do not — Friend's model is unrelated. Reproduces the paper's Table V verification values to the printed digits for both fluids; the one inexact entry is O2 at its critical point (+0.165 % on lambda, where the critical enhancement is ~90 % of the value). Found: `Helmholtz::v()` cannot invert p->rho within ~0.1 % of Tc for any Helmholtz fluid. H2 viscosity added the same session from Muzny et al. 2013 **with the 2022 erratum** (three corrections, without which the correlation is wrong; Table 3 also mis-extracts and was confirmed numerically) — reproduces the erratum's test values to 0.001 %. H2 thermal conductivity added from Assael et al. 2011 — that paper **does** distinguish the spin isomers, so `lambda()` is isomer aware where `mu()` is not; 5 of 6 Table 7 points to 0.01 %, the sixth being the one state where the critical enhancement matters (+1.9 % normal, +5.9 % para, traced to the enhancement being fitted against a pre-Muzny background viscosity). All four Helmholtz fluids now answer both `mu()` and `lambda()`; coverage matrix and the isomer/near-critical caveats documented in the module README
- [dl20260827_shared_libs_darwin.md](dl20260827_shared_libs_darwin.md) — one `libbelfem` from per-module OBJECT libraries, `USE_SHARED_LIBS`, `make install` (headers, `share/`, `examples/` via `install(DIRECTORY)`), `@loader_path`/`$ORIGIN` RPATH, install-time re-sign, compiled-in data dir; every gate run on macOS x86_64 with evidence. Found: `gComm`/`gLog` live in `main()` (dylib needs `-undefined dynamic_lookup` until they move, O7) and a moved install needs `BELFEM_DATA` (O8). Also 15 pre-existing printf format/argument mismatches fixed. Linux R4/R9/R11/R12 outstanding
- [dl20260827_shared_libs_install_linux.md](dl20260827_shared_libs_install_linux.md) — the Linux half of the same plan: `USE_SHARED_LIBS=ON` builds `libbelfem.so.0.9.0` with clean `ldd` and `make check` 12/13 (the one failure, `Genome.RandomizeLogScaleBranch`, could not depend on linkage — both library types come from the same objects — and was fixed the same session: `Genome::get_values()` now clamps the decoded value to the caller's bounds, which `set_values()` and `randomize()` already did, so the log branch no longer overshoots `max` by a few ulp; gated differentially with a standalone probe); `make install` to a scratch prefix runs the shipped `2D_Tapestack` deck with `BELFEM_DATA` **unset**, and a `DESTDIR`-staged tree, once moved, resolves `libbelfem.so.0` through `$ORIGIN/../lib64` and runs with `BELFEM_DATA` set — O8 reproduces identically on ELF. `lib64` vs `lib` confirmed via `GNUInstallDirs`. The `::` in the build-tree RUNPATH was chased and cleared: it is CMake fencing off the toolchain-appended portion, not a BELFEM bug, and the installed binaries do not carry it. New finding **O9** — both plugin templates put `${BELFEM_DIR}/include` alone on the include path while headers install as `include/belfem/<module>/…`, so no plugin can compile against an install; their backend-detection block is dead code. Gate only, no source or CMake edits. R11 and R12 outstanding
- [dl20260828_dr37_r13_backendfree_template.md](dl20260828_dr37_r13_backendfree_template.md) — **DR-37 struck (both gates run), then a three-AI round on the template and the repairs it forced.** R12's in-tree gate executed on both backends with the strip proven exact and both `#error` guards proven non-vacuous; R13 closed by **deletion, not implementation**. The jury round then found the template's own documented workflow had never worked — `BELFEM_DIR` was a `CACHE` variable without `FORCE`, so the placeholder written on the first configure shadowed every later edit; that also indicts the R13 gate, which passed only via `-DBELFEM_DIR=`. Second measured find: `cl_Material.hpp` pushes diagnostics on `__GNUC__` but popped on `BELFEM_GCC`, so every plugin TU silently lost `-Wunused-*`/`-Wformat` (0 vs 2 warnings); fixed in the header. Fixing the sibling `UserLibraryTemplate.cmake` surfaced the mirror-image defect — its include set reaches `src/linalg`, which IS backend-gated, so it needed exactly the `target_compile_definitions` DR-37 R13 asked for: **the dead block had been in the wrong file all along.** Also: installed-tree include path fixed in both templates and gated against 626 staged headers, `SHARED`→`MODULE`, Windows `.dll` claim dropped (both loaders are POSIX `dlopen`), `GNUInstallDirs`, four false comments, `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE` defaulted. Codex's ABI finding cited a **fabricated** line (`lessons_learned_evidence.md:408` is INC-316); its `CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS OFF` advice was refuted and not applied. O9 closed; DR-131 (macOS gate owed) and DR-132 (no ABI handshake) filed. macOS and Intel untested; `make check` not run
- [dl20260828_db2exo_converter.md](dl20260828_db2exo_converter.md) — audit and repair of the new `db2exo` database→Exodus converter, then a jury round on the repair. The draft's grid check compared against `num_nodes()` ( total ) instead of `num_nodes_vector()` ( per-dim ); `index_t` is unsigned, so every entry wrapped to ~4.29e9 and **every table after the first was silently dropped** — `sp-ap`/`sst-1` converted with `jc` and without `n`, which is the symptom Christian hit. Five more in the same file including a release-only short-buffer write ( the only downstream check is a `BELFEM_ASSERT` ). Codex then found `order == 0` reaching `( points - 1 ) % order` **before** the order check, because `mNumElementsPerDim` is declared ahead of `mElementType` — SIGFPE where a clean error was intended — plus self-overwrite of an `.exo` input under `EX_CLOBBER` and dotfiles collapsing to `.exo`. Grok, blocked 13 min by a host podman-socket sandbox fault, recovered and read the **live** tree, so it audited the fixes: `collect_names` had its body guarded but not its `H5L_info2_t` **signature**, a tree-wide break of `USE_HDF5=OFF` reproduced as a compile gate and now a green regression gate. It also refuted my own comment claiming a string compare caught self-overwrite ( `file.EXO` on a case-insensitive volume ). Order-3 corner-connectivity degradation ruled acceptable ( 2026-08-29 ) and implemented as a warning; Codex's order-1 remesh declined, not deferred. Never built or run
- [dl20260831_dr116_hdf5_fixed_width_file_types.md](dl20260831_dr116_hdf5_fixed_width_file_types.md) — DR-116: all five HDF5 writers create datasets with fixed-width file types and write through the NATIVE memory type, closing the row's latent big-endian clause. Integer widths dispatch on `sizeof`/`std::is_signed` rather than on the C type name, because `long` is 8 bytes under LP64 and 4 under LLP64 and `char` is signed on x86 but unsigned on ARM. The change is a **no-op on disk** and that is the evidence: `H5Tequal` holds for all 15 specialized types and old-path vs new-path files are byte-for-byte identical (`cmp`, `h5diff -v` 0 differences), so the reader provably receives unchanged input. The register's own gate turned out to be a **null gate** — `h5dump -H` already printed `H5T_STD_U64LE` before the fix, because a native LE copy carries that exact description — and was replaced. Uncovered and fixed a real reader defect: `load_bool_from_file` passed the FILE type as the `H5Dread` MEMORY type (DR-82's pattern, un-migrated because that round covered only the four TEMPLATED loaders), so a 4-byte dataset read into a one-byte `hbool_t` **clobbered 3 bytes past it** while still returning the right value — reproduced with a canary before fixing. `long double` kept native (80-bit precision, `F64LE` would truncate); `load_string_from_file` examined and found correct; the `cl_HDF5_Dataset` vlen path left untouched. Four gates run including end-to-end against the real fixed code. **DR-116 struck and archived the same session; `make check` then RAN and PASSED, closing the row with no residue** — with the honest caveat recorded that the suite's two bool round-trip cases would have passed before the fix too, so the discriminating evidence is the canary probe, not the suite. Also records a register-editing error made and repaired here: a positional cell write shifted the row's columns and dropped its `blocking 1.0` value
- [dl20260831_bearing_removal_examples.md](dl20260831_bearing_removal_examples.md) — pre-commit cleanup: the deprecated `bearing` section removed from 11 example decks and, more to the point, from the two generators that would have re-emitted it (`tapestack3d.geo` in both copies, `corc_solder/python/corc/belfem.py`). The Python generator had justified node 1's exclusion from the periodic triple *by* its being the bearing node; that rationale is rewritten to the real one (three points already fix the affine cap-to-cap map). `circuit` and `RLC_Circuit` keep an empty `boundary conditions { }` because `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:144` asserts the section exists. `bearing` stays in the language but is now documented as an expert function and **not recommended** in all three artifacts — `doc/input_file_reference.md`, `doc/input_schema.yaml`, `examples/README.md` — since a deck bearing replaces the automatic pins rather than adding to them, floating every φ component the deck does not name. `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37; generator executed and inspected. Nothing built or run, so *reviewed*, not verified; the Codex language sweep over the two user-facing docs is owed
- [dl20260831_sch04_tables_removed.md](dl20260831_sch04_tables_removed.md) — the two brochure-derived Fujikura tables `fesc-sch04.hdf5` / `fysc-sch04.hdf5` removed from `share/material/` before the release. They are a different wire from `fesc`/`fysc` (4 mm SCH04 vs 12 mm S12) and a different kind of data: digitised from the Fujikura brochure Rev JUL2024 rather than measured, with an angular structure that is a **model** — flat in θ for FESC, a fitted ellipse for FYSC — where every other REBCO table in the directory carries measured asymmetry that `doc/input_file_reference.md` explicitly tells decks not to fold away. The decisive reason is copyright: the encumbrance is not just the interpolant but `source/points` (the ~308 digitised marker centres), the generator in `python/`, and `meta/pdf_sha256`. Nothing referenced them — no source, deck, test, or schema — and `share/` installs as a whole directory, so no CMake change was needed; the README drops from eight tables to six. `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37. **The blobs are still in history** on `main`/`devel`/`claude` and on `origin`; the purge plan is `todo/sch04_history_purge.md` and none of it has been run
- [dl20260831_homology_doc_d6_closure.md](dl20260831_homology_doc_d6_closure.md) — closes the D6 residue the repair campaign left open: `homology_usage_guide.md` still taught a manifold-filtering pipeline built on `manifold_filter_3d()` and `check_surface()`, which exist nowhere. The residue was **four** sites, not the two recorded — the missed one was a troubleshooting entry telling readers to tune the nonexistent filter's cycle-density and size thresholds, the most actionable form the error can take. Marked, not deleted, with the sibling documents' design-note banner; Tarjan corrected from "does not exist" to "archived prototype, `archive/graph/fn_Graph_tarjan.{hpp,cpp}`, outside the build" — one more artifact reached by the `ugrep --ignore-files` false negative. **A second defect of the same class was found by checking an adjacent claim:** the advertised debug outputs `thick_cut_*.vtk`, `thin_cut_*.vtk` and `manifold_*.vtk` are written by no code (`manifold` appears zero times in the module's sources); `save_debug_meshes()` emits `cut_<index>.vtk` and belongs to `CutProcessor`, not `CutFactory`, whose only call to it is commented out at `cl_CutFactory.cpp:471` — so the module's debug output is unreachable without a rebuild, flagged for Gregory rather than acted on. Completeness checked mechanically: all 44 code identifiers asserted across the six homology documents diffed against `src/` and `archive/`, three absent, two real and one (`rectify_to_unit_or_certify`) a false positive already labelled "Proposed". Documentation only, source untouched; reviewed, not verified. Also records the two rulings Christian gave the same evening and the plans they produced: **F2 → wire the parallel nested dissection** (`todo/parmetis_ptscotch_wiring.md`, with F1's release-build out-of-bounds write promoted to D1 and a precondition, since wiring is what makes it reachable — `gNoOwner` confirmed as `INT_MAX` exactly), and **Q1 → AC loss is a real gap** (`todo/ac_loss_postprocessing.md`; the loss density `rho*|J|^2` is already assembled with `w(k)` and `dV(k)` at `mt_thermal_h.cpp:49,124`, so it is a reduction, not a physics task — the plan logs the three ways the sum goes quietly wrong: resistivity source vs isothermal runs, the `[0,1e10]` clamp that `mRhoClamped` already tracks, and thin shells, where the loss actually lives and no thermal shell kernel exists)
- [dl20260901_heatloss_and_iv_globals.md](dl20260901_heatloss_and_iv_globals.md) — jury round on two new features and the repairs it forced: a `heatloss` mesh global integrating `∫ρ|j|²dV` over the h-domain, and per-terminal `I_nn`/`U_nn` globals beside `iv_results.csv`. Two P0s, both agreed 3/3. The accumulator was reset and MPI-reduced only in `iterate_magnetic`, while all three executables drive `solve_coupled` — so on the production path it grew monotonically as a rank-0 partial across every iteration and every timestep, and a warm restart resumed from it; Grok predicted before the fix existed that guarding only the coupled HEAD would miss the line-search retrial, which is what the first repair did. And `h_newton_mu0` added `dotQ` twice, once in the tangent guard — that kernel is dispatched precisely when μ is constant, i.e. it is the **HTS** kernel. Both closed, plus: `save()` ran before `save_IV()` in all three executables so the first Exodus frame had no I/U globals and later ones carried the previous step's values (reordered); `I_nn` and `U_nn` were created with the same explicit id, which a 2026-08-15 comment in `create_global_variable` exists to prevent (auto-id now). Then the repair round introduced its **own** P0 — `new std::ofstream( name )` followed by `open()` leaves the stream open-but-failed after the constructor already truncated, so `iv_results.csv` became 0 bytes every run; caught by a fifteen-line probe, not by review, and fixed with a stack stream opened once. csv header now spells its columns through the same `sprint` as the globals (`I_01 U_01 …`, was `I_0 V_0 …`). Four of my own claims retracted, including every `file:line` in the pre-registration being 2-3 lines low (read off hunk offsets); one Codex finding refuted on in-tree precedent. Left open by decision: `save_IV`'s `compute_full_matrices()` runs a second element pass into the accumulator. Units recorded nowhere — `heatloss` is power, not energy. `make check` 15/15 green — but the suite has no heat-loss and no IV case, so a 0-byte csv and a drifting accumulator would both have passed it; the probes are the discriminating evidence and a two-rank run is still owed. `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp` also carries a wholesale reindent that should be reverted before commit
- [dl20260901_dr_tag_sweep.md](dl20260901_dr_tag_sweep.md) — DR- tracker indices and `( Christian's ruling )` attributions removed from code comments: 77 sites in 36 `src/` files, 46 in 18 `tests/` files. DR numbers are ephemeral — they resolve only against a register the code reader does not have, and point at nothing once the row is struck. Comments only, applied as count-asserted exact matches; where the tag *was* the noun the defect is now described ("the historical stray message", "the curl-sign gate", "a real defect"), and one cross-reference became a file name that stays true. Orphaned sub-indices (`G11`, `D4`, `F3`) went with their parents. `todo/`, `devlog/` and the register keep theirs; `doc/` (24 refs, 6 rulings) and the in-code cross-review bookkeeping (`round-3 C5`, 13 sites) are owed decisions. Nothing built or run
- [dl20260901_dotq_rename_and_docs.md](dl20260901_dotq_rename_and_docs.md) — the ohmic-dissipation mesh global renamed `heatloss` → `dotQ` and documented. The old name did not say whether the number was a power or an energy; it is a **power in watts**, and an AC loss per cycle is its time integral, which nothing in the tree takes. 18 count-asserted edits across five files — the string, the error message, and `save_/reset_/collect_heatloss` with it, since renaming only the string would leave `save_heatloss()` writing into `dotQ`. Breaking for downstream readers, accepted: the field is one day old, unpushed, in no deck, no schema and no doc, with no consumer in the repo. Name collision with the NODAL `dotQ` of `IWG_StaticHeatConduction` examined and accepted — separate namespaces in `Mesh` and in Exodus (`EX_NODAL` vs `EX_GLOBAL`), different meshes, and both are honestly heat rates. New §9.3 in the maxwell usage guide records units, scope (one mesh-wide scalar, no per-block split), the lifecycle, why `h_ghost()` is excluded, and that a clamped `rho` makes `dotQ` a lower bound. Codex sweep flagged two of my claims and both were right: executables *may* narrow `gRhoMin`/`gRhoMax` (none here does), and the low side of the clamp trips on negative resistivity rather than never. `check_doc_claims.py` 37/37; nothing built or run
- [dl20260901_iv_globals_unified.md](dl20260901_iv_globals_unified.md) — the duplicate `current` mesh global traced to the block-label fallback (the literal deck token, hence ungreppable) and removed for the four terminal types via a new `has_block_global()` predicate shared by the three guard sites; `save_IV` becomes the single terminal channel and folds the deck label into its names (`I_coil1`/`U_coil1`, running suffix on repeats, positional `I_1` otherwise) for the csv header and the globals alike. The temperature global moves from `MaxwellFactory` (frozen `gTbulk`) to `Controller::save()`, refreshed every save as `T_max` = live max(T) in a coupled run or `T_fixed` = `gTbulk` otherwise — `T_ad` proposed and withdrawn, adiabatic being the thermal model's word. Syntax-clean with the tree's flags; not built, gate is a `tape_quench` global list
- [dl20260902_doxygen_deck_data_pages.md](dl20260902_doxygen_deck_data_pages.md) — the two `examples/*/data/README.md` deck notes stopped being top-level Doxygen chapters. They became pages because `update_doc_index.py` walked all of `examples/` and Doxygen indexes every `.md` under `INPUT` — and because the session that met the resulting `no PAGE_GROUPS entry for…` error satisfied the check by promoting both files instead of asking whether the pages should exist. Fixed on both halves, since either alone puts them back: `NOT_DOCUMENTATION_DIRS = {"data"}` pruned from the generator's walk, `*/examples/*/data/*` in `EXCLUDE_PATTERNS` (stripping the anchor alone only yields an auto-labelled `md_…` page under Related Pages), anchors removed, `doc/mainpage.md` regenerated. `*/CLAUDE.md` and `*/AGENTS.md` excluded in the same edit — `examples/corc_solder/python/CLAUDE.md` would have rendered as a chapter titled "CLAUDE.md" on the next `make doc`. `--check` 0 out of date, 27 modules / 99 pages; verified in a scratch Doxygen run against the real tree, which keeps the deck plugin sources. `make doc` not run
- [dl20260903_copper_lambda_field_flag.md](dl20260903_copper_lambda_field_flag.md) — `material copper --rrr 50 -b 20` prints the zero-field thermal conductivity because `Metal::set_RRR` rebuilds the lambda spline through `SplineLookupTable::set_spline`, which wipes the property's dependency bitset back to T-only after `create_kohler()` had set normB/angleBxJ; the Wiedemann–Franz overload exists and works (probe: 311 → 182 W/(m·K) at 4 K, 20 T) but neither the tool nor `cl_FEM_Calculator` ever selects it, so coupled runs use a field-free stabilizer conductivity beside field-aware Joule heating. Every pure metal since bc4a525c (2026-07-02); alloys and YBCO unaffected. Probe + Codex + Grok agree; fix applied on approval: one gated `set_kohler_dependencies()` at the end of `set_RRR`, all nine metals verified by probe; regression test `test_MetalFieldDependence.cpp` added (fails against the unfixed archive, passes with the fix); one contract paragraph in the materials invariants doc; Codex + Grok diff audits both "ship". Second finding, fixed on approval: `set_RRR` (and the YBCO ctor) passed ρ₀/L₀ as the λ spline's start tangent where the slope is L₀/ρ₀, so λ was −79 % at 0.5 K and +3 % at 5-6 K against the Hust curve; now < 0.1 % for seven metals, ≤ 1 % between knots for indium and lead (knot spacing). Tool's resistivity header relabelled `1e-8 Ω·m` (was `A/m²`). Third gtest case locks the tangent. jc/n wiring checked and clean on the solve path (routes on the JcFunction's own flags, argument order consistent end to end); one latent trap fixed on approval: `set_custom( jc | n )` clobbered the `rho_i` / `debye` function pointers and was reachable through the one-argument user-material overload — both entry points now reject jc/n before any flag is raised, the post-processor's unreachable `jc_custom` fallback is gone, gtest added. `jc_custom`/`n_custom` kept: the reduced `rho_powerlaw( normJ, T )` overloads still call them. Incident: a `git checkout -- cl_Material.hpp` to undo my own dead-code removal also discarded a concurrent session's uncommitted sweep edits; recovered verbatim from its applier log and transcripts and re-applied. `make check` owed
- [dl20260904_rebco_tables_n_monotone_b16.md](dl20260904_rebco_tables_n_monotone_b16.md) — the five REBCO jc/n tables regenerated: `n` made non-increasing in `log10 B` by a running minimum on the B-spline control net (the shipped `n` rebounded 13 -> 58 in sp-ap at 25 K, 7.9 T and 4 -> 14 in the 79-85 K collapse), field axis widened from 25.1 T to 39.8 T (`89 x 37 x 181`; 1.5 asked, 1.6 because order 2 needs an odd node count), `build.py` sweep dedupe so the embedded generator runs again; reproduces the pre-projection tables to round-off, all reader-side gates pass, `jc` accuracy vs measurement unchanged to the fourth decimal; bscco-2223 untouched; stage scripts not jury-audited
- [dl20260905_examples_reorg.md](dl20260905_examples_reorg.md) — `examples/` reorganized for 0.9.0: developer-named decks renamed by what they show (`corc_periodic_bc`, `corc_garber_effect`, `corc_twolayer`, `tapestack2d_layered`, `tapestack2d_racetrack`, `tapestack_circuit`, `racetrack_usermat`), the byte-identical `tapestack3d` twins merged, stray `defect.cpp`, `CLAUDE.md` and `make_mesh_box.py` deleted, `pancake` mesh tool renamed to `main.py` so the launcher finds it. Layout stays flat: a Codex+Grok plan audit showed dimension is the only clean partition and a `2d/`/`3d/` level breaks the wrappers, the `belfem_conf` glob and every first-run path. All 18 `input.conf` re-indented with tabs and column-aligned, gated by an identical `belfem_conf` parse tree; 18 deck READMEs written and Codex-swept; feature matrix in `examples/README.md`. Three inventory errors caught by the auditors are recorded. Nothing built or run
